# standard-delivery is the second workflow on the script engine

**Status:** accepted, 2026-10-20

## Context

ADR-0035 chose one Claude Workflow script per workflow. ADR-0036 put analysis-only on that engine as slice 1 and deferred the git questions to slice 2. Issue #409 tracks the work. Slice 2 moves the pre-PR part of standard-delivery onto the engine.

Three things in standard-delivery cost the user today. Every planner question ends the run as blocked. Every transition costs an overlord turn, and 52% of overlord tool calls are ledger bookkeeping. Git safety is written in prose. The slice must also avoid a new state machine in prose and side-effect agents that repeat their effects when a run is replayed (ADR-0035 decision 8, spike S5).

This ADR records the slice decisions, the user's three calls, the step invariants, and the results of spikes S11 and S12. It does not restate the rules. Each rule is stated once in its home file, named below as "Text:". Most homes do not exist yet; "Lands in:" names the PR in Landing PRs below that creates each one. This ADR also does not specify the fields, limits or step mechanics of code that does not exist yet. Where it names one, as in the hand-off table, that is an obligation for the landing PR, whose schemas and tests settle it. A review finding on such a detail is recorded as an obligation for that PR, in the hand-off table or under Deferred items, and is tracked under #409. It changes a decision or an invariant here only when it shows that the decision or invariant is wrong.

## Scope

This ADR governs:

- the new engine's standard-delivery workflow, from the request up to and including opening the PR;
- the committed git scripts behind the git skills, which both engines use;
- the interim start of brood children without the Workflow tool (decision 10). Amended 2026-10-24 (slice 4): the interim start has ended, and brood children launch the script engine (see Amendment 2026-10-24: brood children on the script engine (slice 4));
- the removal of Bash from cerebrate (decision 1), which also changes the current engine.

The post-PR watch and pr-feedback-remediation stay on the current engine until slice 3. Every other ADR keeps the scope ADR-0035 gave it. New permission-engine facts are recorded in ADR-0010 under "## Amendment 2026-10-20: tool grants and prompts for workflow agents"; this ADR uses them and does not repeat them.

## Workflow outline

`hivemind:standard-delivery` runs these steps in code. The order and the routes between steps are code, the step invariants below hold at every step boundary, and each step's rules live in the homes the decisions name.

1. Facts: a runner runs the facts script, which reports the branch as it is so that the planner can plan what is left: branch state against a freshly fetched trunk, the branch's history against trunk, the branch's PR, and the text of any issue or PR the request names. Contract: `plugin/references/facts-schema.json` and the facts script's tests (PR2), which cover the "Facts to plan" row of the hand-off table below.
2. Plan: cerebrate returns a plan checked against a schema. Its questions and the plan acceptance go through `decide()` or the pause contract.
3. Branch: a runner creates or confirms the working branch.
4. Waves: code computes each ready wave and runs the workers in parallel.
5. Version bump: a runner runs the bump-type engine, and a drone makes the edit. Amended 2026-10-29 (issue 457): this step is gone. A plan step sets the version files, and the validate step checks them (see Amendment 2026-10-29: a stop pauses unless nothing can go on, and the version check moves into validation (issue 457)).
6. Validate: a drone runs the project's validation commands, with bounded fix rounds.
7. Local review: `hivemind:local-reviewer` runs, and a code table routes its exit reasons.
8. PR: a runner pushes through `push-branch.sh` and opens the PR or reuses an open one.
9. The overlord relays the message, applies the ADR-0029 watch rule, and hands the watch to pr-feedback-remediation on the current engine.

A workflow script cannot run a shell, so it sees only what agents report. The hard stops sit in the committed scripts, and the workflow reads their routing lines from a schema field and blocks when a line is missing or malformed.

## Step invariants

Five review findings on PR #419 had one root: a decision or step was written in prose without checking it against what its consumer requires, and each fix added one more field or action for the next review to probe (see References). Patching each one leaves the next one open, so the class is closed at the step boundary instead. Invariants 1 to 3 cover the tree, the commit and the facts a step hands on, invariant 4 covers what every payload says about itself, and field-level detail is left to the landing PRs (see Context). These invariants hold at every boundary, each is stated only here, and the decisions cite them by number.

1. **Clean tree at every step boundary.** Every step that can change files ends with the commit script (decision 12), whatever the step returned, so the next step starts on a clean tree at a known commit. A run starts only on a clean tree: dirty paths in the facts stop the run before planning, and the run never commits, stashes or discards an edit it did not make. Held by: the commit script refuses a dirty path outside the step's file list, and the branch and PR scripts refuse a dirty tree.
2. **A gate holds only for the commit it ended on.** A gate is a step whose pass the PR step depends on, such as the bump step, validation and local review today. Each gate holds only for the commit its step ended on, and the PR step needs every configured gate to hold for the commit it pushes. So any commit made after a gate, such as a validation fix round or a local-review fix, sends the run back through the gates it invalidates: every gate that ended on an earlier commit. Going back through the gates is bounded through `decide()` kind `gate_rerun`, and the run stops and asks at the bound. A gate that is not configured for the run, such as local review when the Codex plugin is absent, does not stop the PR step and does not count as held: it is listed on the PR with the run's decisions (decision 3). Held by: the workflow takes each step's end commit from the commit script's routing line and opens the PR only when every configured gate held on the same commit, and `push-branch.sh` takes that commit and refuses, in the same process as the push, when HEAD is another commit. Amended 2026-10-29 (issue 457): the gates are validation and local review. The bump step is gone, and validation checks the version on the commit it runs on (see Amendment 2026-10-29: a stop pauses unless nothing can go on, and the version check moves into validation (issue 457)).
3. **Every planning pass starts from fresh facts.** The run enters planning only through the facts step: at the start, after `needs_replan`, and after a local-review escalation or root cluster. A replan sees the commits the run made and plans what is left, as a restart does (decision 2).
4. **Every payload says whether it is complete and which of its fields are external.** Each payload that crosses a step boundary, including the facts output the overlord passes to the planner on the current engine (decision 1), is checked against its schema. The schema marks each field that can carry external content, and each list or text that a producer may cut says whether it is complete or cut, with its full count when cut. A step refuses a cut field it depends on: the run stops before that step and names what was cut, so no step treats part of its input as all of it. External content crosses only under `plugin/governance/security-policy.md` (External Content Boundary and Injection-Suspect Classification); analysis-only already applies that boundary to its request (`plugin/workflow-src/analysis-only/main.js`). Held by: each payload's schema requires these labels, and a payload that fails its schema blocks the step that reads it.

Text: each step's preconditions and postconditions are rows of a contract table in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/contract.js` (amended 2026-10-24: the rows moved there from `main.js`, which keeps the checks they name; see Amendment 2026-10-24: short run messages), with a test for each row in `tests/workflow-scripts/`; each refusal is in its git script (decision 5); the `gate_rerun` kind is a row of `plugin/references/decision-autonomy-table.json` (invariant 2); and each payload's labels are in its schema, such as `plugin/references/facts-schema.json` and `plugin/references/plan-schema.json` (invariant 4). This ADR names the invariants and does not restate the rows. Lands in: PR2 (the script refusals and the facts schema), PR3 (invariant 4 for the facts the planner gets on the current engine and in analysis-only), PR4 (the plan schema), PR6 (the table, invariants 1, 3 and 4), PR7 (invariant 2, and the rows for the bump, validation, local review and PR steps).

Each hand-off in the outline is an obligation for the PRs in the last column: each covers it with a contract table row or a schema, and a test. The table says what the consumer needs, not how the code provides it, and invariant 4 holds on every row.

| Hand-off | What the consumer needs | Invariants and decisions | Row and test land in |
|---|---|---|---|
| Facts to plan | A freshly fetched trunk, from a fetch that changes no branch, HEAD, index or file; the branch, trunk, HEAD, trunk freshness and dirty paths with their status; the branch's whole history against trunk: the merge-base with trunk (the remote trunk when there is one), each commit since it with its subject, and each changed path with its status (added, modified, deleted, or renamed with its old path) and its added and removed line counts; the branch's PR and its state (open, merged or closed); the text of any issue or PR the request names, scanned and marked as external content. Planning starts only when no path is dirty, no merged or closed PR is on the branch, and no history the planner depends on is cut | Invariants 1, 3 and 4; decisions 1 and 2 | PR2 (facts schema and script tests), PR3 (current engine and analysis-only), PR6 |
| Plan to branch | create-working-branch's inputs: the base, the plan's branch name and work classification, and trunk freshness that is fresh or decided; a clean tree | Invariant 1; decision 14; `decide()` kind `trunk_stale` before the branch step | PR4 (plan schema), PR6 |
| A wave to its commit | Dirty paths only inside the wave's declared files | Invariant 1; decision 12 | PR6 |
| A wave to planning (`needs_replan`) | The wave's edits committed; current facts | Invariants 1 and 3 | PR6 |
| Commits to bump-type | Every change committed, each commit typed from the plan's classification and breaking flag; the plan's answers to the two judgments in `plugin/governance/versioning.md` ("## Bump Type Determination"). Amended 2026-10-29 (issue 457): the consumer is the version check of the validate step, which also gets the plan's version files and the facts step's `trunk_commit` | Invariant 1; decision 14 | PR4 (plan schema), PR7 |
| Bump to validation | The bump edit committed; a bump step that runs again changes nothing. Amended 2026-10-29 (issue 457): there is no bump step. A plan step sets the version files and its wave commits them. Validation checks them read-only, a version already at its target passes, and a fix round corrects a mismatch | Invariants 1 and 2; decision 26 | PR7 |
| Validation fix rounds to the PR | Each round committed, and every configured gate held on the pushed commit | Invariants 1 and 2; decision 16 | PR2 (`push-branch.sh` expected commit), PR7 |
| Local review to the PR | Every configured gate held on the pushed commit, which includes the reviewer's fix commits; no edits left behind | Invariants 1 and 2; decision 17 | PR7 |
| Local review to planning | Current facts | Invariant 3 | PR7 |
| PR to the watch | The PR, a clean tree on its branch, and every decision the run took and every gate not configured for the run shown on the PR | Invariants 1 and 2; decision 3 | PR7, PR8 |

## Decision

Decisions 1 to 3 are the user's calls. Decisions 4 to 23 are journal items J1 to J20 of the slice plan. Decisions 24 and 25 come from the S11 surprises. Decision 26 and the step invariants come from the review of PR #419.

1. **The planner has no shell (call 1, option A).** Decided: cerebrate's `tools:` drops Bash and every `Bash(...)` entry. A facts step gives the planner what the shell gave it: branch state, the branch's history against trunk, and the text of any issue or PR the request names (outline step 1). That text is external content and reaches the planner only as invariant 4 allows. The planner reads files with Read and searches with Glob and Grep. It loses git research beyond the facts, such as `git blame`, the log of one path, and a file at an older revision. standard-delivery runs that step anyway. analysis-only gains a facts step. On the current engine, the overlord runs the facts skill and passes its output to the planner when it delegates, under invariant 4. Reason: the cerebrate tool-list finding below. Read-only becomes true by construction instead of by prompt, Glob and Grep come back (S11-c), and planner prompts drop to near zero. S11 surprise 3 backs it: any agent with Bash loses Glob and Grep. Text: `plugin/agents/cerebrate.md` (frontmatter, and the line that prefers Read, Glob and Grep), the CLAUDE.md line on read-only enforcement, a pin in `tests/policy/` that replaces the overclaimed rule in `safety-planner-no-write.json`, the new facts skill and `plugin/references/facts-schema.json`, `plugin/workflow-src/analysis-only/main.js`, `plugin/agents/overlord.md`. Lands in: PR2 (facts skill, script and schema), PR3 (the rest).
2. **No run record for standard-delivery (call 2, option A).** Decided: git and GitHub are the record: the working branch, its commits (decision 12), and the PR. A new session restarts the request. The planner plans what is left on the branch from the facts step's branch history (outline step 1) and the current files, which it reads with Read, and each git step checks for its own earlier effect (decision 5). When that history is complete, it gives the planner all a restart needs: the commits show which waves landed, the changed paths show what the branch did, including deletions and renames that Read cannot show, and the branch's PR shows whether the PR step ran. Dirty paths stop the run before planning (invariant 1), so edits cut off before their commit wait for the user, and a merged or closed PR on the branch stops the run too, as does a cut history (invariant 4). A replan inside a run reads the facts the same way (invariant 3). The facts carry no file contents from trunk, because what is left is judged against the files as they are now. A request that needs a file's trunk side, such as undoing part of the branch, becomes a plan step, and the worker reads that side with its own shell. The history comes from the branch the checkout is on. After step 3 that is the working branch, so a new session in the same checkout sees it; a session started on trunk sees no history and plans the whole request. A run that starts on a branch other than trunk delivers on that branch: the plan must name it, and the planner asks when the branch's history does not fit the request. This matches analysis-only (ADR-0036 decision 10). Reason: a second record duplicates durable state and can go stale; 35 of 37 fallback runs were on old definitions. Spike S9 is not needed. Text: `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`. Lands in: PR6.
3. **Trust the planner's gate flags and show every taken decision (call 3, option A).** Decided: `decide()` keeps trusting the planner's irreversible, architectural and safety flags, as in slice 1, and a plan runs with no review stop. Every decision a run takes for the user is listed in the final message and in the PR body, or in a PR comment when the run reuses an open PR. One stop is added: the plan schema requires each step to declare any effect outside the repo, such as a deploy, a publish, a migration or other write to shared data, or a write to an outside service over the network. A plan that declares an irreversible one goes to the user before the first wave, through `decide()` kind `external_effect`, which asks at every level. A declared reversible effect is listed with the run's decisions. Reason: an effect inside the repo stays on the branch until the user merges, and the merge is the one irreversible step for it. The PR body puts every taken decision in front of the user at that step, and a branch change undoes one. An effect outside the repo happens during the waves, before the PR exists, so the PR comes too late for it, and a branch change cannot undo an irreversible one. A review stop on every plan would add stops the user does not want: in the usage research, 175 of 483 user prompts were approvals or continuations, and their root cause was planner questions that ended the run, not a missing plan review (`docs/research/2026-10-hivemind-usage-analysis.md`). An effect outside the repo that the plan does not declare is a recorded residual. Text: `plugin/workflow-src/lib/decide.js` (unchanged), the `external_effect` entry of `plugin/references/decision-autonomy-table.json`, the declaration in `plugin/references/plan-schema.json` and in the `plan:` block in `plugin/agents/cerebrate.md`, the stop before the first wave in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`, `plugin/workflow-src/lib/result.js`, the PR step in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/`, the open-plan-pr script under `plugin/skills/open-plan-pr/`. Lands in: PR4 (the plan schema), PR6 (cerebrate's plan block, the `external_effect` entry and the stop before the first wave), PR7 (the rest).
4. **Scope (J1).** Decided: the pre-PR part of delivery goes on the engine. When the result names a PR, the overlord applies the ADR-0029 watch rule and runs pr-feedback-remediation (intake `watch`) on the current engine. Reason: the watch stays in the main session (ADR-0035 decision 10), and post-PR remediation is already tested there. Post-PR on the engine moves to slice 3 with reviewer briefs. Text: `plugin/agents/overlord.md` (`## Engine`). Lands in: PR8. Amended 2026-10-25 (issue 453): the watch stays on the current engine, and it now pauses on questions. A GitHub reviewer question or high-severity rejection, and a remediation planner's open questions, go to the `question` user gate, and the answer goes back to the state that asked, in the same run (see Amendment 2026-10-25: open questions pause on both engines, and a run can be stopped (issue 453)).
5. **Git side effects only through committed scripts (J2).** Decided: branch, commit, push and PR open run only through committed scripts behind the plugin skills. The scripts hold the hard stops and the checks for earlier effects: an existing branch is confirmed, nothing to commit means already done, an open PR is reused, a repeated push is harmless. The skills keep their contracts and routing lines, so the current engine uses the same scripts. Each script that writes also checks the tree it needs (invariant 1). Reason: ADR-0035 decision 8 and ADR-0036's deferred item "git hard stops as code". Text: scripts under `plugin/skills/create-working-branch/`, `plugin/skills/molt/` and `plugin/skills/open-plan-pr/`, plus `plugin/skills/push-branch/scripts/push-branch.sh`; shell tests; the `tools/validate.sh` mapping. Lands in: PR2.
6. **No seeded deny or ask rule for raw git push (J3).** Decided: the seed template gets no rule that tries to stop `git push` to trunk. Reason: Bash rules match the command as written, so `git -C . push` gets past them, and such a rule is a deny-list. S12 confirmed that nothing in the harness stops it: the auto-mode classifier accepted a raw `git push origin HEAD:main` from a workflow agent, and the push landed. Trunk safety lives in `push-branch.sh`, and server-side branch protection closes the gap. See Recorded residuals. Text: `plugin/skills/push-branch/scripts/push-branch.sh`. Lands in: no change; the script already refuses trunk.
7. **No committed allow rules for git writes (J4).** Decided: the git scripts run under each skill's own `allowed-tools` grant, which names the exact plugin path. Wildcard script-path rules are rejected. This replaces the project allow rules for exact commands in ADR-0035 decision 7. Reason: S11-a passed, so the primary branch holds: the script ran with no prompt in manual mode, and S12 shows the same grant path in auto mode. A wildcard rule would run a file planted at a matching path without a prompt. Each Skill call from a workflow agent still prompts once in manual mode unless a `Skill(...)` rule covers it (S11-a, S11-f); see Deferred items. Text: the `allowed-tools` frontmatter of the git skills. Lands in: PR2. Amended 2026-10-29 (issue 467): on the script engine the runners are `hivemind:synapse`, which has no Skill tool, so no skill grant reaches them, and a preloaded skill's grant applies to nothing (ADR-0010, Amendment 2026-10-29, U4). A plugin PreToolUse hook allows synapse the five runner commands in their exact form under `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT`, with no prompt in manual or in auto mode. For every agent it denies any other form of a Bash command that names one of those scripts. Wildcard rules stay rejected: the hook matches the exact script path and a fixed value class per flag, not a pattern. The skill grants stay for every other caller, the current engine included (see Amendment 2026-10-29: script runners are hivemind:synapse behind a plugin hook (issue 467)).
8. **Resume by name, no seeded `Workflow(...)` rule (J5).** Decided: the overlord resumes a paused run with `Workflow({name, resumeFromRunId, args})`, never with `scriptPath`. The harness's "don't ask again" is the user's per-project opt-in, and the seed template adds no `Workflow(...)` rule. Reason: S11-e. A resume by name served earlier agents from cache and raised no prompt once "don't ask again" had written `Workflow(<name>)` to the project's `.claude/settings.local.json`. Left alone, the model resumed by `scriptPath` on its persisted copy, which prompts every time and is not covered by that rule (S11 surprise 2). See Recorded residuals. Text: `plugin/agents/overlord.md` (`## Engine`). Lands in: PR5.
9. **Seed-template additions (J6).** Decided: the seed template gains three rules, and nothing else: `Skill(hivemind:route-workflow)`, `Edit(.hivemind/molt-inputs-*.json)` and `Edit(.hivemind/pr-inputs-*.json)`. A fourth rule, `Read(~/.claude/plugins/cache/brenpike/hivemind/**)`, waits for the check below. Amended 2026-10-23 (issue 428): the seed template keeps its three rules and gets no Read rule. The Read rule moves into skill `allowed-tools` as the entry `Read(~/.claude/plugins/cache/brenpike/hivemind/**)` (see Amendment 2026-10-23: skill allowed-tools file grants). None of these rules names a machine or a plugin version, so each is the same correct text for every teammate and every release in the committed `.claude/settings.json`, and the merge, which never removes an entry, has nothing stale to leave behind. A permission rule expands no `${...}` variable (ADR-0010's 2026-08-25 amendment), so no rule can follow `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}`. The Read rule instead uses the documented home-relative `~/` anchor and the documented cache layout: a marketplace install sits at `~/.claude/plugins/cache/<marketplace>/<plugin>/<version>/`, and `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` points at it. The rule covers every installed version of this repository's marketplace entry (`brenpike`, `hivemind`). A plugin loaded from anywhere else matches no rule and keeps today's Read prompt; see Recorded residuals. Runners do not depend on the Read rule: the script gives them their task, the Skill tool gives them their skill text, and the haiku drone in S11-g read no governance doc. The planner, the workers and the reviewer do, because their agent files load governance docs by path. The two Edit rules are inputs-file rules, like the six the template already has. The molt and open-plan-pr scripts take free text, such as a commit message or a PR body, from a file under `.hivemind/` that the model writes with the Write tool, so no free text goes into a shell command. The skill text fixes each file's path, and the rule only stops the Write prompt for it. Reason: S11-f showed the Skill rule stops the main-session Skill prompt, and the same rule form stops workflow-agent Skill prompts. S11-d showed a Read allow rule for a plugin folder stops a workflow agent's Read prompts for plugin files while other Reads outside the repo still prompt. S11-d tested absolute `//` rules in the gitignored `.claude/settings.local.json` with plugins loaded by `--plugin-dir` (see Spike limits). So the `~/` rule must first be checked from the committed `.claude/settings.json` against a marketplace install, with the result recorded in ADR-0010. PR5 did not run that check: it needs a manual-mode session with a user present to answer the prompts. The PR8 live smoke ran it, and the result was inconclusive (see PR8 live smoke). The sessions loaded the plugin from a checkout, not from the cache, so no Read matched the rule. And route-workflow's bare `Read` entry in `allowed-tools` approved every Read of the workflow agents launched in the same turn, so no Read could prompt either way. The check moves to https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/428 (see Deferred items), and the template gets no Read rule. It gets the rule only if a later check passes, because the merge never removes an entry, so a seeded rule can never be withdrawn. Amended 2026-10-23: no later check adds it; the skill entry takes its place. The PR8 smoke also showed that a skill's `allowed-tools` entry `Edit(<glob>)` approves a Write to a matching path while the skill is active, in the main session and in a workflow agent. So molt and open-plan-pr now carry `Edit(.hivemind/molt-inputs-*.json)` and `Edit(.hivemind/pr-inputs-*.json)` in their own `allowed-tools`, and a consumer that has not run seed-hive again no longer needs the seeded rules for those Writes. The seeded rules stay, since the merge never removes an entry. Text: `plugin/skills/seed-hive/`, `plugin/skills/_shared/settings-merge.sh`, the `allowed-tools` frontmatter of `plugin/skills/molt/SKILL.md` and `plugin/skills/open-plan-pr/SKILL.md`, the `Read(~/.claude/plugins/cache/brenpike/hivemind/**)` entry in the `allowed-tools` frontmatter of the non-executor skills under `plugin/skills/`, and CHECK 16 in `tools/policy_check.sh`, which ties that entry's marketplace and entry names to `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`. Amended 2026-10-23: the skill frontmatter and CHECK 16 join the list, and CHECK 16 replaces the planned check in `tools/test_seed_hive.sh`. Lands in: PR2b (the two Edit rules), PR5 (the Skill rule), PR8 (the Read rule check, inconclusive, and the two skill `Edit(...)` entries, 78119e1). The Read rule and its test wait for issue 428. Amended 2026-10-23: the skill entry and CHECK 16 land with issue 428, and no seed rule lands.
10. **Brood children stay on the current engine until slice 4 (J7).** Decided: spawn-brood starts children with `--disallowedTools Workflow`, so the hatchery's started-evidence keeps working. This supersedes the brood clause of ADR-0036 decision 2 until slice 4. Reason: S11-h showed the Workflow tool is absent from such a session and the overlord falls back in one line and runs the state machine to `complete`. Text: `plugin/skills/spawn-brood/scripts/spawn-brood.sh`. Lands in: PR8. Amended 2026-10-24 (slice 4): this decision has ended. spawn-brood no longer passes `--disallowedTools Workflow`, children launch the script, and started-evidence also counts a gather-facts output file, so the hatchery sees script children start (see Amendment 2026-10-24: brood children on the script engine (slice 4)).
11. **Runners are `hivemind:drone` on haiku, invoking the skill (J8).** Decided: every runner is a haiku `hivemind:drone` that invokes the skill for its step. The six-agent roster stays. Reason: S11-g. Both the drone and an untyped haiku agent were correct 3 of 3. The drone used about 28% fewer tokens (76k against 106k) and took about 1.6 s longer (8.2 s against 6.6 s). The haiku drone read none of the governance docs its body says to load; see Risks. Text: `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`, `plugin/agents/drone.md` (the git-script task exception). Lands in: PR6. Amended 2026-10-29 (issue 467): the facts, branch, commit and pull request runners are a haiku `hivemind:synapse`. Its only tools are Bash and Write, it loads no skill and no governance doc, and it runs the one command its prompt names. Its first request is about 3.8k input tokens, against about 26k for the drone (ADR-0010, Amendment 2026-10-29, U6). The roster has seven agents. The scan, validation and fix rounds stay drones, and the drone's git-script task exception is removed. Text: `plugin/agents/synapse.md`, `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/relay.js` and `pr.js`, `plugin/workflow-src/lib/facts.js` (see Amendment 2026-10-29: script runners are hivemind:synapse behind a plugin hook (issue 467)).
12. **One commit script closes every step that can change files (J9).** Decided: the commit script runs at the end of each wave, waves of one included and whatever the workers returned, and at the end of the bump step, each validation run or fix round, and local review. It commits exactly the step's file list and refuses any other dirty path. The list is the plan's file scopes for a wave, the artifact files for the bump, and the files a fix round reports. It is empty after a validation run and after local review, whose fixes the reviewer commits itself through the same script. With nothing to commit it changes nothing. Either way it reports the commit the step ended on (invariant 2). Every commit it makes takes its type from the plan (decision 14). Parallel workers never commit, and a wave that ends blocked is committed before the run stops. Reason: one code path, and invariant 1 then holds by code. Text: the commit script behind `plugin/skills/molt/`, `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`. Lands in: PR2 (script), PR6 (waves), PR7 (bump, validation, local review). Amended 2026-10-29 (issue 457): there is no bump step and no bump commit. The wave of the plan step that sets the version files commits them, and a version fix is a validation fix round, committed with the files it reports (see Amendment 2026-10-29: a stop pauses unless nothing can go on, and the version check moves into validation (issue 457)).
13. **Waves are a pure function that mirrors next-wave (J10).** Decided: code computes each ready wave from dependencies and file scopes, with parity tests against the next-wave fixtures. Reason: one rule set for both engines, tested. Text: `plugin/workflow-src/lib/waves.js`; tests: `tests/workflow-scripts/`. Lands in: PR4.
14. **The plan is checked against a schema (J11).** Decided: the planner's plan is validated against a schema. Delivery is single or multi only, and both run as one branch and one PR, as in the current engine graph. Brood stays out until slice 4. The plan also carries every input that a later step needs and that takes judgment, such as the working branch (decision 2) and the work classification; the "Plan to branch" and "Commits to bump-type" rows of the hand-off table list them. Reason: runners do not read governance (see Risks), so no runner judges an input. Text: `plugin/references/plan-schema.json`, the `plan:` block in `plugin/agents/cerebrate.md`. Lands in: PR4 (schema), PR6 (cerebrate's plan block). Amended 2026-10-24 (slice 4): each brood child now runs this workflow as a single or multi delivery in its own worktree, and its planner names the working branch, because the worktree boots on a detached HEAD at the base. The plan schema still has no brood delivery: splitting a request into strains stays with hatchery-dispatch on the state machine, as slice 5 scope (see Amendment 2026-10-24: brood children on the script engine (slice 4)).
15. **Taken decisions are shown (J12).** Decided: decisions taken for the user go in the final message and, under decision 3, in the PR body. Text: as decision 3. Lands in: PR7.
16. **Validation is a drone step with bounded fix rounds (J13).** Decided: a drone runs the project's documented validation commands. Failures get a bounded number of fix rounds through `decide()` kind `validation_remediation`, then the run asks. Each fix round ends with the commit script and sends the run back through the gates it invalidates (invariants 1 and 2). Text: `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`, `plugin/references/decision-autonomy-table.json`. Lands in: PR7.
17. **Local review runs inside the workflow (J14).** Decided: escalation and root-cluster go back to planning through the facts step (invariant 3). A remediation plan always asks, as the table says. user-input-required and high-severity-rejection ask in plain words until slice 3. The reviewer's fix commits send the run back through the gates they invalidate, and a later commit, such as a validation fix round, sends it back through local review as well (invariant 2). Text: the local-review table in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/`. Lands in: PR7. Amended 2026-10-25 (issue 453): user-input-required and high-severity-rejection no longer end the run. They pause it, and the reply reruns local review on the same commit with `user_answer` and the reviewer's fix ledger. A rejection the user keeps is recorded at its source by the local reviewer, which commits the record and goes on. A surfaced escalation or root-cluster ask pauses too, and its reply starts a new planning pass (see Amendment 2026-10-25: open questions pause on both engines, and a run can be stopped (issue 453)). Amended 2026-10-25 (issues 441, 445 and 452): a remediation plan no longer always asks. It asks only when the plan's `remediation_gate` trips the Promotion Gate through its `promotion_gate` (irreversible, architectural or safety-relevant, as in a decision brief's gate), or marks it adding more surface than it removes, or following a fix on the same surface that did not close its class. Any other remediation plan is decided for the user and listed in the final message and the PR body. The rule's one home is `plugin/governance/decision-autonomy.md` (Promotion Gate). A local review question shows only short fields the reviewer writes for the user (see Amendment 2026-10-25: user-facing text comes from bounded fields (issues 441, 445 and 452)). Amended 2026-10-28 (issue 460): user-input-required and high-severity-rejection pause with the reviewer's own question and options, and no menu that asks whether to answer the finding or stop wraps it (see Amendment 2026-10-28: one message shape (issue 460)). Amended 2026-10-29 (issue 462): a local review call is no longer always asked. The run takes an agreed call that is reversible and not architectural at the high and balanced levels, lists it in the final message and the PR body, and reruns local review with the call as its answer, which the reviewer records at its source naming the run. Every other review call still pauses, per `plugin/governance/decision-autonomy.md` (`### Review Calls`) (see Amendment 2026-10-29: agreed review calls are taken (issue 462)).
18. **Draft marker and approval limits in the build (J15, amended in PR8).** Decided: the build skips a workflow marked as a draft, and tests build it in memory, so delivery lands in pieces without going live. Amended in PR8: the build also keeps every built script approvable at the Workflow approval prompt. It strips comments, writes each data constant as indented JSON, and fails when a script is over 190,000 characters or breaks one of the approval-preview rules recorded in ADR-0010's amendment 2026-10-22. Reason: in manual mode, Claude Code 2.1.308 offers only No at the approval prompt for a script over 200,000 characters, or for one whose preview would be cut, and the first build of `standard-delivery.js` was 237,625 characters (see PR8 live smoke). The 190,000 budget leaves room below that limit for later growth. Text: `tools/build_workflows.mjs` (its header names each limit), `tests/workflow-scripts/build.test.mjs`. Lands in: PR4 (the draft marker), PR8 (the size and preview checks, 844745a and 11a21cc). Amended 2026-10-25 (issues 441, 445 and 452): the build now writes each data constant as JSON with one key or element per line and no indent. The data is unchanged. This frees about 2,800 characters per script, after the bounded-fields fix took `standard-delivery.js` over its 190,000 character budget (see Amendment 2026-10-25: user-facing text comes from bounded fields (issues 441, 445 and 452)). Amended 2026-10-26 (issue 459): the data in each constant is no longer the whole file. The build cuts each one to the fields a script reads before it writes it in the same one-per-line form: `AUTONOMY_TABLE` keeps only what `projectAutonomyTable` returns, and `BRIEF_SCHEMA` and `PLAN_SCHEMA` keep `$schema` and every validation keyword, with no title and no description except the repository path mark. The build also compacts the code of each library, module and `main.js`. The 190,000 budget is unchanged (see Amendment 2026-10-26: the delivery engine shrinks and splits into modules (issue 459)).
19. **No new opt-in for go-live (J16).** Decided: the workflow approval prompt is the opt-in and a `permissions.deny` rule is the opt-out, as in slice 1. A No at the approval prompt interrupts the turn: nothing runs and nothing falls back, because the harness ends the turn and the overlord gets no turn to fall back in. A `Workflow(hivemind:<id>)` entry in `permissions.deny` refuses the launch with no prompt, and the overlord falls back to the state machine in one line (PR8 live smoke: PASS). In manual mode, a script is approvable only while it stays within the approval-preview rules, which the build enforces (decision 18). In auto mode the classifier decides the launch and no approval prompt is shown. Text: `plugin/agents/overlord.md` (`## Engine`). Lands in: PR8.
20. **Spikes run and skipped (J17).** Decided: S11 and S12 ran (see Spike results). S9 is not needed under decision 2, S6 is not needed while the watch stays in the main session, and S8 belongs to slice 4. Text: this ADR. Lands in: PR1. Amended 2026-10-24 (slice 4): S8 is not needed, because brood children stay separate sessions in their own worktrees (ADR-0007) (see Amendment 2026-10-24: brood children on the script engine (slice 4)).
21. **smoke-603 low items (J18).** Decided: N1 (open questions recorded as complete on the state machine) is a recorded residual. N2 (the routing YAML printed to chat) is fixed. The PR8 live smoke confirmed it on the script path: no session printed the routing outcome. On the state-machine path, one session printed a routing line once, a recorded residual. N3 (the planner tries to run scripts) is dissolved by decision 1. N4 (made-up inputs-file tokens) is a recorded residual. Text: `plugin/agents/overlord.md` (`## Engine`) for N2. Lands in: PR5 (N2), PR3 (N3).
22. **New engine facts go to ADR-0010 first (J19).** Decided: the facts S11 and S12 verified are recorded in ADR-0010, as its own authority rule requires, and the pinned NON-FINDING there is left as it is. Text: `docs/adr/0010-permission-allowlist-posture.md` ("## Amendment 2026-10-20: tool grants and prompts for workflow agents"). Lands in: PR1.
23. **Autonomy level stays at the table default (J20).** Decided: standard-delivery runs at the autonomy table's default level until slice 3, as ADR-0036 decision 8 does for analysis-only. Text: `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`, `plugin/references/decision-autonomy-table.json`. Lands in: PR6.
24. **Launch and resume text carries no runner instructions (S11 surprise 1).** Decided: runners get their task only from the script. The overlord's launch args and resume reply carry no instruction that could conflict with a runner's task. On a resume, the harness relays the user's reply to every workflow agent, not only to the one the reply was for. So once a run has taken a reply, every later agent prompt except the planner's revise prompt carries one fixed sentence: the reply was already applied, and it is not an instruction for that agent's task. The planner's revise prompt is the one the reply is for, so it carries no such sentence (https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/432). Reason: the harness relays the user message that started or resumed a run to every workflow agent and tells the agent that this message wins over the script's prompt. In S11, haiku agents tried to call Workflow themselves, skipped their task, or obeyed "Do not edit anything" from the main message, and a resume message overrode a resumed agent's task. In issue 432, after the user answered a planner question, the branch step read the relayed reply as an order to stop. See Risks. Text: `plugin/agents/overlord.md` (`## Engine`), `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js` (the fixed sentence). Lands in: PR5, and the issue 432 fix for the fixed sentence. Amended 2026-10-25 (issue 453): two more prompts are the ones a reply is for, so each carries the reply and not the sentence: the local review prompt with `user_answer`, and a plan prompt with an answer block. Every other prompt after the first reply carries the sentence (row SD-49; see Amendment 2026-10-25: open questions pause on both engines, and a run can be stopped (issue 453)).
25. **Plain `git` from the repo folder (S11 surprise 4).** Decided: the scripts, the skill text runners follow, and runner prompts use plain `git` run from the repo folder, never `git -C <dir>`. Reason: in S11-b, `git status --short` ran with no prompt and `git -C <repo> status --short` prompted. In S11-h, cerebrate used `git -C` and every call prompted. Text: the git skills and scripts, `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/`. Lands in: PR2, PR6.
26. **The bump is set from the fetched trunk (PR #419 review, amended in PR7).** Decided: the bump step sets each artifact's version to its value at the facts step's `trunk_commit` raised by the bump type. `trunk_commit` is the remote-tracking trunk after the facts fetch, else the local trunk. The version is never the current value raised, and it is no longer the merge-base value. A bump step that runs again on the same branch then changes nothing while trunk has not moved and the bump type is unchanged, and the commit script finds nothing to commit. Reason: invariant 2 runs the bump step again after any later commit, and a replan runs it again, so raising the current value would bump twice. The merge-base value is wrong when trunk bumped after the branch diverged: both sides would then carry the same version (https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/409#issuecomment-6283285070, item 2). A trunk bump that lands after the facts step is a recorded residual. Text: the bump step in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`, the `trunk_commit` line in `plugin/skills/gather-facts/scripts/gather-facts.jq`. Lands in: PR7. Amended 2026-10-29 (issue 457): superseded. No bump step sets a version. A plan step sets each version file beside the change log, and the validate drone checks it with a read-only `hivemind:bump-type --version-file` run against its value at the same `trunk_commit` raised by the bump type. A version already at its target passes, so a check that runs again after a replan or a head move changes nothing. A mismatch is fixed in place by a fix round (see Amendment 2026-10-29: a stop pauses unless nothing can go on, and the version check moves into validation (issue 457)).

## Cerebrate tool-list finding

At runtime cerebrate has Read, WebSearch, WebFetch and Bash, and nothing else that matters here. Glob, Grep, LSP and the claude-mem tools are missing, and Bash is the whole tool.

Evidence:

- **Tool list.** Claude Code 2.1.304, cerebrate spawned through the Agent tool outside a workflow: Read, WebSearch, WebFetch, Bash and SubagentHandback. Spike S3 (2.1.302, a workflow agent) showed the same set plus StructuredOutput and advisor, so the finding is not specific to workflows.
- **Glob and Grep.** The tools reference (code.claude.com/docs/en/tools-reference, "Glob tool behavior") says that on macOS, Linux and WSL they are left out of the default tool set, and a subagent gets them back only when its `tools:` lists them and leaves out Bash. cerebrate's 28 `Bash(git ...)` and `Bash(gh ...)` entries count as Bash. S11-c and S11 surprise 3 confirm it.
- **LSP** stays inactive until a code-intelligence plugin is installed. The **claude-mem tools** are inherited MCP tools and are absent when claude-mem is not connected.
- **Bash is not scoped.** The sub-agents docs define no `Bash(...)` specifier for `tools:`. In smoke-414, smoke-602 and smoke-603, planner commands outside the patterns reached a normal permission prompt, not a refusal. S11-b confirmed it: an agent with `tools: Read, Bash(git status *)` had the whole Bash tool, and `touch` prompted.

What follows: the planner was read-only only because its prompt said so. The CLAUDE.md line "Read-only enforcement on cerebrate is done by restricting `tools:` list" is false as written. `tests/policy/safety-planner-no-write.json` pins only that Write and Edit are absent, so its rule "Planner cannot write files" claims more than it checks. cerebrate's line that prefers Read, Glob and Grep points at tools it does not have, so every search becomes a Bash call, the main source of planner prompts in manual mode (up to 19 per round in smoke-602). Decision 1 resolves all four.

## Spike results

Claude Code 2.1.304. S11 ran in manual mode and S12 in auto mode, with haiku workflow agents, in throwaway repos with local bare remotes. Full results: `~/code/hivemind-workflow-spikes/results-s11s12.md` and https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/409#issuecomment-6281842682.

| Item | Verdict | Decides | Branch taken |
|---|---|---|---|
| S11-a: a skill's script runs under the skill's exact-path grant inside a workflow agent with no prompt. The Skill call itself prompts once without a `Skill(...)` rule | PASS (script); Skill call prompts | J4 (decision 7) | Primary: no committed git allow rules. The J4 fallback is not needed for the script call |
| S11-b: `tools: Read, Bash(git status *)` grants the whole Bash tool; `touch` prompts, not refused | PASS | Backs J4 and decision 1 | None |
| S11-c: an agent with `Read, Glob, Grep` and no Bash gets Glob and Grep | PASS | Backs call 1 option A | Option A |
| S11-d: an untyped workflow agent's Read of plugin files loaded with `--plugin-dir` from outside the repo prompts; `Read(//<plugin folder>/**)` rules in the gitignored `.claude/settings.local.json` stop it | PASS | J6 (decision 9) | Seed the home-relative cache rule after a check passes (PR8: inconclusive; issue 428). Amended 2026-10-23: superseded; no seed rule, and the same rule text is a skill `allowed-tools` entry (see Amendment 2026-10-23: skill allowed-tools file grants) |
| S11-e: resume by `name` serves earlier agents from cache, and "don't ask again" covers it | PASS, with two conditions (resume by name must be required; the relayed resume message) | J5 (decision 8) | Adopt resume by name; decision 24 covers the relay |
| S11-f: a `Skill(...)` rule stops main-session and workflow-agent Skill prompts | PASS | J6 (decision 9) | Seed `Skill(hivemind:route-workflow)` |
| S11-g: haiku drone against untyped haiku, one skill each: both 3 of 3; drone 8.2 s and 76k tokens, untyped 6.6 s and 106k | PASS, no large cost | J8 (decision 11) | Keep the drone |
| S11-h: with `--disallowedTools Workflow` the tool is absent and the overlord runs the state machine to `complete` | PASS | J7 (decision 10) | Adopt |
| S12 flow: commit, scripted push and `gh pr create --dry-run` from workflow agents in auto mode, untyped and drone, 0 prompts, 0 blocks | PASS | ADR-0036 deferred item "auto mode for git run from workflow agents" | Auto-mode delivery can go live |
| S12 raw push: the auto-mode classifier accepts `git push origin HEAD:main` from a workflow agent, and it lands | Recorded | J3 (decision 6) | Residual stands |

Spike limits: S12 used pre-placed `Workflow(...)` rules, so it did not observe auto mode's own launch prompt. Its `gh pr create --dry-run` exited before any GitHub call, because origin was a local path; it proves the command is not blocked, not the dry-run output. The PR8 live smoke covered both (see PR8 live smoke).

S11-d loaded both plugins with `--plugin-dir` from folders outside the repo, used an untyped haiku agent, and put absolute `//` rules in the repo's gitignored `.claude/settings.local.json`. It did not test a marketplace install in the plugin cache, a rule in the committed `.claude/settings.json`, a `~/` rule, or a typed hivemind agent. The check of the rule decision 9 would seed in that setting did not run in PR5, was inconclusive in the PR8 live smoke, and moves to issue 428 (see Deferred items). Amended 2026-10-23: the seed rule is dropped, and the same rule text becomes a skill `allowed-tools` entry (see Amendment 2026-10-23: skill allowed-tools file grants).

Every verdict is read from the transcripts (the permission decision on each tool result, the workflow journal), the clones on disk and read-only `gh` calls, not from a model's summary. Full results: `~/code/hivemind-workflow-spikes/pr8/results-smoke1.md`, `~/code/hivemind-workflow-spikes/pr8/rerun/results-b.md`, `~/code/hivemind-workflow-spikes/pr8/delivery/results-delivery.md` and `~/code/hivemind-workflow-spikes/pr8/delivery/rerun/results-rerun.md`.

| Item | Verdict | Decides |

Amended 2026-10-24 (issue 445): the exact text stays in the PR body and the reuse comment and leaves the final message, which shows each taken decision on one line (see Amendment 2026-10-24: short run messages) | PASS | Decisions 3 and 15 |

Smoke limits: the plugin loaded from a checkout, not from a marketplace install, so the Read rule check did not reach its target. The Codex plugin was not loaded in any session, so the local reviewer did not run. The `Edit(<glob>)` check used untyped workflow agents and only paths that match the glob. Each verdict comes from one to three sessions.

Amended 2026-10-24: settled for brood children by slice 4, which launches them in auto mode with remote control and no `--disallowedTools` (see Amendment 2026-10-24: brood children on the script engine (slice 4)) |

Amended 2026-10-24: settled for brood children by slice 4. A child keeps no run record, as decision 2 says, and spawn and the dashboard read the gather-facts output file as started-evidence (see Amendment 2026-10-24: brood children on the script engine (slice 4)) |

A declared irreversible effect outside the repo asks before the first wave (`decide()` kind `external_effect`); an undeclared one is a recorded residual |

## Considered Options

| Option | Rejected because |
|---|---|
| Call 1 B: keep the planner's shell and add code checks | Read-only stays a promise. Prompts remain, because allow rules cannot cover every way of writing a command. analysis-only gets no check |
| Call 1 C: fix the docs and the pin only | Leaves every observed problem in place |
| Call 1 A with the whole branch diff in the facts | Its size has no bound, and what is left is judged against the current files, which the planner reads with Read |
| Call 2 B: agents write a run record at milestones, plus a resume-point argument | An extra agent call per milestone, needs S9, and brings back record drift and version skew |
| Call 2 C: the overlord writes a run record when a run pauses or ends | The model copies data, smoke-602 showed copy errors, and the record is coarse |
| Call 3 B: code raises gate flags for plan steps on a project-declared list of sensitive paths | The list is a deny-list that grows with each miss and needs setting up per project |
| Call 3 C: a second model reviews each decision before it is taken | An extra model call per decision, and it is still model judgment |
| Call 3 A with a review stop on every plan | Adds a stop to every run, and the usage research behind decision 3 shows the user wants fewer stops, not more. Effects inside the repo already wait for the merge, and the one case the PR comes too late for, a declared irreversible effect outside the repo, asks (decision 3) |
| J3: a seeded deny or ask rule for raw `git push` | Bash rules match the command as written, so `git -C . push` gets past them. A deny-list |
| J4: wildcard allow rules for plugin script paths | A file planted at a matching path would run without a prompt |
| J4: committed exact-command allow rules for git writes (ADR-0035 decision 7) | Not needed: S11-a shows the skill's own exact-path grant runs the script with no prompt |
| J5: a seeded `Workflow(...)` allow rule | "Don't ask again" already writes that rule as the user's own per-project opt-in |
| J5: resume by `scriptPath` | Prompts on every resume and is not covered by the `Workflow(<name>)` rule (S11-e) |
| J6: no Read rule, because no workflow agent reads a plugin file (ADR-0036 decision 17, extended to workflow agents) | Only runners read none (S11-g). The planner, the workers and the reviewer load governance docs by path on both engines, and ADR-0036 decision 11 recorded planner prompts for the plugin's own governance files. Delivering governance as skill text or script prompt data instead changes every agent and the current engine, which is outside this slice. Without that change, no rule leaves every such Read prompting |
| J6: a per-machine Read rule in the gitignored `.claude/settings.local.json`, written and refreshed by seed-hive | `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` names a version folder, so the rule goes stale at every plugin update until each contributor runs seed-hive again (an update never re-merges settings), and dropping the stale entry needs a remove step the merge does not have. The home-relative rule needs nothing per machine |
| J6: the resolved absolute `Read(//<plugin folder>/**)` rule in the committed `.claude/settings.json` | It names the seeder's home folder and plugin version in a file the team shares and the merge never prunes, so it approves only the seeder's current install while stale entries pile up. ADR-0010's 2026-08-24 amendment keeps a `$HOME` cache path out of that file for the same reason |
| J8: an untyped runner agent | No large cost from the drone in S11-g. The drone keeps the six-agent roster and used fewer tokens |
| Keep each step's fields in this ADR and patch each review finding | Each fix added a field and the next review found the next gap, five findings in three rounds. Prose cannot be tested; a schema and its tests can (see Context) |
| Facts that cut a long history and say so, with the planner planning from the part it has | The planner has no shell and no run record, so a cut list can hide landed waves, deletions and renames, and the planner repeats or misses work |
| Calls 1 A, 2 A and 3 A, with the journal as recorded (CHOSEN) | (chosen) |

## Consequences

**Positive**

- Delivery transitions cost no model turns. Fixed agent calls per run are facts, plan, branch, bump, validate, review and PR, plus a commit runner at the end of each step that can change files (decision 12); each wave adds its workers. Amended 2026-10-27 (issue 463): each pass also makes one Scope call, after the facts step and the scan and before planning, so the fixed calls go up by one per pass (see Amendment 2026-10-27: the planner reads less (issue 463)). Amended 2026-10-27 (issue 464): validation edits nothing and has no commit runner, and with version files the bump is one drone and a commit runner, not three agents (see Amendment 2026-10-27: workflow cost outside the planner (issue 464)). Amended 2026-10-29 (issue 457): the bump is no longer a fixed call. With version files, the validate drone runs bump-type in the same call (see Amendment 2026-10-29: a stop pauses unless nothing can go on, and the version check moves into validation (issue 457)).
- Planner questions are decided or asked. Amended 2026-10-23: a delivery run that stops with questions still open ends blocked and names the next action, so complete always means a pull request exists (see Amendment 2026-10-23: question keys, blocked stops and the question template). Amended 2026-10-25 (issue 453): a delivery run pauses for every open question, the delivery tail included, with no pause limit, and the reply resumes the same run. It ends blocked with questions open only when the replies are too long to carry or an exact question is too long to show (see Amendment 2026-10-25: open questions pause on both engines, and a run can be stopped (issue 453)).
- Git hard stops and the checks for earlier effects are code, shared by both engines.
- No step hands the next one a dirty tree, a commit that skipped a configured gate, or a cut or unlabeled payload, and fetched issue and PR text reaches the planner only as external content (step invariants).
- The planner is read-only by construction, and its searches no longer prompt. Amended 2026-10-23 (issue 428): its Reads and Greps of plugin files outside the working folder skip the prompt only for a marketplace install in the default cache, in the turn that loads route-workflow or another skill with the `Read(~/.claude/plugins/cache/brenpike/hivemind/**)` entry. A resume turn, Agent-tool subagents on the state machine and other load locations still prompt (see Recorded residuals and Amendment 2026-10-23: skill allowed-tools file grants).
- Delivery keeps no state that can drift from git and GitHub.

**Negative or costs**

- A new session re-plans a paused delivery run, which takes a few minutes. A crash mid-step leaves uncommitted edits, and the next run stops on them before planning until the user commits, stashes or discards them (invariant 1).
- An under-flagged architectural choice still costs a round of rework.
- On the current engine, the overlord must run the facts skill and pass its output to the planner, and WebFetch covers only public GitHub.
- The planner loses git research beyond the facts, such as `git blame`, the log of one path, and a file at an older revision.
- In manual mode, each Skill call from a workflow agent prompts once unless a `Skill(...)` rule covers it.
- Users approve the workflow once per project, through "don't ask again".
- A wave that ends blocked is committed before the run stops, so the branch can hold a worker's partial edits. The run's message names that commit.
- Any commit after a gate runs the gates it invalidates again, up to the `gate_rerun` bound (invariant 2). Each validation fix round costs a fix, a commit runner, a bump check and a validation run, and one made after local review also costs another local review. Amended 2026-10-27 (issue 464): a fix round commit made by the commit runner with the plan commit type and no version file keeps the bump held with no agent call, only when the held bump's relayed counts have one row and no dropped revert and its range already held such a runner commit, and a fix round that commits nothing runs no validation, since its failures are kept for that head (see Amendment 2026-10-27: workflow cost outside the planner (issue 464)).
- The plan's one classification types every commit the workflow makes, so bump-type sees the plan's kind of change, not each step's.
- A branch whose history the facts cannot fully carry stops before planning (invariant 4). PR2a settled this: the facts are one complete file per run, no list is capped, and long text is chunked. The facts script checks every line of the file it writes against one bound, whatever field it holds, so each line fits one Read page. A chunk can be whitespace only (see the ADR-0036 amendment 2026-10-20). A cut arises only from a failed GitHub page or no merge-base, and both stop planning (see Deferred items).

## Risks

- **The relayed user message outranks runner tasks.** Decision 24 keeps the overlord's own text clean, but the user's request is relayed to every runner as written. A delivery request worded like "no changes", or one that names a git action, can make a runner skip or change its step. The workflow blocks when a script's routing line is missing or malformed, which catches a skipped git step, but not a drone that edits less than its task asks. The PR8 live smoke covered it once: under a request that ended "Make no other changes", every runner and worker ran its step (see PR8 live smoke). One session does not retire the risk. The resume case has the same root cause. The harness relays the user's resume reply to every agent the resumed run starts, and tells each one that the reply wins over its task. In issue 432, the user answered a planner question, and the branch step read that answer as an order to stop and returned blocked. The mitigation is the fixed sentence of decision 24: every later agent prompt except the planner's revise prompt says the reply was already applied and is not an instruction for that task. A sentence is a prompt, not a control, so a step can still refuse. Bounded impact: a step that still refuses stops the run as blocked, and the run's message shows the reason the step returned. Git writes go only through the committed scripts (decision 5), so a step that misreads the reply cannot write to git outside them. Link: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/432. Amended 2026-10-27 (issue 464): the relay is a recorded limit, by the user's decision on Q2, on ADR-0038's ground that no fix on this engine can close it (see Amendment 2026-10-27: workflow cost outside the planner (issue 464)).
- **Step text in a blocked message.** Step and script text reached the user-visible blocked message raw, and each site handled it differently. Local review findings 5b459198 and d460cb76 on this branch found it, under issue 432. A step reason could add lines, terminal escapes or bidi controls to the message. The fix is one encoder, `encodeUntrustedText` in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/result.js`. It JSON-encodes the value and writes every code unit outside printable ASCII as a `\u` escape. Reasons go through `quoteDiagnostic` and `describeReason`, which first make the value one line and cut it to 200 characters. Standard-delivery and analysis-only both use it. Tests allow `.blocker` and `.stderr` reads only in `describeReason`, `describeRelayBlocker` and `describeCommitBlocker`. Three residuals remain. (a) Markdown inside a quoted reason can still render inline. Bound: the reason is one quoted line after fixed text, so it cannot start a heading, a list or a new line. Rejected: escaping Markdown characters garbles paths and identifiers, and a code span around each value adds noise to every message. (b) Planner prose is shown as written: brief fields and verbatim text rendered by `plugin/workflow-src/lib/brief.js`, and the analysis-only report. Bound: the planner writes it for the user, GitHub text is scanned before planning, and it is display only. Rejected: the ASCII encoder would garble text the user must read, and issue 431 reworks that template. Annotated 2026-10-23: issue 431 reworked the template (see Amendment 2026-10-23: question keys, blocked stops and the question template). The residual stands, since brief fields still show as the planner wrote them. Amended 2026-10-24 (issue 445): verbatim text shows only for an ask the workflow marks exact, today an irreversible effect, under "Word for word:", so planner verbatim reaches a question only there (see Amendment 2026-10-24: short run messages). (c) Non-ASCII text in a reason shows as `\u` escapes. That readability cost is accepted for a closed allowlist: only printable ASCII passes as written. Link: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/432. Amended 2026-10-28 (issue 460, findings 2c8e2101 and 75535594): residual (b) narrows to inline Markdown. Planner prose shown in a question is one line, option letters come from position in code, and the verbatim text of an exact ask shows encoded, so planner prose can no longer start a line of the question structure. Markdown inside one line still renders (see Amendment 2026-10-28: one message shape (issue 460)).
- **Runners skip governance.** The haiku drone in S11-g read none of the governance docs its body says to load. Runner safety rests on the committed scripts, not on the drone's reading.
- **External text the scan does not catch.** The injection-suspect patterns are a list, so the text of a named issue or PR that matches none reaches the planner as data and can still shape the plan. The commit script refuses edits outside each step's files (decision 12), git writes go only through the scripts (decision 5), and every decision the run took is on the PR before the user merges (decision 3). An effect outside the repo does not wait for the PR: the plan must declare it, and an undeclared one is a recorded residual. Today the planner reads that text itself, under the boundary alone.
- ADR-0037 names text homes that do not exist yet, and the hand-off table can miss an obligation. Each landing PR's schemas, contract table rows and tests are the check (see Context). A later PR that changes a decision amends this ADR in the same PR.
- PR2 changes git skills the fallback uses. Their routing lines stay unchanged, their contracts change only by additions such as the optional expected commit for `push-branch.sh`, and tests cover both call paths.
- The spike facts are tied to Claude Code 2.1.304, and the PR8 live smoke facts to 2.1.308.

A chunk can be whitespace only (see the ADR-0036 amendment 2026-10-20). A cut arises only from a failed GitHub page or no merge-base, and both stop planning (invariant 4). No. Without them, each such Skill call prompts once in manual mode, in the main session (S11-a). ## Recorded residuals

- **An effect outside the repo that the plan does not declare (decision 3).** Root cause: workers have the whole Bash tool, since `tools:` cannot scope it (the cerebrate tool-list finding), and whether a step has an effect outside the repo, and whether that effect is reversible, is the planner's judgment, as its gate flags are. A worker's effect that the plan did not foresee, or a declared one the plan wrongly marks as reversible, runs during the waves, before the PR exists. Bounded impact: only the harness permission mode catches it. In manual mode the command prompts unless an allow rule covers it. In auto mode the classifier blocks actions it judges risky, though S12 shows it can accept one (decision 6). In bypass mode nothing catches it. Brood children start in bypass mode and stay on the current engine until slice 4 (decision 10), so neither this stop nor a prompt applies to them. Amended 2026-10-24 (slice 4): brood children now run this workflow in auto mode, so the `external_effect` stop and the auto-mode classifier apply to them as to any auto-mode run (see Amendment 2026-10-24: brood children on the script engine (slice 4)). The exposure is inherited: on the current engine, workers have the same shell, and a plan goes from planning to implementation with no stop. A step whose task is the effect, such as a deploy step, is in the plan and must be declared. A plan step's file list can name a path that is a symlink to a target outside the checkout, so a worker's edit there lands outside the repo; this is the same exposure, since workers already have the whole Bash tool, and it is the filesystem-alias residual recorded in `plugin/skills/next-wave/scripts/next-wave.sh`, whose rule `plugin/workflow-src/lib/waves.js` mirrors (local Codex review finding 5a089cde on PR6). Why the obvious fix was rejected: a deny or ask rule for deploy, publish, migration or network commands is a deny-list. Bash rules match the command as written, so another spelling or a wrapper script gets past them, and the list grows with each miss and differs per project (decision 6, call 3 B). A review stop on every plan is rejected in decision 3. Review thread: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/pull/419#discussion_r4321284654.
- **Raw push to trunk (decision 6).** Root cause: the harness has no control that stops a raw `git push origin HEAD:main`. Bash rules match the command as written, and the auto-mode classifier accepted that push from a workflow agent in S12, where it landed. Bounded impact: hivemind's own pushes go through `push-branch.sh`, which refuses trunk. Only an agent that steps outside its scripts can push to trunk, and server-side branch protection on the remote stops it there. A typed drone in S12 refused on its own, which helps but is not a control. Why the obvious fix was rejected: a deny or ask rule is a deny-list that `git -C . push` gets past.
- **A plugin update between pause and resume (decision 8).** Closed 2026-10-28 by issue 472 (see Amendment 2026-10-28: answers kept across replans, and a build check on resume (issue 472)). Root cause: a resume by `name` runs the currently installed script, not the copy the run started with. A changed script changes the cache keys, so every step ran again from pass 1 and the stored answers were never read. Now the build injects `BUILD_ID`, a resume carries it in `resume_args`, and a resume with an absent or different build stops before any agent call. The build id is a replay guard, not a run record, so decision 2 is unaffected. Resume by `scriptPath` stays rejected: it runs the stored copy, but it prompts on every resume and no rule covers it.
- **Plugin Reads outside the default plugin cache (decision 9).** Rewritten 2026-10-23 (issue 428; see Amendment 2026-10-23: skill allowed-tools file grants). Root cause: `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` is substituted only in `allowed-tools` Bash rules, and a settings rule expands no `${...}` variable (ADR-0010's 2026-08-25 amendment), so the skill entry `Read(~/.claude/plugins/cache/brenpike/hivemind/**)` names the default cache folder of this repository's marketplace entry, not the folder `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` resolves to. Bounded impact: when the plugin loads from anywhere else, such as `--plugin-dir`, a marketplace added from a local path, a plugin cache moved by `CLAUDE_CODE_PLUGIN_CACHE_DIR`, a plugin synced from claude.ai, or a fork under another marketplace name, no entry matches, and Reads and Greps of plugin files outside the working folder prompt in manual mode. Before this change, route-workflow's bare `Read` entry hid those Read prompts in the launch turn, because it approved every Read of every path for the rest of the turn, workflow agents included. The entry never approves a Read outside hivemind's own cache folder. Per-user remedy: a `Read(//<plugin folder>/**)` rule in the project's gitignored `.claude/settings.local.json`, the form S11-d passed. Why the obvious fixes were rejected: a bare `Read` grants every path, which is the defect issue 428 removes; a rule per load location is a list that grows with each location; and a per-machine rule goes stale at every plugin update (see Considered Options). Review thread: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/pull/419#discussion_r4320879672.
- **A trunk bump that lands after the facts step (decision 26).** Root cause: the bump step reads each artifact's version at the facts step's `trunk_commit`, and trunk can move between the facts fetch and the merge. Bounded impact: a trunk bump in that window leaves the branch with the version trunk already has. This is the same race at merge time that today's engine has. The version is shown on the PR, so the user sees it before the merge, and a later run or a rebase picks up the new trunk. Why the obvious fix was rejected: a second fetch before the push still races with a trunk bump that lands after it, and it adds a step. Amended 2026-10-29 (issue 457): the bump step is gone, and the residual holds for the version check of the validate step, which reads each version at the same `trunk_commit`.
- **Workflow-agent searches outside the working folder prompt (PR8 live smoke F2).** Rewritten 2026-10-23 (issue 428; see Amendment 2026-10-23: skill allowed-tools file grants). Root cause: a workflow agent's Read or Grep of a path outside the working folder prompts (`reasonCode: workingDir`) unless a rule or a skill grant covers it, and the plugin's governance files sit outside the consumer's working folder wherever the plugin loads from, a checkout or the plugin cache under `~/.claude/plugins/cache/`. The planner's agent file loads governance by path and searches it with Grep. Covered now: for a marketplace install, the turn that loads route-workflow, or any skill with the `Read(~/.claude/plugins/cache/brenpike/hivemind/**)` entry, pre-approves Read and Grep of plugin cache files for the workflow agents it launches, since a Read rule also applies to Grep (per the permissions docs; the 2026-10-23 headless test verified it, recorded in ADR-0010). Still prompting, inherited: a resume turn, where the main session loads no skill before it resumes the workflow by name; Agent-tool subagents on the state machine (untested); and the load locations outside the default cache in the residual above. Bounded impact: one prompt per such search, in manual and in auto mode, and the run waits for the answer: an unattended auto run waited 1h54m in the PR8 live smoke. Nothing runs past the prompt without the user's answer, and an answer for the session covers the path from then on. The condition is inherited: the planner loads and searches the same governance files by path on the current engine, and ADR-0036 decision 11 recorded planner prompts for the plugin's own governance files before slice 2. Why the obvious fixes were rejected: a bare `Read` or `Grep` skill grant approves every path (issue 428); a seeded rule can never be withdrawn, because the merge never removes an entry; a per-machine rule goes stale at each plugin update; and delivering governance as skill text or script data changes every agent and the current engine (see Considered Options, J6). A prompt rule telling the planner not to search plugin files would be a promise, not a control. Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/428, `~/code/hivemind-workflow-spikes/pr8/delivery/rerun/results-rerun.md` (F2).
- **A routing line printed on the state-machine path (smoke-603 N2, PR8 live smoke).** Root cause: on the state machine, the overlord writes its own text between many bookkeeping tool calls. In one PR8 smoke session, right after a `silent_turn_reminder` from the harness, it printed "Route: exploratory (no commit/PR excludes delivery)", against the `plugin/agents/overlord.md` (`## Engine`) rule that keeps the routing outcome out of chat. The script path has no such turns, and no script-path session printed it (decision 21). Bounded impact: the inherited engine only, one line once in the smoke, with no secret in it and no step changed. Why the obvious fix was rejected: another prompt rule restates the one that the model broke here, and the state machine retires in slice 5. Links: `~/code/hivemind-workflow-spikes/pr8/delivery/results-delivery.md` (item 2d), https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/409#issuecomment-6281012540 (smoke-603).
- **Open questions recorded as complete on the current engine (smoke-603 N1).** Root cause: the fallback's `analyze` state maps open questions to blocked, while the overlord in one of three sessions recorded `complete` and listed the questions, as the script engine would. The two engines disagree on this case. Bounded impact: current engine only, low severity, and the questions were shown to the user. Why the obvious fix was rejected: rebuilding `decide()` in fallback prose is what failed in #408, and the fallback retires in slice 5. Amended 2026-10-25 (issue 453): the fallback's `analyze` state now sends open questions to the `question` user gate, so the overlord asks the user and the answer goes back to `analyze` in the same run. Open questions are recorded as neither blocked nor complete, so the case this residual names no longer arises. The fallback has no pause cap, a residual of that amendment (see Amendment 2026-10-25: open questions pause on both engines, and a run can be stopped (issue 453)).
- **Made-up inputs-file tokens (smoke-603 N4).** Root cause: the overlord writes the inputs-file token itself, with a fixed fake timestamp and a low-entropy suffix, while the skill relies on it for per-invocation uniqueness (ADR-0019). Bounded impact: current engine only, and it matters only with two concurrent overlords in one checkout. Why the obvious fix was rejected: it costs one extra tool call per transition, and the code retires in slice 5. Amended 2026-10-29 (issue 467): extended to the script engine, where `hivemind:synapse` makes the token for its commit and pull request runs (see Amendment 2026-10-29: script runners are hivemind:synapse behind a plugin hook (issue 467)).
- **Scan coverage relies on the facts runner's count (invariant 4).** Root cause: a workflow script cannot read files, so analysis-only decides whether to run the injection scan, and checks that the scan returned one result per `text_id`, from the `external_texts` count and ids that the facts runner relays from `gather-facts`. If the runner miscopies the count or skips an id, GitHub text can reach the planner unscanned. On the current engine the overlord counts the scan results itself, by judgment. Every check tried moves trust from one model's report to another's. Bounded impact: the facts runner never reads GitHub text itself, the scan runner gains nothing by under-reporting, and the planner has no shell, Write or Edit tools (decision 1), so unscanned text can shape a plan only as data. The commit script, git-writes-only-through-scripts and the PR showing every taken decision (decisions 3, 5, 12) still apply. The exposure is no worse than before this change, when the planner read that text itself under the boundary alone. Why the obvious fix was rejected: a check value printed by `gather-facts` and recomputed by the workflow (a hash over the routing values and content-bound ids) closes honest copy errors, but adds a hash kept in step between jq and JS, new id formats and test rewrites, and still trusts the runners against deliberate forgery. Its cost outweighs the bounded impact here. Hardening, if any, moves to PR6, where the shared lib holds the check: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/409. standard-delivery's facts step relays `gather-facts` stdout too. The workflow parses that relay with the script's routing spec and re-checks it for consistency in code. The shared check lives in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/facts.js`. A forged relay of `external_texts`, `cut`, `delivery` or `delivery_stops` that is still consistent is the same bounded residual. No check value is added. Review findings: Codex local review findings 112d4411 and b94aaa9c on PR3.

smoke-603 N3 (the planner tries to run scripts) is not a residual: decision 1 removes the shell.

## Amendment 2026-10-23: question keys, blocked stops and the question template

This amendment covers issues 433, 434 and 431, under epic 435. It updates three places in place: the Consequences line on planner questions, the deferred item on delivery-tail questions, and Risks residual (b). ADR-0036 gains a matching amendment for its decisions 7 and 16. The rest of ADR-0037 is unchanged.

- **Every question key is derived from its kind, a scope and the text shown (issue 433).** Root cause: a key built per call site from a hand-picked subset of fields. It was too narrow for local review findings 7578f16f and 0c804e49, and too wide for issue 433.
