Plan acceptance was keyed on the plan summary, which the planner's revise step rewrites when it records the reply, so the settled question was asked again until the pause limit. Decided: every question key is derived in `resolveDecision` from three parts: its kind; a scope; and the exact text `renderQuestions` shows. The scope is the pass for most kinds, the pass and the whole plan for both plan acceptances, and the run for `trunk_stale` and `external_effect`. Plan acceptance is asked only when no other question of the pass is open. A reply that settles it runs the plan result it showed, never the revise output. Eliminated class: no field list exists, so an answer cannot carry over to a question shown with other text, and a rewrite the user cannot see cannot re-ask it. The key binds what a question shows, not everything a plan does: the plan acceptance question shows the plan summary as its verbatim text, so a settled acceptance runs the whole plan result it was shown with (see the residual on plan acceptance below). Rejected: a field-subset key (complete-the-known-set), a plan digest compared with the adopted revise output (a summary rewrite re-asks, issue 433), and a prompt rule, which is a promise, not a control. Text: `resolveDecision`, `resolvePlanAsks` and `runPlanningPass` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`, rows SD-09 and SD-52.
- **Planner questions get a key.** A planner question is keyed like every other question: its kind, the pass, and the exact text `renderQuestions` shows for it. Consequences: a settled planner question raised again with any visible change is asked again, within the pause limit, and at the limit the run ends blocked (below). An accept reply that also asks for a change runs the plan as shown. Acceptance can cost one more pause when other questions are open. Rejected: a key on the brief id, which the model writes, or on a label like Q1, which moves with the order of the questions. Amended 2026-10-25 (issue 453): standard-delivery has no pause limit, so such a question pauses again in the next round (see Amendment 2026-10-25: open questions pause on both engines, and a run can be stopped (issue 453)).
- **Residual: plan acceptance shows the summary, not the plan.** Amended 2026-10-24 (issue 445): the question no longer shows the summary. It shows one line built in code from the plan, its PR title, step count and branch, and says the full plan is in the run's per-agent results; the bound and the rejected options below stand (see Amendment 2026-10-24: short run messages). Root cause: `buildPlanAcceptanceBrief` has shown `plan.summary` as its verbatim text since PR8 (D1), so the user approves the summary while the run executes the plan's steps, files, branch, versioning and reversible effects. Local review finding 8fe07586 on this branch raised it. Inherited: this amendment changes only the key and which plan result runs, the one shown, never what the question shows. Bounded impact: at the default autonomy level `plan_acceptance` is taken and never shown, and `remediation_plan_acceptance` always asks. Either way the steps run only on the working branch, the commit script commits only each step's files (decision 12), every irreversible effect is asked on its own with its exact text (row SD-11), and the reversible effects and every taken decision are in the final message and on the PR before the user merges (decision 3). Rejected: showing the whole plan in the question. A multi-step plan competes with the question for the pause message size budget (`plugin/workflow-src/lib/result.js`), so a compact form would cut the text the user approves, it undoes the ask-first template of issue 431, and at the default level no acceptance question is shown at all, so it is not the approval boundary. Link: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/433. Amended 2026-10-25 (issues 441, 445 and 452): the question shows a short problem built in code and a detail block with the plan's `digest` and one line on what the plan touches, with no pointer to per-agent results, and `remediation_plan_acceptance` asks only when a `remediation_gate` flag is set (see Amendment 2026-10-25: user-facing text comes from bounded fields (issues 441, 445 and 452)).
- **An irreversible effect is asked again when its text changes.** The `external_effect` key is the shown text, which holds the effect's exact summary, with the run and the full step id as scope. The shown step id is cut to its first 16 code points for the message only, so two steps whose ids share that prefix stay two questions (PR #458 review). Reason: the user approves an irreversible effect word for word, so a changed text is a new question the user must approve (decision 3). Asking it again is the intent, not the bug. Text: `resolveExternalEffects` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`, row SD-11. Amended 2026-10-28 (local review finding 7703cd64): the question names no step id, so no step id is cut. The key still holds the run and the full step id as scope (see Amendment 2026-10-28: one message shape (issue 460)).
- **A delivery run that stops with questions open ends blocked (issue 434).** Before, a run that reached the pause limit, held replies too long to carry, had an exact question too long to show whole, or asked after the last wave (stop reason `before_pr`) ended complete with the questions listed, so complete did not mean a pull request existed. Decided: each of these stops ends blocked, lists the questions still open, and puts one next-action line right after its first block. The line says to run the request again with the answers in it. When the run made commits, it names the branch and says the new run plans what is left from the commits on it, and at a `before_pr` stop it also says the pull request can be opened from those commits. Amended 2026-10-23 (issue 444): the `before_pr` line no longer offers a pull request, because a gate may not have held on those commits. It says no pull request opens until every gate holds on them (see Amendment 2026-10-23: local review holds the pull request on every path). This covers the tail asks: the bump, validation and gate rerun limits, and the local review questions. Only the PR step ends complete, so complete always means a pull request exists. analysis-only is out of scope and still completes (ADR-0036 amendment 2026-10-23). Text: `nextActionLine` and `finishOpenQuestions` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`, rows SD-09, SD-20, SD-23, SD-27, SD-37 and SD-51. Amended 2026-10-25 (issue 453): this stop kind was keyed on where a question came up and on a round count, and that key is gone. standard-delivery has no pause limit and no `before_pr` stop: every open question pauses, the tail asks included, and the reply resumes the same run. Only `reply_size` and `exact_text_size` still end the run blocked with questions open, and `nextActionLine` has one form for both. Complete still always means a pull request exists (see Amendment 2026-10-25: open questions pause on both engines, and a run can be stopped (issue 453)). Amended 2026-10-28 (local review finding 49be981e): `nextActionLine` is gone. `finishOpenQuestions` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/record.js` ends the stop as one cause with next action `answer` through `finishDeliveryBlocked` and `finish`, so the Next line is `nextStepLine('answer')`, "Next: run the request again with the answers in it." It no longer names the branch or says the new run plans what is left. With commits, the branch line that every blocked end shows names the branch (see Local review finding 49be981e).
- **A guard against a settled question coming back.** A settled plan acceptance freezes its plan result, and `trunk_stale` is keyed on the pass facts, so neither can come back within a pass. If a pause round would still show a settled `plan_acceptance`, `remediation_plan_acceptance` or `trunk_stale` question, that is a key defect and a workflow defect: the run neither pauses nor counts the round toward the pause limit, and ends blocked naming the question kind. The guard fails loudly where the old code looped until the limit. It leaves out `external_effect` and planner questions, whose changed text is a new question by design. Text: `runPlanningPass` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`, row SD-09. Amended 2026-10-25 (issue 453): standard-delivery has no pause limit, so the guard ends the run blocked with no pause, and no round count is involved (see Amendment 2026-10-25: open questions pause on both engines, and a run can be stopped (issue 453)).
- **The question template puts the ask first (issue 431).** A pause message opens with a bold heading naming the labels to answer. Each question shows a bold "Qn. problem" line, one list line per option with its reply token and summary and the recommended option marked, the recommendation and its why on one line, and at most two lines on why it matters: the criticality line and the reason the run asks. Each decision already taken shows as one line, with its label, problem and chosen option. Its full text stays in the final message and the PR body. Cases for and against, effort, value, reversible and assumptions are not shown. Text: `plugin/workflow-src/lib/brief.js`; test: `tests/workflow-scripts/brief.test.mjs`.
- **The relay may follow the user's output style.** The overlord presents `message`, for a question and for the final report alike, as its whole reply in the user's active output style, such as a terse style. It keeps exactly as written every question label, every option and reply token, the recommendation, and every number, path, URL, commit, branch and identifier. It adds no line before or after the message and no comment on it, and drops nothing the user must answer. Residual: the overlord restyles only what the user sees. The reply and the resume args stay verbatim. This amends ADR-0036 decision 16 (see its amendment 2026-10-23). Text: `plugin/agents/overlord.md` (`## Engine`), the relay contract in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/result.js`; test: `tests/workflow-scripts/overlord-contract.test.mjs`. Amended 2026-10-25 (issues 441, 445 and 452): the rule's one home is `plugin/agents/overlord.md` (`## Engine`). The relay contract header in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/result.js` keeps only a pointer to it, so the Text line is now `plugin/agents/overlord.md` (`## Engine`); test: `tests/workflow-scripts/overlord-contract.test.mjs` (see Amendment 2026-10-25: user-facing text comes from bounded fields (issues 441, 445 and 452)).

Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/433, https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/434, https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/431, https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/435.

## Amendment 2026-10-23: skill allowed-tools file grants (issue 428)

This amendment covers https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/428. On Claude Code 2.1.308, a bare `Write`, `Read`, `Glob` or `Grep` entry in a skill's `allowed-tools` pre-approves that tool for every path for the rest of the turn, workflow agents included. Decided: the seven inputs-file navigators drop their bare `Write` and keep only an `Edit(...)` entry for their own inputs file. Every bare `Read` in the non-executor skills, route-workflow included, becomes `Read(~/.claude/plugins/cache/brenpike/hivemind/**)`, and improving-architecture drops its bare `Glob` and `Grep`. CHECK 16 in `tools/policy_check.sh` is a positive allowlist: it rejects any other file-tool entry and takes the cache path from `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`. The six executor skills are exempt by path until their own decision. The canonical rule is the Skill File-Tool Grants section of `plugin/governance/security-policy.md`, and ADR-0010 records the posture. Left for their own decision: the executor skills' bare `Read`, `Write` and `Edit` (plus refactor-to-depth's `Glob` and `Grep`), triage-backlog's bare `Bash`, seed-hive's bare `Skill` and adaptation-cycle's bare `Agent`.

It updates these places in place, each with a dated note:

- Decision 9: the seed template keeps its three rules and gets no Read rule. The Read rule moves into skill `allowed-tools` as `Read(~/.claude/plugins/cache/brenpike/hivemind/**)`. CHECK 16 in `tools/policy_check.sh` replaces the planned check in `tools/test_seed_hive.sh` and ties the entry to the marketplace manifest. The Text list adds the skill frontmatter and CHECK 16.
- Spike results: the S11-d row and its Spike limits paragraph point here.
- PR8 live smoke: the rows for the Read rule check and F2 point here.
- Deferred items: the row for the Read rule check is settled. No seed rule; skill entries instead; the live check is the headless test, which passed on 2026-10-23 and is recorded in ADR-0010.
- Recorded residuals: "Plugin Reads outside the default plugin cache" and "Workflow-agent searches outside the working folder prompt (PR8 live smoke F2)" are rewritten.
- Consequences: the line saying the planner's searches no longer prompt gets the same bound as the F2 residual.

The rest of ADR-0037 is unchanged.

Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/428.

## Amendment 2026-10-24: short run messages (issues 445 and 441)

This amendment covers https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/445 and https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/441. Root cause: a question pasted planner text whole into the message, the plan summary in every plan acceptance and every taken decision's full form in chat, so the ask sank under text the user did not need to read. Reasons were cut mid-word at 200 characters, a blocked result said "fix the cause" even when nothing in the repo was wrong, and a local review question named a finding without showing it.

- **A brief's verbatim shows only for an ask the workflow marks exact (issue 445).** Today the one exact ask is an irreversible effect, which the user approves word for word (decision 3). Its text shows under "Word for word:", in the full and the compact form. The plan acceptance question shows one line built in code from the plan, its PR title, step count and branch, and says the full plan is in the run's per-agent results. Eliminated class: no planner text is pasted whole into a question unless code marks it exact. Text: `renderQuestion` in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/brief.js`, `describePlanLine` and `buildPlanAcceptanceBrief` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`, row SD-53. Amended 2026-10-25 (issues 441, 445 and 452): the plan acceptance question no longer shows that line or points to the per-agent results. Its problem is short code text, and a detail block after the questions block shows the plan's `digest.problem` and `digest.change` and one touches line: the step count, the distinct file count, the branch, and up to 5 file paths, then the count of the rest (see Amendment 2026-10-25: user-facing text comes from bounded fields (issues 441, 445 and 452)).
- **Chat messages show each taken decision on one line (issue 445).** A pause shows each taken decision as its label, problem and chosen option. The final message shows each as its label, problem, chosen option and why, the why made one line and cut at a boundary. The PR body and the reuse comment keep the full form with the text it accepted, so decision 15 and the PR8 live smoke D1 row still hold on the PR. analysis-only shows the one-line form too. Text: `renderDecisionsCompact` in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/brief.js`, `toMessageBlock` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`, `finishComplete` in `plugin/workflow-src/analysis-only/main.js`.
- **Reasons are quoted whole up to 600 code points, and cut only at a boundary (issue 445).** `describeReason` quotes a planner, worker, gate edit or validation reason whole up to `maxReasonLength()`, 600 code points. Every excerpt and every blocked lead that is too long is cut by `cutAtBoundary`: after the last sentence end that fits, else before the last whitespace, else at the limit, never inside a surrogate pair. A cut quote ends with the mark " (cut)". Other excerpts keep the 200 code point bound. Text: `cutAtBoundary`, `quoteDiagnostic` and `describeReason` in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/result.js`, `buildBlockedLead` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`.
- **A blocked step names its next action (issue 445).** The worker, bump and fix gate edit, and validation schemas carry an optional `next_action`, only with outcome blocked, from the enum `fix_cause` and `run_again`. `run_again` is for a cause outside the repo that passes with no change, such as machine load, a timeout or a network failure. It gives the fixed line "Nothing in the repo needs a fix: run the request again once the cause named above has passed, for example when the machine is idle." `fix_cause`, or no value, gives the fixed line "Fix the cause named above, then run the request again." A blocked wave says `run_again` only when every blocked worker does. The value is the step's judgment; the line is code. Text: `buildNextActionProperty` and `nextStepLine` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`, row SD-54.
- **A local review question shows the open findings (issue 441).** The plain words of decision 17 now carry their detail. The local reviewer's user-input-required and high-severity-rejection exits return `open_findings`: every finding still open in the fix ledger, each with its `finding_id`, `severity`, `title`, `body` and `recommendation`, copied as data. The question names each finding by the first 8 code points of its `finding_id`, never the full id, states how many are open, and its detail block lists each one, every value encoded as external content. The compact form keeps the id, severity and title of each. Text: `plugin/agents/local-reviewer.md` (the exit fields), `buildReviewQuestionBrief` and `buildOpenFindingsBlock` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`. Amended 2026-10-25 (issues 441, 445 and 452): each open finding is now `finding_id`, `severity`, `summary` and `recommendation`, the last two short fields the reviewer writes for the user, and `title` and `body` are gone, so no Codex body reaches a question. The detail block shows the reviewer's `question` or `rationale_text`, its `recommendation_text`, and up to 5 open findings, then one line counting the rest (see Amendment 2026-10-25: user-facing text comes from bounded fields (issues 441, 445 and 452)). Amended 2026-10-28 (issue 460): `open_findings` and the detail block are gone. A local review question is the reviewer's own `user_question`, with its own options (see Amendment 2026-10-28: one message shape (issue 460)).
- **The contract rows moved to `contract.js`.** `contractRows()` is test data, so it moves from `main.js` to `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/contract.js`. The build reads only `meta.js` and `main.js`, so the rows never reach the built script, and the tests load the file and require each row's check to be a function of the built script. The 190,000 character budget of decision 18 holds unchanged, with room for this fix. Text: `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/contract.js`, `tests/workflow-scripts/standard-delivery.test.mjs`.

It updates these places in place, each with a dated note:

- Step invariants: the Text line names `contract.js` as the home of the contract table.
- Amendment 2026-10-23, the residual "plan acceptance shows the summary, not the plan": the question now shows the line built in code and the pointer to the full plan. Its bound and rejected options stand.
- Risks, step text in a blocked message, residual (b): verbatim shows only for exact asks.
- PR8 live smoke: the D1 row says the exact text stays in the PR body and the reuse comment and leaves the final message.
- Deferred items: the row on delivery-tail questions names the open findings detail.

The rest of ADR-0037 is unchanged.

Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/445, https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/441.

## Amendment 2026-10-23: local review holds the pull request on every path (issue 444)

This amendment covers https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/444, under epic https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/442. It updates one place in place, with a dated note: the issue 434 bullet of the amendment on question keys, blocked stops and the question template. It also adds one Deferred items row, for the state-machine gates residual below. The rest of ADR-0037 is unchanged.

- **Root cause.** The rule for which local review exits hold the gate before a pull request lived only inside the two engines: `localReviewRoutes()` in the workflow script and the `local_review` transitions in `plugin/workflows/standard-delivery.json`. A path outside both read its PR preconditions from workflow.md (PR Requirements) and the open-plan-pr checklist. Both named validation and the version bump, but not local review. The issue 428 run shows the effect. Its delivery tail stopped blocked, and the overlord finished the tail by hand. It ran the local reviewer with an iteration limit of 1, which no rule allowed. The reviewer fixed a high-severity finding in that round and returned `max-iterations-reached`. The overlord took that exit as a pass and opened PR 443, so the fix reached the pull request unreviewed. Also, at a `before_pr` stop the script's own next-action line said the pull request can be opened from those commits, even when a gate had not held on them. The state machine also sent `diminishing-returns` straight from `local_review` to `open_pr`, so an advisory nobody took could open the pull request.
- **One home for the gate rule.** workflow.md (PR Requirements) is the one statement of which local review exits hold the gate and which hold nothing. Other text points to it and does not restate it (P20). The open-plan-pr checklist checks it, and its stop list names it. Rejected: a copy of the exit list in each consumer, since a second copy drifts (P22).
- **The `before_pr` line no longer offers a pull request.** It says no pull request opens until every gate holds on those commits.
- **The reviewer limit is fixed at 10.** The local reviewer's iteration limit is always the `max_iterations` value of its Input Contract. A delegation that asks for any other limit, under any key name, ends blocked at initialize. Before, a caller could lower the limit with no rule allowing it, so `max-iterations-reached` could follow a single round.
- **Diminishing returns follows the two-round rule.** The reviewer honors a `diminishing-returns` verdict only when the fix-ledger shows this iteration and the one before it each found only unusual-setup edge cases or wording findings. A finding that needed a real fix resets the count. So the exit never fires at iteration 1, and never right after a real fix made in the same round.
- **Diminishing returns waits for a decision on the state machine.** `local_review` sends `diminishing-returns` to the overlord decision state `diminishing_returns_decision`, which the `diminishing_returns_exit` row of `plugin/references/decision-autonomy-table.json` resolves. Only its `taken` outcome reaches `open_pr`. `not_taken` and `blocked` end the run at `blocked`, before the pull request, as an ask does on the script (SD-38). The definition moves from version 3 to version 4, so a run resumed from version 3 meets the version-skew doors. Rejected: keeping the direct route and recording the state-machine path as a judgment residual, because a declarative state closes it. Rejected: sending `not_taken` back to `local_review` or to `review_remediation_plan`, because a terminal route cannot reach the pull request or loop, and it matches the script.
- **Eliminated class.** A pull request opened from commits whose local review did not hold the gate, on any path that reads workflow.md: the workflow script, the state machine, or a step taken by hand. A test fails when the script table, the state machine or the workflow.md statement disagree. The only state-machine transitions into `open_pr` are `local_review` `clean`, `diminishing_returns_decision` `taken` and `local_review_decision` `skipped`, and a test fails on any other. This class does not cover a commit made after an earlier gate on the state machine; see "Recorded residual: state-machine gates" below.
- **Text homes.** `plugin/governance/workflow.md` (PR Requirements). `plugin/skills/open-plan-pr/SKILL.md` (the checklist and the stop list). `plugin/agents/local-reviewer.md` (The Loop, steps 1 and 9). `nextActionLine` and `localReviewRoutes` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`, rows SD-23 and SD-35. `plugin/workflows/standard-delivery.json` (`local_review` and `diminishing_returns_decision`). The `diminishing_returns_exit` row of `plugin/references/decision-autonomy-table.json`. The policy fixture `tests/policy/safety-pr-requires-local-review.json` pins the workflow.md statement and its consumers.
- **Residual: the hand path stays a judgment path.** Root cause: review questions in the delivery tail have no reply path on the script, so a blocked tail can still be finished by hand. That path is gated by workflow.md and the open-plan-pr checklist, which a model reads and follows. They are not a control the script enforces. Bounded impact: every pull request is in front of the user before merge. The residual goes away when https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/441 gives review questions a reply path on the script. Closed 2026-10-25 by issue 453: a review question no longer ends the run. It pauses, and the reply goes back to the local reviewer in the same run, so no tail ends on a question for a hand path to finish. Blocked stops that are not questions are out of that issue's scope (see Amendment 2026-10-25: open questions pause on both engines, and a run can be stopped (issue 453)).
- **Recorded residual: state-machine gates.** On the state machine, the gates run in a line: version, validation, local review, then `open_pr`. A commit made after a gate, such as a local review fix or a validation fix, does not send the run back through the earlier gates, so the pull request can open on a commit that some gate did not end on. This is inherited from main, not introduced by issue 444. Root cause: the two engines carry separate copies of one gate contract. The script holds it in `runGates` (invariant 2); the state machine holds its own copy in its transitions. Codex local review findings f08ff051, c5e393e1, a0f4552d, e0b6fa5f and 01131cd5 on this branch are members of this class. Rejected: patching the state-machine copy, because three structural attempts on this branch each surfaced the next missing invariant. Fix: slice 4 runs brood children on the script engine. After that, the state machine runs only on a refused launch, where it must stop before the pull request and ask the user. Tracked on https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/409. Bounded impact: the state machine runs only in brood children and refused launches, the local reviewer validates its own fixes, and a human reviews every pull request before merge. Closed 2026-10-24 by slice 4: brood children run the script, and the state machine stops at the `pr_confirm` user gate before the pull request (see Amendment 2026-10-24: brood children on the script engine (slice 4)).

Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/444, https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/441, https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/442.

## Amendment 2026-10-24: brood children on the script engine (slice 4)

This amendment records slice 4 of https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/409. Root cause it closes: the two engines each carry their own copy of one gate contract ("Recorded residual: state-machine gates"), and brood children were the main user of the state machine. After this slice, brood children run the script, and the state machine for standard-delivery runs only on a refused launch, where it stops before the pull request and asks.

- **Children launch the script, which ends decision 10.** spawn-brood launches each child without `--disallowedTools Workflow`, so a child routes and launches `hivemind:standard-delivery` as ADR-0036 decision 2 says for brood children. In auto mode the classifier launches the workflow with no prompt. Text: `plugin/skills/spawn-brood/scripts/spawn-brood.sh`, `plugin/skills/spawn-brood/SKILL.md`.
- **Detached boot.** Each worktree is created with `git worktree add --detach` at the resolved base commit, and spawn-brood creates no strain branch. The child's planner names its own working branch (decisions 2 and 14), as the user decided earlier. The manifest carries no per-strain branch. Text: `plugin/skills/spawn-brood/scripts/spawn-brood.sh`, `plugin/references/brood-ledger-model.md`.
- **task.md carries data only.** It holds the data-boundary preamble first, then a data-only block of brood metadata and the strain description, with no instruction to the child. Reason: the harness relays the request to every workflow agent and tells each one that it wins over its task (decision 24), so an instruction in task.md would reach every runner. Text: `plugin/skills/spawn-brood/scripts/spawn-brood.sh`, `plugin/skills/spawn-brood/SKILL.md`.
- **The facts file is started-evidence.** A script run writes no run ledger (decision 2), so a gather-facts output file under the child worktree's `.hivemind/facts/` now counts as started-evidence beside a run-ledger `state.current`. One shared function, `hivemind_detect_facts_evidence`, serves spawn and the brood-status dashboard, so the two cannot disagree. Neither source can come from the base: the run-ledger leaf is keyed by the run id spawn generates from the brood GUID, and spawn refuses a strain whose new worktree already holds `.hivemind/facts`, so every facts file spawn or brood-status reads was written after launch. That proves when the file was written, not that gather-facts wrote it (see Recorded residuals below: "A process that runs after launch can write a facts-shaped file"). A hostile-base test in `tools/test_brood_compat.sh` pins this. The user chose this on 2026-10-24 over a started marker written by the child, because it reuses ground truth the engine already writes, and because a marker would need an instruction in task.md, which must stay data-only. Text: `plugin/skills/_shared/brood-state.sh` (`hivemind_detect_facts_evidence` and the pre-launch guard `hivemind_brood_facts_absent`; moved there from `ledger-project.sh` on 2026-10-24, finding 3918bfb0 below), `plugin/skills/_shared/brood-status-derive.sh`, `plugin/skills/spawn-brood/scripts/spawn-brood.sh`; tests: `tools/test_shared_libs.sh`, `tools/test_brood_compat.sh`.
- **Auto mode and remote control, by user decision on 2026-10-24.** Children launch with `claude --permission-mode auto` in place of bypass mode. After the manifest is written, spawn-brood turns on remote control (`/rc <short>`) for each strain that showed started-evidence and was alive when spawn ended, through `rc-brood.sh --only`, so it reuses the enable-brood-remote pane-id delivery. A remote-control failure prints a warning line and changes neither the manifest nor the exit code. The user owns monitoring the brood. On 2026-10-24 the user rejected Codex local review finding d570f77e on this branch. That finding said remote control needs an explicit request, as base ADR-0027 says. The user confirmed default-on spawn-time remote control as their decision. This is the user's chosen exception to the stop calibration for brood children: a bypass child never stopped on a permission check, and now a classifier pause can stop a child until the user answers it through remote control or the attach line. ADR-0017 and ADR-0027 record the matching amendments, and ADR-0035 decision 12 lands with this slice. Text: `plugin/skills/spawn-brood/scripts/spawn-brood.sh`, `plugin/skills/enable-brood-remote/scripts/rc-brood.sh` (`--only`).
- **The state machine stops before the pull request (definition version 5).** `plugin/workflows/standard-delivery.json` gains the `pr_confirm` user gate. Every route that reached `open_pr` (`local_review` `clean`, `diminishing_returns_decision` `taken` and `local_review_decision` `skipped`) now reaches `pr_confirm`. The overlord shows the commits made after each gate ended and asks. Only `approved` reaches `open_pr`; `rejected` ends at `blocked` and `cancelled` at `cancelled`. The definition moves from version 4 to version 5, so a run resumed from version 4 meets the version-skew doors. The local-reviewer note that pointed at the old route into the pull request now points at the gate. This closes "Recorded residual: state-machine gates" on the only remaining fallback path, a refused launch: the state machine can still open a pull request on a commit some gate did not end on, but only after the user has seen those commits and approved. Text: `plugin/workflows/standard-delivery.json` (`pr_confirm`), `plugin/agents/local-reviewer.md`; test: `tests/workflow-scripts/standard-delivery.test.mjs`.
- **Spike S8 is not needed.** S8 asked which base a worktree made for a workflow would start from. Brood children stay separate Claude Code sessions in their own worktrees (ADR-0007), created by spawn-brood at the resolved base commit, so no workflow creates a worktree.
- **Disposition of the #409 review comments.** https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/409#issuecomment-6385534658: the pre-PR state-machine copies of the gate contract now sit behind the `pr_confirm` user gate on the refused-launch path, and they retire with the state machine in slice 5. https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/409#issuecomment-6385027849: the post-PR version gate stays slice 3, with post-PR remediation on the engine.
- **hatchery-dispatch stays on the state machine.** Splitting a request into strains is the hatchery-dispatch workflow, which this slice leaves on the state machine. It is slice 5 scope, with the retirement of the JSON engine. The plan schema keeps no brood delivery (decision 14).
- **Same-user brood children are not a security boundary, by user decision on 2026-10-24 (option A).** Codex local review findings 9679f16f, 3918bfb0, 5e027e2f, 3ca37915 and d03f1570 on this branch share one class: each assumes a hostile brood child, or a race by one, that swaps a symlink or renames a file or directory in the coordinator checkout between a check and a later read or write. Brood children run as the same OS user as the coordinator, with full filesystem access, in auto mode, so a hostile child can edit coordinator files directly and needs no race. The user settled the class by this threat model, not by more hardening: findings that assume a hostile same-user child, or a race by one, are outside the threat model and are rejected. Recorded in `plugin/governance/security-policy.md` (Trust-Boundary Discipline, item 3). Removed, with their tests and policy pins: the verified directory-handle helpers in `plugin/skills/_shared/containment.sh`, which returns to main's content; spawn-brood's handle writes, handle cleanup and task paste from memory; the handle walk and the descriptor-identity read in `brood-state.sh`; the one-time snapshot of the coordinator settings, whose only job was stopping a swap between steps; and CHECK 17 in `tools/policy_check.sh` with its pin `tests/policy/safety-brood-state-reader.json`. Kept: the exact tmux `=session` and pane-id targets (finding eadbd5d2); the `settings.local.json` leaf guards, both the child-side guard against a symlink committed in the base and the coordinator-side refusal of a symlinked or non-regular file before any worktree exists; one read of the spawn inputs file into one snapshot, parsed once; the manifest temp file created exclusively under a random name (finding 0c6b006b), which stops a stray file at the temp path from redirecting the write; the accident-level checks for a stray symlink, a non-regular file, a NUL byte and several JSON documents; and the one shared brood-state reader below. Text: `plugin/governance/security-policy.md`, `plugin/skills/_shared/containment.sh`, `plugin/skills/_shared/brood-state.sh`, `plugin/skills/spawn-brood/scripts/spawn-brood.sh`; tests: `tools/test_shared_libs.sh`, `tools/test_brood_compat.sh`.
- **Every coordinator read of brood state goes through one reader.** Root cause: each coordinator script that read brood state wrote its own read. These were the brood-status projector's manifest and child-ledger reads, the collector's second read of the manifest for its brood-id check, `rc-brood.sh`'s manifest read, the `brood-discover.sh` glob, and the path-based readers in `ledger-project.sh` and `manifest-json.sh` that spawn-brood also used. Each relied on jq or a separate `tr` or `wc` pass to catch a NUL byte, and each repeated the same checks. The fix: one sourced library, `plugin/skills/_shared/brood-state.sh`, is the only code that reads brood state. It takes identifiers (a checkout root and a brood id, or a worktree root and a run id) and derives every location from them. It tests each directory component under the canonical root, refuses a symlinked or non-directory component and a symlinked or non-regular leaf, reads each file once, refuses a NUL or any other raw control byte, and parses that same snapshot as exactly one JSON document. Every consumer moved onto it: the projector takes a brood id instead of a manifest path and prints a `BROOD` line with the manifest's validated `brood_id`, so the collector never opens a manifest; the brood listing, `rc-brood.sh`, and spawn-brood's child-ledger, facts-evidence and pre-launch facts-guard reads use it too. A symlinked or non-directory `.hivemind/broods` is a blocker, not an empty list. The path-based readers in `ledger-project.sh` and `manifest-json.sh` were deleted. Why the reader stays after the threat-model decision: it is smaller and simpler than the scattered reads it replaced. With the handle code removed, `brood-state.sh` has 375 lines, and the path-based readers it replaced in `ledger-project.sh` and `manifest-json.sh` had 411, before counting the per-script reads in the brood-status scripts and `rc-brood.sh`. The checks are accident-level data hygiene, not isolation (see the bullet above). Text: `plugin/skills/_shared/brood-state.sh`, `plugin/skills/brood-status/scripts/brood-status-project.sh`, `plugin/skills/brood-status/scripts/brood-status-collect.sh`, `plugin/skills/brood-status/scripts/brood-discover.sh`, `plugin/skills/enable-brood-remote/scripts/rc-brood.sh`, `plugin/skills/spawn-brood/scripts/spawn-brood.sh`, `plugin/governance/security-policy.md` (Trust-Boundary Discipline, item 3); tests: `tools/test_shared_libs.sh`, `tools/test_brood_compat.sh`, `tools/test_script_bootstrap.sh`.

Recorded residuals:

- **The dashboard of script-engine children.** Root cause: the brood-status claimed-state columns come from a child's run ledger, and a script run writes none (decision 2). The facts file shows that a child started, not where it is. Bounded impact: for a script child the claimed-state columns stay blank, and a paused or blocked child shows `running` while its session lives. This is display only: the child is reachable by remote control or the attach line, and its pull request, when it opens, shows on the dashboard. Why the obvious fix was rejected: a state file written by the child needs an instruction in task.md, which must stay data-only (decision 24), or a run record, which decision 2 rejects.
- **Strains still starting when spawn ends get no remote control.** Root cause: spawn-brood sends `/rc` only to strains with started-evidence and a live session when it ends, because a keystroke sent to a child that is still booting can land in a boot dialog or in the task input. Bounded impact: such a strain gets a `remote: skipped` line and runs on, reachable by the attach line, until the user asks for `hivemind:enable-brood-remote`, which reaches every live strain. Why the obvious fix was rejected: holding spawn open until every strain starts makes one slow child delay the whole brood past the started-evidence window, and sending `/rc` blind risks the swallowed paste above.
- **Hosts where auto mode is unavailable.** Root cause: auto mode depends on the account and on the classifier model (reason 1 of ADR-0017's #170 amendment). Where it is unavailable, the child starts in manual mode. Bounded impact: the child waits at its first prompt, writes no started-evidence, shows `starting`, and gets no remote control. Nothing runs past the prompt without the user, and the attach line reaches it. Why the obvious fix was rejected: falling back to bypass mode brings back the unattended blast radius this slice removes.
- **A process that runs after launch can write a facts-shaped file.** Root cause: started-evidence is a file found by path and name shape, tied to no run. The pre-launch absence guard proves only that a later file was written after launch, not that gather-facts wrote it. The run-ledger source has the same exposure, because task.md carries the run id. The class is inherited: on main, any process in the child worktree could write the run-ledger `state.current`. Bounded impact: the possible writers are base-tree code, such as a SessionStart hook or an MCP server, which runs only under the base-trust precondition in `plugin/governance/security-policy.md` (Brood Spawn Bypass-Mode Mitigation), the child itself, or a process with the user's own rights. The effect is on coordination only: spawn and the dashboard show a false `running`, spawn skips its bare-Enter nudge, and `/rc` can reach a child that is still booting. Nothing is committed, pushed or merged on that basis, and the user owns monitoring the brood. Why the obvious fix was rejected: a spawn-generated nonce checked inside a record the workflow writes would have to reach the child through task.md or the launch args, where the same writers can read it, so it moves trust without closing the class. It would also change the gather-facts output contract and the data-only task.md of decision 24. Per the initiative rule, no check is stacked on a class this engine cannot close. Linked findings: Codex local review finding 3a8c859b on this branch, and round-one finding 24808b07 of the same class, whose fix ae8f27f closed only the pre-launch base-tree source.
- **Spawn inputs carry no byte limits.** Root cause: `spawn-brood.sh` checks the inputs file's JSON syntax, its required fields and the value classes of `name`, `base` and `overlap_risk`, but sets no byte limit on the file or on any field: `description`, `overlap_details`, `workflow_hint`, `hatchery.run_id` and `hatchery.workflow`. Each value is read through a jq command substitution. After launch, the manifest jq call gets each one as an `--arg`. One field over the Linux per-argument limit (128 KiB, `MAX_ARG_STRLEN`) fails that call, so the run prints its `recovery:` line naming every live session and exits 1 with no manifest. The class is inherited: the 7.0.5 release has the same reads and the same post-launch `--arg` call. Bounded impact: the navigator writes the inputs file from task text the human approved before spawn (`plugin/governance/security-policy.md`, Brood Spawn Bypass-Mode Mitigation control 2). The human sees any oversized text at that approval. Every failure stays in the operator's own sessions and worktrees, the `recovery:` line names each live session, and nothing is committed, pushed or merged because of it. Why the obvious fix was rejected: byte limits add new pre-flight stops and change the spawn-brood inputs contract, with thresholds that would be arbitrary, for text a trusted author wrote and a human approved. Per the initiative rule, no check is stacked on this branch for an inherited class. Promotion: if spawn inputs ever come from a source the human did not approve, this residual becomes a tracked issue with the same scope. Linked findings: Codex local review finding 86d817fb on this branch (round s6, iteration 1).

It updates these places in place, each with a dated note:

- Scope: the interim start of brood children has ended.
- Decisions 10, 14 and 20.
- Settled ADR-0035 open items and ADR-0036 deferred items: the two brood rows.
- Deferred items: the brood, S8 and auto mode row, and the state-machine gates row, both closed.
- Recorded residuals: the brood sentence of "An effect outside the repo that the plan does not declare".
- Amendment 2026-10-23: local review holds the pull request on every path, "Recorded residual: state-machine gates": closed.
- This amendment, on 2026-10-24 for finding 3918bfb0: the new bullet "Every coordinator read of brood state goes through one reader", and the Text pointer of "The facts file is started-evidence", which moves to `brood-state.sh`.
- This amendment, on 2026-10-24 by the user's threat-model decision (option A): the bullet "spawn-brood reads and writes only through checked handles" is replaced by "Same-user brood children are not a security boundary"; "Every coordinator read of brood state goes through one reader" is rewritten without verified handles or a policy check; and the recorded residuals "Launching in a directory", "Limits of the verified-handle helpers" and "The state-machine ledger writers" are removed, settled by that threat model.

The rest of ADR-0037 is unchanged.

Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/409, `docs/adr/0007-fleet-children-unaware-coordinator-dashboard.md`, `docs/adr/0017-brood-spawn-mechanism.md`, `docs/adr/0027-agent-invocable-remote-control-via-description-scoping.md`, `docs/adr/0035-one-script-per-workflow-engine.md`.

## Amendment 2026-10-24: first-finding triage on the script engine (issue 448)

This amendment covers the script changes of https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/448. `docs/adr/0038-root-cause-at-the-first-finding.md` records the decisions, and `plugin/governance/remediation-doctrine.md` states the rules. The script follows the doctrine and copies none of it. The state-machine definitions are unchanged.

- **`rejected_findings` is a required review field.** The local reviewer returns every finding it rejected under a recorded decision, each with its `finding_id`, `title` and `record`, the path and section of the decision it cites. The list is empty when there are none. Each value is labelled as able to carry external content. A result without the field stops the run blocked, as any result that fails `REVIEW_SCHEMA` does. Text: `rejectedFindingTexts`, `buildFindingItemSchema`, `buildOpenFindingSchema`, `buildReviewSchema` and `buildReviewPrompt` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`, row SD-33.
- **The review gate note gives the count.** When the review that held the gate lists rejected findings, its note adds a code-built count: "N findings rejected under recorded decisions". The pull request body and comment carry that count and no rejected-finding text, so no Codex text reaches the pull request. Text: `describeRejectedCount`, `routeLocalReview`, `resolveDiminishingReturns`, `runReviewGate` and `runGates`.
- **The details go in chat only.** The final complete message carries a detail block right after its first block, headed by that note. It names each rejected finding by the first 8 code points of its `finding_id`, with its title and record, each value encoded as external content. Its compact form keeps only the id and the title. An empty list adds nothing. Text: `renderRejectedFindingLine`, `buildRejectedFindingsBlock`, `finishDeliveryComplete` and `runPullRequestStep`, row SD-55. Amended 2026-10-28 (local review finding 7703cd64): the block names each rejected finding by its short id only, with no title and no record (see Amendment 2026-10-28: one message shape (issue 460)).
- **No zoom-out trigger.** The script sets no zoom-out trigger, and every review replan gets the same remediation reason. The planner makes the zoom-out call by judgment from the branch commits in its fresh facts (ADR-0038 decision 3). This branch's first draft keyed the reason on a run-wide flag, which made any second review replan look like a non-closing structural fix (local review finding 65415fd0), so the flag and its branch are removed. Remediation plan acceptance reads the run's taken review-routing decision instead of the flag, with unchanged behavior. Text: `requestReviewReplan`, `resolvePlanAcceptance` and `createRunState`, rows SD-39 and SD-40.
- **Only the escalation reaches the planner.** A local review that sends the run back to planning passes the planner only its `exit_reason` and the fields that `exit_reason` names. So a finding rejected under a recorded decision, the fix ledger path, the open findings and the counts never reach remediation planning. The projection is a positive allowlist drawn from the same per-exit field table the review schema uses. Text: `projectReplanReview` and `requestReviewReplan` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`, row SD-39.
- **The rejection option text.** Option A of a `high_severity_rejection` question now says that rejecting the finding has the next run record the decision at its source, so review stops raising it. Decision 17 stands: the question still ends the run before the pull request until slice 3. Text: `buildReviewQuestionBrief`, row SD-37. Amended 2026-10-25 (issue 453): the question now pauses the run instead of ending it. Option A says local review records the decision at its source and goes on, and option B says local review fixes the finding, or sends it to the planner, and goes on (see Amendment 2026-10-25: open questions pause on both engines, and a run can be stopped (issue 453)).
- **Review values carry their domain.** Each conditional array field of the review result holds at least one entry, so a field the workflow forwards to the planner or shows the user is never present but empty. `member_count` is gone from the review result, so the root-cluster escalation brief names no count and the planner gets `cluster_ids`, which no count can disagree with. `buildReviewFieldSchema` refuses a conditional field type without a domain: it throws on any type other than string, list or findings. Counts are floored at zero by the validator (ADR-0036, Amendment 2026-10-24: integers are counts). A result outside these domains stops the run blocked, as any result that fails `REVIEW_SCHEMA` does. Text: `reviewFieldTexts`, `reviewExitFields`, `buildReviewFieldSchema` and `buildReviewEscalationBrief` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`, rows SD-33 and SD-39.

The built `plugin/workflows/standard-delivery.js` is 178,341 characters, within the 190,000 character budget of decision 18. The rest of ADR-0037 is unchanged.

Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/448, `docs/adr/0038-root-cause-at-the-first-finding.md`.

## Amendment 2026-10-25: fix round files are checked by molt.sh alone (local review finding c2e0ada3)

**The finding.** Local review finding c2e0ada3 said the `files` list of a fix round result accepts empty and non-path members: blank, absolute, traversal or `.git` parts. It asked the workflow to check them.

**Decided 2026-10-25, user call: rejected.** This is a recorded decision under `plugin/governance/remediation-doctrine.md` "## Recorded Decisions and Repeat Findings" (ADR-0038 decision 4). It is not a recorded residual, because no defect is left unfixed.

**Reasons.**

- A blank member already fails `validateSchema` in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/schema.js`, which refuses a blank string for a `type: string` with no `enum` and no `minLength`.
- A traversal or absolute path reaches only `molt.sh`, whose `is_exact_path` rule in `plugin/skills/molt/scripts/molt.sh` refuses it before any change. That is the recorded design in the `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js` header ("Fix round files are not plan fields"). The parity test "isRepositoryPath gives the verdict of the molt.sh files rule on one corpus" in `tests/workflow-scripts/standard-delivery.test.mjs` keeps the two rules the same.
- An empty list is the designed no-change fix round, after which validation runs again (test "a fix round that commits nothing reruns only validation").
- A check in the workflow would copy the `molt.sh` rule and change no behavior. It adds surface and removes none (doctrine "## Surface-Growth Escalation"). It also names no class it eliminates, so it fails "### Closed-by-Construction Acceptance Test".

**Scope.** A finding is a REPEAT of this decision when it asks standard-delivery to check fix round `files` members for blank, empty, absolute, traversal or `.git` shape before `molt.sh`. A finding is NEW, and gets First-Finding Triage, when it shows any of these:

- a fix round path that reaches a change before the `molt.sh` rule runs;
- `molt.sh` accepting a path its rule should refuse;
- the parity between `isRepositoryPath` and `molt.sh` breaking.

**The key.** This record is matched by the scope above, not by the finding id, since finding ids change from run to run.

The rest of ADR-0037 is unchanged.

Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/448, `docs/adr/0038-root-cause-at-the-first-finding.md`.

## Amendment 2026-10-25: a suspect scan result needs no diagnostic field (local review finding 87c0420e)

**The finding.** Local review finding 87c0420e said the injection-suspect variant of `scanStepSchema` in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/facts.js` leaves `pattern_category`, `field_excerpt` and `reason` optional. It asked the schema to require them.

**Decided 2026-10-25, user call: rejected.** This is a recorded decision under `plugin/governance/remediation-doctrine.md` "## Recorded Decisions and Repeat Findings" (ADR-0038 decision 4). It is not a recorded residual, because no defect is left unfixed.

**Reasons.**

- The design is inherited from `main` and tested. On `main` the scan result schema already left the three fields optional. Commit b0f59aa added the test "a suspect with no category or excerpt still blocks" in `tests/workflow-scripts/analysis-only.test.mjs`, and `renderSuspectLine` shows "no category given" and an empty excerpt for a suspect that carries neither.
- `describeScanProblem` stops the run before planning for any suspect, whatever fields it carries. A result that fails `scanStepSchema` stops the run at the same point. So requiring the fields changes no routing, and no flagged text reaches the planner either way.
- The change names no class it eliminates, so it fails "### Closed-by-Construction Acceptance Test". It adds a check and removes none (doctrine "## Surface-Growth Escalation").

**Scope.** A finding is a REPEAT of this decision when it asks the scan result schema to require any of `pattern_category`, `field_excerpt` or `reason` on an injection-suspect result, or flags that a suspect may omit them. A finding is NEW, and gets First-Finding Triage, when it shows any of these:

- a suspect that does not stop the run before planning;
- flagged text that reaches the planner;
- a suspect line that shows a value without `encodeUntrustedText`;
- scan routing that depends on those fields.

The local reviewer's own injection-suspect exit in `reviewExitFields` is out of this record's scope.

**The key.** This record is matched by the scope above, not by the finding id, since finding ids change from run to run.

The rest of ADR-0037 is unchanged.

Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/448, `docs/adr/0038-root-cause-at-the-first-finding.md`.

## Amendment 2026-10-25: local review counts are display only (local review finding de83f9cf)

**The finding.** Local review finding de83f9cf said `iterations_completed`, `findings_resolved` and `findings_open` in `buildReviewSchema` (`plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`) are not bounded or checked against each other. It asked the workflow to check them.

**Decided 2026-10-25, user call: rejected.** This is a recorded decision under `plugin/governance/remediation-doctrine.md` "## Recorded Decisions and Repeat Findings" (ADR-0038 decision 4). It is not a recorded residual, because no defect is left unfixed.

**Reasons.**

- The design is inherited from `main`, where `REVIEW_SCHEMA` held the same three integer counts with no bound and no cross-check.
- The counts are display only. `localReviewRoutes` keys only on `exit_reason`. `projectReplanReview` keeps them from the planner, since no exit reason that replans names a count in `reviewExitFields`. They show only in the review stop message of `max-iterations-reached`, in the diminishing-returns question, and in the diminishing-returns gate note, which the pull request body and comment carry as a number.
- A negative count already fails `validateSchema` in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/schema.js` (ADR-0036, Amendment 2026-10-24: integers are counts).
- Doctrine "## Bounded-Impact Gating" assesses them as informational only. A count validator adds a check path and removes none (doctrine "## Surface-Growth Escalation").

**Scope.** A finding is a REPEAT of this decision when it asks standard-delivery to bound these counts, for example against `maxReviewIterations()`, or to check them against each other, `exit_reason`, `open_findings` or `rejected_findings`. A finding is NEW, and gets First-Finding Triage, when it shows any of these:

- a count that routes the run or holds or releases a gate;
- a count that reaches the planner;
- a count that reaches the pull request text other than as the `findings_open` number in the diminishing-returns gate note;
- a negative or non-integer count that passes the validator.

**The key.** This record is matched by the scope above, not by the finding id, since finding ids change from run to run.

The rest of ADR-0037 is unchanged.

Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/448, `docs/adr/0038-root-cause-at-the-first-finding.md`.

## Amendment 2026-10-25: open questions pause on both engines, and a run can be stopped (issue 453)

This amendment covers https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/453. It changes both engines and folds the post-PR watch on the current engine into the same change, by the user's call.

- **Root cause.** Whether a run paused or ended on a question was keyed on where the question came up and on a round count, not on whether an answer lets the run go on. A planner question paused up to the pause limit and then ended the run. A question after the last wave never paused: it ended the run blocked under stop reason `before_pr`. That key was inherited from the issue 434 amendment above and from decision 17. On the state machine, every planner and reviewer question outcome (`open_questions`, `user-input-required`, `high-severity-rejection`) routed to an end state: `blocked`, `user_input_required` or `review_rejected`. So an answer the user gave could only reach a new run, which planned again from the start.
- **The user's calls.** Q1: fold the watch into this pull request (option A), so the state-machine watch gets the same reply path in the same change. Q2: the question gate goes into every state-machine definition, not only the delivery and watch definitions (option A). Q3: a review that ran with an answer and then escalates carries the answer into its replan (option A).
- **Every open question pauses the delivery run (script engine).** The reply resumes the same run, and earlier agents replay from cache. standard-delivery has no pause limit: `pauseLimit()` in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/result.js` now applies to analysis-only alone, which keeps its cap. A delivery run still ends blocked with questions open only when the replies are too long to carry (`reply_size`) or an exact question is too long to show whole (`exact_text_size`). `before_pr` is gone, and `nextActionLine` has one form for both stops (Amended 2026-10-28, local review finding 49be981e: `nextActionLine` is gone, and both stops are one `answer` cause through `finishDeliveryBlocked`). A tail ask pauses through `pauseDelivery`, which now also carries the open findings detail block of a review question and each gate that did not run. Tail rounds share `run.replies` and the round numbering with planning rounds. Text: `askTail`, `isRunEnd`, `buildUndecidedAsk`, `pauseDelivery`, `runPlanningPass`, `nextActionLine` and `finishOpenQuestions` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`, with `finishTailAsk` removed; `renderStopIntro` in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/brief.js`, which drops `before_pr`; the `pauseLimit` header of `plugin/workflow-src/lib/result.js`; rows SD-09, SD-20, SD-23 and SD-51.
- **An answer to a local review question goes back to the local reviewer.** The reply reruns the review gate on the current head, with the next `gateLabel` count and `user_answer` `{question, reply}` in its inputs block. The reviewer continues from its persisted fix ledger, so the fixed limit of 10 iterations holds across the answer. A finding the user rejects is recorded at its source per `plugin/governance/remediation-doctrine.md` (Recorded Decisions and Repeat Findings): the reviewer commits the record through `hivemind:molt` and goes on. A finding the user wants fixed goes through the reviewer's fix step. A reply that settles nothing returns `blocked`. The commit runner and `localReviewRoutes()` apply to the rerun as to any review run. Option A of a `high_severity_rejection` question now says local review records the decision at its source and goes on, and option B says local review fixes the finding or sends it to the planner. Text: `askReviewQuestion` (which replaces `finishReviewQuestion`), `buildReviewQuestionBrief`, `buildTailBrief` (the `other` option text), `buildReviewPrompt`, `runReviewGate` and `runGates` (`gates.answer`) in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`; `plugin/agents/local-reviewer.md` (the `user_answer` input and The Loop step 1); `plugin/governance/remediation-doctrine.md` (the write obligation in Recorded Decisions and Repeat Findings, and Engine Consumers); rows SD-32, SD-37 and SD-57.
- **Any other answer after the last wave starts a new planning pass.** The bump verdict that needs the user, the fix round and gate rerun limits, and a surfaced `validation_remediation`, `gate_rerun`, `diminishing_returns_exit`, `planner_escalation_routing` or `root_cluster_routing` ask each pause. The reply starts a new pass through the facts step (invariant 3), and its plan prompt carries an answer block `{question, reply}` as the user's decision on that question. That replan does not count against `maxReplans()`. Option B of each such tail brief now says the run plans again with the reply. Text: `replanFromTailAsk`, `resolveTailDecision`, `hasReplanAnswer`, `buildPlanPrompt`, `buildBumpIndeterminableBrief`, `buildFixLimitBrief`, `buildGateRerunBrief`, `runValidateGate`, `resolveGateRerun` and `resolveDiminishingReturns` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`; rows SD-07, SD-26, SD-27, SD-38, SD-39 and SD-57. Amended 2026-10-29 (issue 457): the bump verdict question is gone with the bump step. A version question now comes from the validate step, and its reply runs one fix round in place and never plans again (see Amendment 2026-10-29: a stop pauses unless nothing can go on, and the version check moves into validation (issue 457)).
- **The answer is carried into a review replan (Q3).** When the review that ran with `user_answer` returns `planner-escalation` or `root-cluster-suspected` and its routing is taken, the replan carries the same answer block beside the review block, and counts against `maxReplans()` as any review replan does. Text: `routeLocalReview` and `requestReviewReplan` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`, row SD-57.
- **The reply sentence of decision 24.** The review prompt with `user_answer` and a plan prompt with an answer block carry the reply itself and not the fixed sentence, as the planner's revise prompt does. Every other prompt after the first reply carries the sentence. Text: `addResumeNote`, `runPlanningPass` and `runReviewGate` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`, row SD-49.
- **The question gate on the state machine (Q2).** Every state-machine definition gains one `question` user gate, and every `open_questions`, `user-input-required` and `high-severity-rejection` transition goes there. The overlord asks the user in plain words with the options and a recommendation, records the gate outcome as the name of the state that asked, or `cancelled` when the reply cancels, and runs that state again with `user_answer`: the question as shown and the reply word for word, plus the finding's `candidate_url` when the GitHub reviewer asked. When the state that ran with an answer escalates to a planning state, that planning delegation carries the same answer. GitHub text inside a question stays external content. The new versions: `plugin/workflows/standard-delivery.json` 5 to 6 (the gate covers `plan`, `local_review`, `review_remediation_plan`, `github_review_loop` and `review_remediation_plan_postpr`), `plugin/workflows/pr-feedback-remediation.json` 3 to 4 (the gate covers `github_review_loop`, `github_reviewer_fix` and `review_remediation_plan`), `plugin/workflows/analysis-only.json` 1 to 2 (`analyze`) and `plugin/workflows/hatchery-dispatch.json` 1 to 2 (`brood_plan`). A run resumed from an older version meets the version-skew doors. Text: the four definitions; `plugin/agents/overlord.md` (`## Workflow State Execution`, the Question gate paragraph).
- **The watch takes the answer (the fold-in).** `hivemind:github-review-loop` gains a `user_answer` input, passed to the cycle-0 reviewer dispatch only. `hivemind:github-reviewer` gains the same input, matched only against the step-2 candidate URLs and never put into a command, and its reply is never posted to GitHub word for word. At step 4, a reply that keeps a high-severity rejection makes the GitHub reviewer record the decision at its source, commit and push the record with the cycle's fixes at step 7, and reply citing the record, so the candidate counts as addressed. A reply that wants the finding fixed routes it as a simple fix or a `planner-escalation`, and a reply that settles nothing returns `user-input-required` again. Text: `plugin/skills/github-review-loop/SKILL.md` (Inputs, Cycle 0 and the Dispatch contract), `plugin/agents/github-reviewer.md` (Fix Mode Input and step 4).
- **The overlord can stop a launched run.** The overlord's tools gain `TaskStop`. When the user asks to stop a launched run, from any surface including Remote Control, the overlord calls `TaskStop` with the `taskId` the `Workflow` tool returned and says in one line that the run stopped. It stops a run only when the user asks. A stopped run keeps every completed agent result, and when the user asks to go on, the overlord resumes it by name with `resumeFromRunId`, as decision 8 says. Text: `plugin/agents/overlord.md` (the `tools:` frontmatter and `## Engine`).
- **Tests and pins.** `tests/workflow-scripts/standard-delivery.test.mjs` (no pause limit, tail asks that pause, the review rerun with `user_answer`, the answer block, the replan count), `tests/workflow-scripts/brief.test.mjs`, `tests/workflow-scripts/overlord-contract.test.mjs` (the TaskStop paragraph and the Question gate paragraph), and the new `tests/workflow-scripts/state-machine-questions.test.mjs`. The policy fixture `tests/policy/safety-review-user-answer.json` (new) pins the GitHub reviewer's `user_answer` input and its consumers, and `tests/policy/safety-recorded-decision-repeat.json` pins the new write obligation of the doctrine.
- **Eliminated class.** No question ends a run on either engine. On the script, a question pauses the run while a pause can be built, and the reply goes back to the step that asked or to a new planning pass in the same run. On the state machine, `tests/workflow-scripts/state-machine-questions.test.mjs` fails the suite when any definition routes a question outcome anywhere but its `question` gate, when a gate outcome other than `cancelled` names a state that does not route a question to the gate, or when a definition declares a `user_input_required` or `review_rejected` state.
- **Surface account.** Removed: the `user_input_required` and `review_rejected` end states from the two definitions that had them, the `before_pr` stop reason, `finishTailAsk` and the delivery pause limit. Added: one `question` user gate per definition, one optional `user_answer` input on each reviewer and on the watch skill, and one answer block on the plan prompt.
- **record-state-result keeps its mapping.** `plugin/skills/record-state-result/scripts/record-state-result.sh` still maps `user_input_required` and `review_rejected` to `blocked` engine-wide. No definition now reaches either state, so the mapping is unused, and the script is unchanged.

Bounded residuals:

- **A record commit costs one more review pass.** After a reviewer commits a decision record, at least one more review pass still runs on the new commit: a Codex pass of the local reviewer's loop, or a GitHub review of the pushed commit. Bounded impact: the next pass reads the record and rejects a repeat by citing it, so the user is not asked again. Why the obvious fix was rejected: skipping the pass would let a commit through that no review read (invariant 2).
- **Tail asks at non-default autonomy levels plan again.** A tail ask that asks only at a non-default autonomy level, such as a `gate_rerun` or `validation_remediation` ask, routes its reply to a new planning pass, not back to the step that asked. Bounded impact: standard-delivery runs at the table default (decision 23), where these asks are taken, and the planner gets the answer block with fresh facts. This holds until slice 3 brings reviewer briefs and the autonomy level as `userConfig`. Amended 2026-10-29 (issue 457): version questions are excepted. Their reply runs one fix round and never plans again.
- **Two stops still end blocked.** `reply_size` and `exact_text_size` still end the run blocked with questions open, because no pause can be built. The next-action line says to run the request again with the answers in it. Amended 2026-10-29 (issue 457): these two size limits still end the run. Every other stop pauses, unless its cause is terminal, an agent failure or the launch check (see Amendment 2026-10-29: a stop pauses unless nothing can go on, and the version check moves into validation (issue 457)).
- **A question at iteration 10.** The fixed limit of 10 iterations holds across an answer, so a review question raised at the local reviewer's iteration 10 ends at `max-iterations-reached` once the answer comes back. Why the obvious fix was rejected: a fresh count after each answer would undo the fixed limit of the issue 444 amendment.
- **A resumed state machine asks again.** A state-machine run resumed at the `question` gate asks the question again, because the ledger does not store the reply. The overlord records the outcome that names the state that asked, and that state runs again with no answer.
- **The watch's cycle count restarts.** After an answer, the watch loop's cycle count starts at 0 again, as it does after a remediation push today. Bounded impact: `max_remediation_cycles` still bounds each watch, and each answer comes from the user.
- **The analysis fallback has no pause cap.** On the state machine, `analysis-only.json` sends every `open_questions` result to the gate with no limit, while the script keeps `pauseLimit()`. Bounded impact: each round waits for the user, who can cancel at the gate.
- **The stop rests on documented contracts.** Stopping a workflow with `TaskStop` on its `taskId` rests on the documented `Workflow` and `TaskStop` contracts. No live check ran on this branch. A headless `claude -p` check that launches a trivial workflow and stops it is owed on https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/453, and the stop part of that issue stays open until it passes.

Out of scope: blocked stops that are not questions, such as a failed pull request step (https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/447), a git preflight that needs a user action, and a failed state-machine validation; and the wording of approval questions (https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/452). Amended 2026-10-29 (issue 457): on the script engine, those stops now pause unless their cause is terminal, a failed pull request step and a dirty tree at launch included. The state machine's stops are unchanged (see Amendment 2026-10-29: a stop pauses unless nothing can go on, and the version check moves into validation (issue 457)).

The built `plugin/workflows/standard-delivery.js` is 184,790 characters, within the 190,000 character budget of decision 18.

It updates these places in place, each with a dated note:

- Decisions 4, 17 and 24.
- Consequences: the line on planner questions.
- Deferred items: the row on post-PR remediation on the engine, and the row on delivery-tail questions, settled.
- Amendment 2026-10-23 on question keys, blocked stops and the question template: the bullets on planner question keys, the guard against a settled question coming back, and issue 434.
- Amendment 2026-10-23 on local review holding the pull request: "Residual: the hand path stays a judgment path", closed.
- Amendment 2026-10-24 on first-finding triage: the bullet "The rejection option text".
- Recorded residuals: open questions recorded as complete on the current engine (smoke-603 N1).

`docs/adr/0038-root-cause-at-the-first-finding.md` gains matching notes on decision 4, the Q1 C option and the consequence on the first rejection. The rest of ADR-0037 is unchanged.

Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/453, https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/434, https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/441, https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/444, `docs/adr/0038-root-cause-at-the-first-finding.md`.

## Amendment 2026-10-25: no cumulative pause cap for delivery runs (local review finding 2884d256)

**The finding.** Local review finding 2884d256, severity high, titled "Standard-delivery no longer has a finite pause/resume bound", said the issue 453 change removed the `canPause` and `pauseLimit` guard from standard-delivery. A planner that keeps returning a changed or unsettled question then makes the loop call `pauseDelivery` again with no ceiling. It recommended a fixed, persisted cumulative pause ceiling.

**Decided 2026-10-25, user call: option A, rejected.** Keep the issue 453 design with no cumulative pause cap for standard-delivery. This is a recorded decision under `plugin/governance/remediation-doctrine.md` "## Recorded Decisions and Repeat Findings" (ADR-0038 decision 4). It is not a recorded residual, because no defect is left unfixed.

**Reasons (the threat model).**

- Every new pause needs a user reply. `takeNextReply` returns the stored reply for an answered round, or the one pending reply once. A new round with no reply pauses again through `pauseDelivery`. No agent can resume a run. Only the user's reply does. So a planner that keeps asking cannot loop on its own: each round waits for the user.
- Replies are finite. `canCarry` in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/result.js` limits `resume_args` to half of the 8000 character notice budget. After a finite number of replies the run ends blocked with stop reason `reply_size`. That byte budget is the final bound.
- A tail-ask replan that does not count against `maxReplans()` always follows a user reply. A replan with no reply (`needs_replan`, a branch switch, a taken review escalation) still counts against `maxReplans()`.
- A round-count cap would reverse issue 453. Whether a run pauses or ends would again depend on a count, not on whether a question is open. It would also add surface and name no class it eliminates, so it fails "### Closed-by-Construction Acceptance Test" (doctrine "## Surface-Growth Escalation").

**Scope.** A finding is a REPEAT of this decision when it asks standard-delivery for a cap on the number of pauses, rounds or user-answered replans, or flags that no such cap exists. A finding is NEW, and gets First-Finding Triage, when it shows any of these:

- a path where a pause or a replan proceeds with no new user reply;
- a resume path that bypasses `canCarry`;
- a replan with no user reply that does not count against `maxReplans()`.

**The key.** This record is matched by the scope above, not by the finding id, since finding ids change from run to run.

The rest of ADR-0037 is unchanged.

Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/453, `docs/adr/0038-root-cause-at-the-first-finding.md`.

## Amendment 2026-10-25: a review answer needs no fingerprint of its question (local review finding abe9ef8c)

**The finding.** Local review finding abe9ef8c, severity high, titled "Local-review answer binding ignores changed finding content", said the local reviewer matches `user_answer` to an open finding only by `finding_id` or `title` (`plugin/agents/local-reviewer.md`, The Loop step 1). If the body, recommendation or severity of a finding changed while its id or title stayed the same, an old reply could settle a version of the finding the user never saw. It recommended a persisted fingerprint of every field the question showed, checked on resume, with the answer dropped on any mismatch.

**Decided 2026-10-25, user call: option A, rejected.** Keep the binding by `finding_id` or `title`, with no fingerprint of the question. This is a recorded decision under `plugin/governance/remediation-doctrine.md` "## Recorded Decisions and Repeat Findings" (ADR-0038 decision 4). It is not a recorded residual, because no defect is left unfixed.

**Reasons (the threat model).**

- One writer. Between a review question and its answer, only the loop that asked writes the fix ledger at `.hivemind/review-loop/fix-ledger.yaml`. That loop writes the ledger before it returns the question, and the next write is by the same loop when the answer comes back to it.
- No review pass in between. The answer goes back to the local reviewer. On the script engine the reply reruns the review gate with `user_answer` (`runGates` and `runReviewGate` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`). On the state machine the `question` gate runs `local_review` again with `user_answer`. The Loop step 1 applies the reply before step 2, so no new review pass can change a finding before the answer is applied.
- The id fixes what the question showed. A `finding_id` is the SHA-256 of the finding's file, lines and title (`plugin/skills/adaptation-cycle/SKILL.md`, step 8). The asking exit copied the ledger entry for that id into the question, and no one else writes the ledger, so the body, recommendation, severity and open findings the question showed are the values the ledger holds for that id at step 1.
- A fingerprint guards only a case this threat model excludes. It adds a stored digest, a comparison on resume and a path that asks again, and it removes nothing (doctrine "## Surface-Growth Escalation"). It names no class it eliminates inside the threat model, so it fails "### Closed-by-Construction Acceptance Test".

**Scope.** A finding is a REPEAT of this decision when it asks the local reviewer to bind `user_answer` to a fingerprint, digest or full copy of the fields the question showed, or flags that a changed body, recommendation, severity or open findings list behind the same `finding_id` or `title` could take an old reply. A finding is NEW, and gets First-Finding Triage, when it shows any of these:

- a second writer of the fix ledger between a question and its answer, for example a concurrent review loop in the same checkout;
- a review pass that runs before the answer is applied.

A finding that asks to pass the fix ledger identity or path back to the reviewer on resume, such as `resume_from_ledger` or the asking review's `ledger_path`, is also a REPEAT of this decision.
