**The key.** This record is matched by the scope above, not by the finding id, since finding ids change from run to run.

**Scope extended 2026-10-25, user call: local review finding 2379110a.** Finding 2379110a, "P1: Local-review resume omits the ledger identity" (`plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`, the review rerun with `user_answer`), asked that the rerun pass the asking review's `ledger_path` back as `resume_from_ledger`. It is a repeat of this record, and the user kept the decision. Reasons:

- The fix ledger with an answer is fixed at `.hivemind/review-loop/fix-ledger.yaml`. The caller does not choose it.
- Step 1 continues from that ledger and never starts a new one. A missing ledger, a wrong last exit, or a named finding that is not open returns blocked.
- A different ledger at that path needs a second writer. That is the reopen condition of this record, and it is unchanged.
- Accepting a caller-supplied path would cut against Trust-Boundary Discipline in `plugin/governance/security-policy.md`.

The rest of ADR-0037 is unchanged.

Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/453, `docs/adr/0038-root-cause-at-the-first-finding.md`.

## Amendment 2026-10-25: user-facing text comes from bounded fields (issues 441, 445 and 452)

This amendment covers https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/441, https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/445 and https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/452 as one root-cause fix.

- **Root cause.** The engine built every user-facing message from pasted agent free text, because no agent result carried a short field written for the user (https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/445#issuecomment-6401203012). Where the engine bounded that text, it cut it mid-thought or quoted more of it, so the 2026-10-24 amendment on short run messages made review questions longer, not shorter. The three issues are one class: a wall of text (445), a local review question the user could not decide from (441), and approval questions with no details, or asked about a clear, reversible remediation plan (452). Inherited: the step schemas have carried no field for the user since slice 2 began.
- **Owning layer: the step result contracts.** What a message shows is owned by the step schemas, not by the code that builds messages. Each agent result that reaches a message carries a bounded field, with `maxLength` in its step schema, that the agent writes for the user. The engine shows only those fields and text built in code. Removed instead of hardened: the plan summary line, the pointer to per-agent results, and the Codex title and body in questions.
- **Eliminated class.** No agent free text reaches a message unbounded. Every agent text a message shows is a `maxLength` field the agent writes for the user. Row SD-58 holds the rule, and its test checks that every message stays within a fixed bound when every unbounded agent field (`plan.summary`, a worker summary, the validation failures) holds 5000 characters. Text: row SD-58 in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/contract.js`; test: `tests/workflow-scripts/standard-delivery.test.mjs`. Amended 2026-10-28 (local review finding 7703cd64): row SD-58 had let a value that "is cut by code" stand, so the class stayed open. That carve-out is gone. Every agent value a message shows is bounded in its step schema and shown whole, or no message shows it (see Amendment 2026-10-28: one message shape (issue 460)).
- **The plan digest (issues 445 and 452).** `plugin/references/plan-schema.json` gains a required `digest` of two plain sentences, `problem` (what the plan addresses: the request's ask, or the root cause a review found) and `change` (what the plan changes), each with `maxLength` 200 and the external content label. A plan acceptance question has a short problem built in code, with no plan summary and no pointer to per-agent results. It pauses with a detail block after the questions block: `digest.problem`, `digest.change`, and one touches line with the step count, the distinct file count, the branch, and up to 5 file paths, then the count of the rest, each value encoded as external content. Text: `plugin/references/plan-schema.json`, `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`, `plugin/agents/cerebrate.md`, row SD-53.
- **Bounds on the other agent fields.** Planner question briefs gain bounds in `plugin/references/decision-brief-schema.json`: `problem` 300, each option summary 160, the recommendation's `why` 200, and `criticality.blast_radius` and `criticality.if_ignored` 160 each, each described as written for the user. A plan step id gets `maxLength` 64 and an external effect summary 300. The plan, worker, bump, fix gate edit, validation and analysis blocker fields get `maxLength` 400. Every brief built in code passes the brief schema with worst-case inputs, so the diminishing-returns, escalation and root-cluster problems embed no reviewer free text, only a short finding id and counts. Text: the two schema files, `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`, `plugin/workflow-src/analysis-only/main.js`, row SD-20.
- **The remediation gate.** The plan gains a required `remediation_gate` that carries the whole Promotion Gate as `promotion_gate`, with the same three booleans as a decision brief's `gate` (`irreversible`, `architectural` and `safety`), plus two booleans of its own, `grows_surface` and `repeats_surface`. A `promotion_gate` key is true only for a plan that remediates review findings and is irreversible, architectural or safety-relevant, as the matching clause of `plugin/governance/decision-autonomy.md` (Promotion Gate) says. `grows_surface` is true only for such a plan that adds more surface than it removes (`plugin/governance/remediation-doctrine.md`, Surface-Growth Escalation), and `repeats_surface` only for one that follows a fix on the same surface that did not close its class. Each is true only while no reply in this run has accepted that condition. Every flag is false for any other plan. The planner sets them by judgment, as Remediation Zoom-Out Mode in `plugin/agents/cerebrate.md` says. Root cause of local review finding be73abc8: the first form of this gate was a narrowed copy of the Promotion Gate. It carried only clause 2 as `architectural`, and the engine hard-coded `irreversible` and `safety` to false in the remediation plan brief, so a remediation plan that could not be undone, or that touched a safety rail, ran without asking. Eliminated class: no Promotion Gate clause can be left out of remediation acceptance, because the plan copies the gate whole, the engine copies `promotion_gate` into the brief's `gate` unchanged, and a parity test ties `promotion_gate` to the `gate` of `plugin/references/decision-brief-schema.json`. Text: `plugin/references/plan-schema.json`; test: `tests/workflow-scripts/plan-schema.test.mjs`.
- **The remediation acceptance rule (issue 452).** Accepting a remediation plan asks the user only when a `remediation_gate` flag is set, on both engines: any `promotion_gate` key, so an irreversible, architectural or safety-relevant remediation plan asks, or `grows_surface` or `repeats_surface`. Any other remediation plan is decided for the user: journaled on the state machine, and on the script engine resolved by the `remediation_plan_acceptance` row, now auto at the high and balanced levels and surface at low, and listed in the final message and the PR body. When it asks, its brief's `gate` is the plan's `promotion_gate`, copied unchanged, and its problem names each flag that holds. The rule has one home, `plugin/governance/decision-autonomy.md` (Promotion Gate). The Tier B root-cluster bullet and Recurrence Interplay in that file, and `plugin/agents/overlord.md`, refer to it and do not restate it. Text: `plugin/governance/decision-autonomy.md` (Promotion Gate), `plugin/references/decision-autonomy-table.json`, row SD-40.
- **The review question fields (issue 441).** The local review result carries short fields the reviewer writes for the user. `user-input-required` carries `question`, the ask in one plain sentence (`maxLength` 300). `high-severity-rejection` carries `rationale_text`, why the reviewer rejects the finding in its own words (`maxLength` 300). Both, and `diminishing-returns`, carry `recommendation_text` (`maxLength` 200, may be empty). Each open finding is `finding_id`, `severity`, `summary` (what is wrong, one plain sentence, `maxLength` 200) and `recommendation` (one plain sentence, `maxLength` 200, may be empty). `title` and `body` are gone, so no Codex body reaches a question. The question brief is short code text that names the finding by the first 8 code points of its `finding_id`, and its recommendation names the reviewer's recommendation when the review gives one. The detail block shows the question or the rejection reason, the reviewer's recommendation, and up to 5 open findings, then one line counting the rest. The rerun review's `user_answer.question` is the text the user saw: the questions block and the detail block. The local reviewer binds a reply by `finding_id` or by the short id the question shows. Text: `REVIEW_SCHEMA` and the review question brief in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`, `plugin/agents/local-reviewer.md` (the Output Contract and The Loop step 1), rows SD-33, SD-37 and SD-58. Amended 2026-10-28 (issue 460): `question`, `rationale_text` and `open_findings` are gone, and so is the detail block. Both exits now carry `finding_id` and `user_question`, the reviewer's own question with its own options and recommendation (see Amendment 2026-10-28: one message shape (issue 460)).
- **The relay rule has one home.** The handoff for this fix found two rules: `plugin/agents/overlord.md` (`## Engine`) said to relay the engine message whole, and the overlord had been rewriting it by hand because the message was a wall of text. Decided: with the message bounded where it is built, the overlord presents `message` as its whole reply, as `## Engine` says, and that section is the rule's one home. The relay contract header of `plugin/workflow-src/lib/result.js` keeps only a pointer to it, with no code change. Rejected: an overlord that rewrites each message in plain words from the run's results. It adds a second author of user text and model work per message, and its labels or reply tokens can drift from what the run reads back. Text: `plugin/agents/overlord.md` (`## Engine`); test: `tests/workflow-scripts/overlord-contract.test.mjs`. Amended 2026-10-28 (issue 460): the rule stays after the logs showed the bounded message was still a wall of text. The fix stays in the engine, the one author of user text, and the bare "A" that clashed with the engine's own option A on #459 is one more reason to reject an overlord rewrite (see Amendment 2026-10-28: one message shape (issue 460)).
- **The bound lives in the schema.** Each bound is `maxLength` in the step schema, so the agent writes the short field at the source. `tests/workflow-scripts/plan-schema.test.mjs` adds `maxLength` to its keyword subset. Rejected: a bound stated only in each field description, with the script cutting an overrun at a sentence or word boundary. It keeps a cap on pasted text, the pattern the root-cause comment names as the reason the earlier fixes failed.
- **The built script stays within its budget.** The fields and checks this amendment adds took the rebuilt `standard-delivery.js` to 191,712 characters, over the 190,000 budget of decision 18. The build now writes each data constant as JSON with one key or element per line and no indent, with the data unchanged. The committed `plugin/workflows/standard-delivery.js` is 188,926 characters, measured as a JavaScript string length with node, within the 190,000 budget of decision 18. Text: `tools/build_workflows.mjs`; test: `tests/workflow-scripts/build.test.mjs`.
- **Surface account.** Removed: the plan summary line and the per-agent results pointer in plan acceptance questions, the Codex `title` and `body` in review questions, the copy of the relay rule in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/result.js`, and the remediation acceptance that always asked. Added: the plan `digest` and `remediation_gate`, which reuses the decision brief's `gate` shape as `promotion_gate` and adds no new gate concept, the reviewer's `question`, `rationale_text`, `recommendation_text` and per-finding `summary` and `recommendation`, `maxLength` bounds on existing fields, and row SD-58.

Bounded residual:

- **The bound rests on two schema checks.** `maxLength` is outside the strict structured-output subset, so it rests on the Workflow agent schema check, which has the agent retry, and on the script check, which rejects what still breaks it. A result that breaks a bound blocks the step like any schema breach. Bounded impact: either way no agent text reaches a message unbounded, and a blocked step names its next action. Why the obvious fix was rejected: cutting an overrun in the script would bring back the cap on pasted text that this amendment removes.

It updates these places in place, each with a dated note:

- Decision 17: "A remediation plan always asks, as the table says."
- Decision 18: "writes each data constant as indented JSON".
- Amendment 2026-10-23 on question keys, blocked stops and the question template: the residual "plan acceptance shows the summary, not the plan", and the Text line of "The relay may follow the user's output style".
- Amendment 2026-10-24 on short run messages: the bullet on the plan acceptance line and the bullet on the local review open findings.

`docs/adr/0038-root-cause-at-the-first-finding.md` gains a matching note on the consequence "On the script engine the zoom-out is the planner's judgment". The rest of ADR-0037 is unchanged.

Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/441, https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/445, https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/445#issuecomment-6401203012, https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/452, `docs/adr/0038-root-cause-at-the-first-finding.md`.

## Amendment 2026-10-26: the harness schema check on agent() output (issue 459)

This amendment holds the headless harness record for https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/459. It records which JSON Schema keywords the Workflow harness enforces on `agent()` output. It changes no decision. The engine checks that duplicate this record are a later step of issue 459. Amended 2026-10-26 (issue 459): that later step kept every engine check of agent output, and the scripts shrank by cutting data text and code whitespace instead (see Amendment 2026-10-26: the delivery engine shrinks and splits into modules (issue 459)).

**Setup.** Claude Code 2.1.311. Each probe was a throwaway workflow with one `agent()` call, launched by one headless `claude -p --permission-mode default --allowedTools Workflow --output-format json` run, from a scratch folder outside the repository, as the `maxLength` check of https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/pull/458 did (https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/pull/458#issuecomment-6403161172). The agent was the default workflow subagent on the session model, `claude-opus-5-5`. Its prompt named a breaching answer for its first StructuredOutput call, and a valid answer to send only if the first was rejected. The script caught any rejection of `agent()` and returned `{kind, value}` or `{kind: 'threw', message}`. Every result below is read from the agent transcript (each StructuredOutput call and its tool result), the workflow journal, and the result in the completion notice, not from a model summary. No probe file was written inside the repository.

"Accepted" means `agent()` took the schema and the agent ran. "Rejected and retried" means the harness answered the breaching call with a schema error, the agent called again, and only the valid value reached the script.

| Probe | Schema under test | Breaching answer | Accepted | Rejected and retried | Result seen |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| type-object | `inner: {type: object}` | `{"inner": 5}` | Yes | Yes | `/inner: must be object`; the script got `{"inner": {"a": "x"}}` |
| type-array | `list: {type: array}` | `{"list": 5}` | Yes | Yes | `/list: must be array`; the script got `{"list": ["x"]}` |
| type-string | `text: {type: string}` | `{"text": 42}`, then `true` and `null` in two reruns | Yes | Not observed | Each value reached the tool as a string (`"42"`, `"true"`, `"null"`) and was accepted. The transcript does not show whether the model or the harness made it a string. The script got a string each time |
| type-boolean | `flag: {type: boolean}` | `{"flag": 1}` | Yes | Yes | `/flag: must be boolean`; the script got `{"flag": true}` |
| type-integer | `count: {type: integer}` | `{"count": 2.5}` | Yes | Yes | `/count: must be integer`; the script got `{"count": 2}` |
| enum | `kind: {enum: [red, blue]}` | `{"kind": "green"}` | Yes | Yes | `/kind: must be equal to one of the allowed values: ["red","blue"]`; the script got `red` |
| required | `required: [a, b]` | `{"a": "x"}` | Yes | Yes | `root: must have required property 'b'`; the script got both keys |
| properties | nested `inner.a: {type: integer}` | `{"inner": {"a": "seven"}}` | Yes | Yes | `/inner/a: must be integer`; the script got `{"inner": {"a": 7}}` |
| additionalProperties false | root `additionalProperties: false` | `{"a": "x", "extra": "y"}` | Yes | Yes | `root: must NOT have additional properties ('extra' is not allowed)`; the script got `{"a": "x"}` |
| items | `items: {type: integer}` | `{"list": [1, "two"]}` | Yes | Yes | `/list/1: must be integer`; the script got `[1, 2]` |
| minItems | `minItems: 2` | `{"list": ["x"]}` | Yes | Yes | `/list: must NOT have fewer than 2 items (got 1)`; the script got two items |
| minLength | `minLength: 5` | `{"text": "ab"}` | Yes | Yes | `/text: must NOT have fewer than 5 characters (got 2)`; the script got `abcdef` |
| maxLength, ASCII | `maxLength: 10` | 26 ASCII letters | Yes | Yes | `/text: must NOT have more than 10 characters (got 26)`; the script got `abcde` |
| maxLength, outside ASCII | `maxLength: 5` | 7 × `é` (7 code points, 7 UTF-16 units) | Yes | Yes | `/text: must NOT have more than 5 characters (got 7)`; the script got 3 × `é` |
| maxLength, counting | `maxLength: 5` | 4 × U+1F600 (4 code points, 8 UTF-16 units) | Yes | Not rejected | Accepted on the first call, and the script got the 4 emoji. The harness counts code points, not UTF-16 units |
| pattern | `pattern: ^[a-z]+$` | `{"code": "ABC-123"}` | Yes | Yes | `/code: must match pattern "^[a-z]+$"`; the script got `abc` |
| minimum | `type: integer, minimum: 0` | `{"count": -3}` | Yes | Yes | `/count: must be >= 0`; the script got `3` |
| anyOf at the top level | root `anyOf: [{required: [a]}, {required: [b]}]` | `{"kind": "x"}` | No | No call made | `agent()` did not throw. The first model request failed with `API Error: 400 tools.9.custom.input_schema: input_schema does not support oneOf, allOf, or anyOf at the top level`, the journal marked the agent `failed`, and `agent()` returned `null` |
| retry limit, agent keeps breaching | `maxLength: 10` | 26 ASCII letters on every call | Yes | Rejected 5 times, no valid value | After 5 rejected calls `agent()` rejected with `agent({schema}): StructuredOutput retry cap (5) exceeded — 5 failed calls with no valid output — last StructuredOutput error: Output does not match required schema: /text: must NOT have more than 10 characters (got 26)`. The journal marked the agent `failed`. The bad value never reached the script |
| retry limit, agent stops calling | `maxLength: 10` | 26 ASCII letters, then the agent ends its turn | Yes | Rejected twice, no valid value | After the first rejection the agent ended its turn with text. The harness sent one nudge, `[structured-output-enforce] You MUST call the StructuredOutput tool to complete this request. Call this tool now.`, the agent breached once more and ended again, and `agent()` rejected with `agent({schema}): subagent completed without calling StructuredOutput (after in-conversation nudge)` |

Notes on the record:

- **What agent() gives the script past the retry limit: a rejected call.** It is not the bad value and not an ended run. In both retry-limit probes the script's catch got the error, and the run went on to return the script's value with status `completed`. A rejection left uncaught by the script was not probed.
- **Type breaches written as unquoted words.** The first type-object, type-array and type-boolean probes asked for a string in place of the object, array or boolean. The agent sent the word without quotes, such as `{"list": not-an-array}`. The harness rejected it as `InputValidationError: StructuredOutput was called with input that could not be parsed as JSON`, and the agent retried with the valid value. Those probes were rerun with a number as the breach, and the reruns are the rows above.
- **A retry-limit probe where the agent gave in.** One more retry-limit probe asked for up to 25 identical calls. The agent sent the valid value after the first rejection, so it did not breach past the limit and is not a row above.

Not probed:

- an agent with an `agentType`, such as `hivemind:drone` or `hivemind:cerebrate`. Every probe used the default workflow subagent.
- `anyOf` below the top level of a schema. `toAgentSchema` in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/schema.js` removes every `anyOf` before a schema reaches `agent()`.
- a breach of `type: string` that reaches the schema check as a non-string value.

The rest of ADR-0037 is unchanged.

Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/459, https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/pull/458#issuecomment-6403161172.

## Amendment 2026-10-26: the delivery engine shrinks and splits into modules (issue 459)

This amendment covers https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/459. At 6b81298 the built `plugin/workflows/standard-delivery.js` was 188,937 characters, 1,063 under the 190,000 budget of decision 18, so the next fix would cross it. Issue 459 shrinks both built scripts, splits the delivery engine into modules, and adds two build guards that keep agent prose out of the scripts (`docs/engineering-principles.md` P24).

**Measured lengths.** Each length is the JavaScript string length of the committed built script, measured with node.

| Stage | `standard-delivery.js` | `analysis-only.js` |
|---|---|---|
| At 6b81298, before issue 459 | 188,937 | 94,244 |
| After the module split and the prompt cuts | 174,868 | 87,100 |
| After the schema text projection | 166,136 | 78,368 |
| After code compaction (final) | 148,222 | 69,999 |

The final `standard-delivery.js` holds 41,480 characters of string and template literals, against a prose budget of 42,000. The final `analysis-only.js` holds 18,481, against a prose budget of 19,000. Both counts are `countProseCharacters` in `tools/build_workflows.mjs`, quotes, backticks and escapes included.

- **The module split.** `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js` is split into the modules `gates.js`, `plan.js`, `pr.js`, `record.js`, `relay.js`, `review.js` and `waves.js` in the same directory. The build bundles the modules after the shared libraries and before `main.js`, in name order. A module holds only function declarations at top level, or the build fails naming the file, so every top-level const stays in `main.js` and the module order cannot change what runs. No workflow source module is over 600 lines. The longest are `standard-delivery/plan.js` (550), `lib/result.js` (530) and `standard-delivery/gates.js` (519). The prompts were cut to the task and the run's data, per P24. Text: `tools/build_workflows.mjs` (`listModulePaths`); test: `tests/workflow-scripts/build.test.mjs`.
- **The schema text projection (P24).** The bundled `BRIEF_SCHEMA` and `PLAN_SCHEMA` carry no field text. `projectSchema` keeps `$schema` and every validation keyword, since `validateSchema` reads them, and drops every title and description, except that a description starting with `REPOSITORY_PATH_MARK` becomes exactly that mark, since `findRepositoryPathProblem` walks the plan schema for it. The field text stays in `plugin/references/plan-schema.json` and `plugin/references/decision-brief-schema.json`, and `plugin/agents/cerebrate.md` makes the planner read both files with Read before it returns a plan or a question. The schema a script gives the planner carries each field's type and limits, not its meaning. `AUTONOMY_TABLE` keeps only what `projectAutonomyTable` returns, a cut already counted in the 174,868 stage. Text: `tools/build_workflows.mjs` (`projectSchema`, `readDataConstant`), `plugin/agents/cerebrate.md`; tests: `tests/workflow-scripts/build.test.mjs`, `tests/workflow-scripts/cerebrate-plan.test.mjs`.
- **The compaction rule.** The build reads every source only through the vendored acorn parser (`tools/vendor/acorn/`): its syntax tree and the tokens and comments it reports while parsing. A source acorn cannot parse fails the build, naming the file. After comments are stripped, `compactCode` compacts the code of each library, each module and `main.js` by writing acorn's tokens of the stripped source. It writes a newline wherever the stripped source has one between two tokens, so automatic semicolon insertion sees the same line breaks, and one space only where the two tokens written together would read as other tokens: two word characters, a regex literal before a word character, a number before a dot, or a join that would read as `++`, `--`, a comment, or the start of an HTML-like comment (`joinsIntoOtherTokens`). Nothing else goes between tokens. `meta.js`, the banner, the data constants and the bytes of every string, template and regex literal are never compacted. Text: `tools/build_workflows.mjs` (`compactCode`).
- **The equivalence checks.** Two AST equality checks run on each library, each module and `main.js`, and each fails the build, naming the file, in both modes. `assertStrippingPreserves` fails unless the original source and the stripped source parse to equal syntax trees, and `assertCompactionPreserves` fails unless the stripped source and the compacted code do. Two trees are equal when every field other than the position fields `start`, `end`, `loc` and `range` is equal, literal raw text included. Neither check uses a rule of the stripper or the compactor, so any change either makes to the code, such as a rewritten regex or two tokens merged into one, fails the build. Text: `tools/build_workflows.mjs` (`findTreeDifference`); test: `tests/workflow-scripts/build.test.mjs`.
- **The prose budget.** The characters of the string and template literal tokens in each built script, live or draft, are at most that workflow's entry in `PROSE_BUDGETS`, and a workflow id with no entry fails the build, naming it. Each budget is the measured count plus a margin of at most 1,000. Raising a budget is a visible, reviewed change to `tools/build_workflows.mjs`.
- **The duplication check.** The comparison set is every regular file `plugin/agents/*.md` and `plugin/skills/<name>/SKILL.md`, found by listing those two directories, never from names in the literals (`listPromptFiles`). A sentence of at least five words of a string literal or a template piece, read as acorn decodes it, that equals a sentence of any file in the set, once whitespace is normalized, fails the build, naming the script, the file and the sentence (`findCopiedSentences`). The check is a verbatim-copy tripwire. A rule pasted whole into one string literal or one template piece fails the build instead of resting on review, whether or not the prompt names that file (P24, P3). A sentence split across literals joined by `+`, across a template substitution or across a join, or reworded, passes it and rests on review, as the decision on local review finding c5199443 below records. Text: `tools/build_workflows.mjs`; test: `tests/workflow-scripts/build.test.mjs`.
- **Every engine check of agent output stays.** The script still checks each agent result against its full step schema with `validateSchema`, for the reasons the header of `plugin/workflow-src/lib/schema.js` gives. The Messages API rejects a tool `input_schema` with `anyOf` at its top level, so each agent gets the flat `toAgentSchema` copy and only the script checks the full schema. In the harness record (Amendment 2026-10-26: the harness schema check on agent() output (issue 459)), a failed `agent()` call returned `null` to the script, no probe used an `agentType`, and no probe showed a `type: string` breach rejected. Planner decision Q1 chose this route, option A: keep the checks, and shrink the scripts by cutting schema text and code whitespace. Option B, dropping the engine checks that the harness record duplicates, was rejected.
- **The budget is unchanged.** `MAX_BUILT_SCRIPT_LENGTH` stays 190,000, as decision 18 records. The final `standard-delivery.js` is 41,778 characters under it.
- **Local review remediation: the build reads JavaScript with a parser (local review findings ef735ee4 and 2926aeb3).** Root cause of ef735ee4: the build read JavaScript with a hand-written lexer, `tokenizeSource`, which chose between a regex and a division from the previous token alone. That cannot be decided after a closing parenthesis, a closing brace or some keywords. Comment stripping (on main since PR8) and this issue's compaction trusted that lexer, and the equivalence check re-read the code through the same lexer with all whitespace removed. So a rewritten regex (`if (e) /a +b/` written as `/a+b/`) or two tokens merged into one (`a + +b` written as `a++b`) passed the build with no error. The defect was latent, since no current source triggers it, but a silent change to a built regex could alter an identifier gate or an injection gate. Root cause of 2926aeb3, a separate one: the duplication check took the files it compared against from the `hivemind:<name>` strings inside the same prose it checked, so a copied sentence in a prompt that named no file was never checked. Planner decision Q1 of the remediation plan, which the user made, chose option A: vendor acorn 8.19.0, unmodified, at `tools/vendor/acorn/`, with its MIT `LICENSE`, a provenance `README.md` that records the registry tarball integrity and the `acorn.mjs` sha256, a `.gitattributes` rule that checks the vendored files out byte for byte, and a test that pins the sha256. Rejected: a hand lexer that refuses the cases it cannot decide, since it would still be a second, partial reading of the language, kept by hand, and each new unclear case would be one more refusal to add, the same shape as the finding. Rejected: acorn as an npm dev dependency, since validation would then need an install step, while today it needs only Node 18, and the root `package.json` is website tooling that validation never installs. Classes ruled out: no build step or check reads JavaScript structure except through a parser that follows the spec, and any change stripping or compaction makes to the code fails the build instead of passing silently, because both AST equality checks compare syntax trees and use no rule of the step they check. The duplication check takes its comparison set from the directory listings, never from the prose it checks. Nothing is deferred, and no residual is recorded, since both roots are closed on this engine. The rebuilt scripts are the same length as before this remediation, so the measured lengths above hold. Text: `tools/build_workflows.mjs`, `tools/vendor/acorn/README.md`, `.gitattributes`; test: `tests/workflow-scripts/build.test.mjs`.
- **Local review decision: the duplication check is a verbatim-copy tripwire (local review finding c5199443).** The finding, severity medium, titled "Copied-rule detection misses prose assembled from multiple literals", said `findCopiedSentences` reads each string literal and each template piece on its own, so a copied sentence split across concatenated literals or across a template substitution passes, and its hand-written escape decoding missed hex and code-point escapes. It recommended reading statically resolvable string expressions as whole runtime strings, or failing closed on constructions the check cannot resolve. Decided 2026-10-26, user call: option A. Keep the check as a verbatim-copy tripwire, record that limit, and read string text from the parser's decoded values. This is a recorded decision under `plugin/governance/remediation-doctrine.md` "## Recorded Decisions and Repeat Findings" (ADR-0038 decision 4), and it records the threat model the check had none of.
  - **Threat model.** The check catches an author who pastes an agent or skill rule whole into a prompt, a slip in a change that review also reads. It does not guard against an author who splits, templates or rewords a rule, by intent or by accident. Matching the strings a script assembles needs a static evaluator of string expressions or a capture of the built prompts. Either grows the surface (doctrine "## Surface-Growth Escalation"), and a reworded copy stays invisible to any exact-match check. The prose budget still bounds all prompt text, and review owns the split or reworded copy.
  - **Fixed.** The check reads each literal's text from acorn's decoded token values (`readProseValues`), not from a hand-written decoder, so every escape form, hex, code point, `\u` and line continuation included, is read as the characters it stands for. Class eliminated: a whole sentence in one literal that the check reads differently from the runtime string because of how it is escaped. Removed: `decodeEscape` and `readLiteralContent`. Test: `tests/workflow-scripts/build.test.mjs` (an escaped copy is found, and a split copy passes).
  - **Scope.** A finding is a REPEAT of this decision when it flags that a sentence split across literals joined by `+`, across template substitutions, across a join or other string assembly, or reworded, passes the duplication check, or asks the check to evaluate assembled strings, fail closed on string expressions, or check the built prompts. A finding is NEW, and gets First-Finding Triage, when it shows a sentence pasted whole into one string literal or one template piece that the check misses.
  - **The key.** This record is matched by the scope above, not by the finding id, since finding ids change from run to run.
- **Local review decision: the build reads only regular files at their own path (local review finding 0de223ac).** The finding, severity high, titled "Workflow source reads can escape the repository through symlinks", said `readSource` follows symlinks for every build input: `meta.js`, `main.js`, the libraries, the three reference JSON files and the agent and skill files the duplication check reads. So a committed symlink could copy a file from outside the repository into `plugin/workflows/*.js`, and `listModulePaths` silently dropped a symlinked module. Root cause: the build read its inputs by path and never established that the bytes were the repository file at that path, and its listings dropped entries that the output side refuses. `readSource` following symlinks is inherited from main; the silent drop came with the module split. Decided 2026-10-26, user call: option A. Refuse such inputs in one place: the build's single file reader rejects any path that resolves elsewhere, and the listings fail on non-regular entries instead of skipping them.
  - **Threat model.** A committed symlink among the build inputs, at the leaf or at any parent directory, is inside the threat model. The build refuses it and never reads through it, the same rule the output side already had. ADR-0031's follow-symlinks policy governs the discovery sites of `tools/policy_check.sh` only, and the build is not one of them. A program running as the same user on the machine, racing the build or making a hard link, is outside the threat model, per the decision on local review finding 916ad92b below.
  - **Fixed.** `readSource` is the build's only file read. It fails the build, naming the path, unless the entry is a regular file that resolves to its own path under the real path of the root (`assertRepositoryPath`). `readDirectory` is the only listing of an input directory, and it fails the same way when the directory resolves elsewhere or holds a symlink or an entry that is neither a file nor a directory. A `.js` entry of a workflow directory, an `.md` entry of `plugin/agents/` and a `SKILL.md` that are not regular files fail the build instead of dropping out. Both modes fail before they list, read or write anything under `plugin/workflows/` unless it resolves to its own path. Class eliminated: no committed build input, nothing git checks out, makes the build read from anywhere but the regular file at its own repository path, and no input listing drops an entry for its kind. The class covers the committed tree only, not a hard link or a race made on the machine (decision on local review finding 916ad92b below). Test: `tests/workflow-scripts/build.test.mjs`, one case per input class and a symlinked `plugin/workflows/` in both modes.
- **Local review decision: same-user programs and hard links are outside the build's threat model (local review finding 916ad92b).** The finding, severity high, titled "Filesystem checks do not bind validation to the object subsequently accessed", said `readSource`, `readDirectory` and the target check in `writeWorkflows` each check a path and then read, list or write by path again. So a program that renames or swaps a file or a parent directory between the check and the access can make the build follow a different file, and a hard link passes both the regular-file check and the real-path check while naming a file also linked outside the repository. It recommended descriptor-based no-follow opens checked with `fstat`, refusing files with more than one link, and race tests. Root cause: the 0de223ac record above stated its eliminated class as every build input and recorded no threat model for actors on the machine, so it claimed more than a path check gives. The read-side check-then-read came with the 0de223ac fix; the write-side check-then-write and the hard-link reads and writes are inherited from main. Decided 2026-10-27, user call: option A. Reject the hardening, record the threat model, and narrow the 0de223ac claim to the committed tree. This is a recorded decision under `plugin/governance/remediation-doctrine.md` "## Recorded Decisions and Repeat Findings" (ADR-0038 decision 4).
  - **Threat model.** The build runs as the developer, or in CI, on a checkout git made. Inside the model: the committed tree, a committed symlink at a leaf or at a parent directory included, which the 0de223ac fix refuses. Outside the model: a program running as the same user on the same machine, whether it races the build by renaming or swapping a file or directory between a check and the later access, or makes a hard link into the repository. Git never checks out a hard link and never races the build, so neither comes from a commit or a pull request. Such a program can already read or overwrite the outside file itself, without the build, so the build is no boundary against it. `plugin/governance/security-policy.md` "Trust-Boundary Discipline" boundary 3 records the same rule for same-user brood children.
  - **Not fixed.** Descriptor-based access, link-count checks and race tests add code paths, checks and tests, remove nothing, and protect nothing against an actor that can already touch the file directly (`plugin/governance/remediation-doctrine.md` "## Surface-Growth Escalation"). Node has no portable no-follow open for parent directories and no descriptor-based directory listing, so the parent-swap race could not be closed portably anyway. Bounded impact: such a program can at worst make the build copy one of the user's own files into a built script, which `--check` and review show as a diff, or overwrite one of the user's own files, which it could already do itself.
  - **Scope.** A finding is a REPEAT of this decision when it flags a race between a build check and the later read, listing or write (a rename, a symlink or a directory swap by another program), or a hard link among the build's inputs or outputs, or asks the build for descriptor-based opens, `fstat` or link-count checks, or race tests. A finding is NEW, and gets First-Finding Triage, when it shows something git checks out, a committed file, symlink or directory, that makes the build read or write outside the repository.
  - **The key.** This record is matched by the scope above, not by the finding id, since finding ids change from run to run.
- **Surface account.** Added: the seven delivery modules, `projectSchema`, `compactCode` with `assertCompactionPreserves`, `PROSE_BUDGETS`, `findCopiedSentences`, and the sentence in `plugin/agents/cerebrate.md` that makes the planner read the two schema files. The local review remediation added the vendored acorn parser at `tools/vendor/acorn/` (one third-party file with its `LICENSE` and a provenance `README.md`), its `.gitattributes` rule and sha256 pin test, and the two AST equality checks, `assertStrippingPreserves` and `assertCompactionPreserves`. It removed the hand lexer (`tokenizeSource` and its helpers, the keyword and hazard lists, the whitespace-free comparison and the top-level scanner) and the comparison file set taken from names in the prose. So the surface grows and a third-party dependency is added, both accepted with the remediation plan's decision Q1. The fix for local review finding 0de223ac added `assertRepositoryPath` and `readDirectory`, and removed the listing filters that dropped non-regular entries. It grows the surface by two checks and their failure paths, accepted with the user's decision on that finding. Removed from the built scripts: schema field text, code whitespace, and agent rules restated in prompts. No engine check and no schema field is removed.

It updates these places in place, each with a dated note:

- Decision 18: the 2026-10-25 amendment's "The data is unchanged", for the data constant form.
- Amendment 2026-10-26 on the harness schema check: the sentence "The engine checks that duplicate this record are a later step of issue 459."

The rest of ADR-0037 is unchanged.

Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/459, `docs/engineering-principles.md` (P24).

## Amendment 2026-10-27: the planner reads less (issue 463)

This amendment covers https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/463. On a delivery run, the planner did most of the run's work by reading: whole governance docs it did not use, and whole documents the request named, one file per turn.

**Root cause.** Triaged per ADR-0038, the cause is inherited from main, and two layers own it. Both decide what the planner reads, and both make it read too much.

- **The agent definitions.** `plugin/agents/cerebrate.md` loaded `definitions.md`, `versioning.md` and `remediation-doctrine.md` on every dispatch. The doctrine is about 30,000 characters, and only remediation planning uses it. `cerebrate.md` also said that over-reading is the cheap failure, and it said nothing about batching calls, reading line ranges or never reading a file again. `plugin/agents/drone.md` and `plugin/agents/overlord.md` also loaded the doctrine on every dispatch, though only their review remediation sections use it.
- **The engine.** The plan prompt passed the request word for word, with no slice. So a request that names a handoff or a plan made the planner read that document whole and check again every path it names.

The order of preference for the fix was to remove input first and add instructions second.

- **The Scope step.** Every pass of `standard-delivery` runs one Scope step after the facts step and the scan, and before planning. It runs the planner agent, `hivemind:cerebrate`, in its new `### Scope Mode` section, on `SCOPE_MODEL` `sonnet` at effort `high`, with label `scope-P` and phase `Scope`. The effort is set in the step, because the cerebrate frontmatter sets `xhigh`. The result, under `SCOPE_SCHEMA`, holds, for each section of a repository document that the request or the facts texts name, the document's path and the line the section starts on, with no text and no range, at most 10 sections; and, for each path a section or the request names for the work, whether it exists now and any drift, at most 30 paths. The plan and revise prompts carry the checked scope as a `scope` data block after the request block, and the new `## Scope Block` section of `cerebrate.md` tells the planner to plan from it, to read the whole section that holds each entry's line, taking where the section starts and ends from the file and not from the entry, to check none of its paths again, and to research only what it does not cover. Amended 2026-10-27 by the fix for local review finding 57aa38e7 below: the result first held each slice word for word. Amended 2026-10-27 by the fix for local review findings 61758d1a and f6871d5b below: the result then held the line ranges of each section, and the planner read only those ranges. The scope never reaches a message, the pull request texts or a worker prompt. A scope result that fails `SCOPE_SCHEMA`, or names a path that `findRepositoryPathProblem` rejects, stops the run blocked before planning, as the facts step does. Amended 2026-10-27 (run wf_a706705c-76f): a scope path that starts with the checkout root and a slash is first made relative to that root, which `gather-facts.sh` prints on its `root` line, `none` when the root holds a control character, and a root of `none` converts nothing; a scope entry whose path then fails `isRepositoryPath` is dropped from the scope, as an entry left out, and only a result that fails `SCOPE_SCHEMA` stops the run. A request that names no document gets empty lists, and planning goes on as before. A resume replays `scope-P` from cache. Text: `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/scope.js`, `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`, `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/plan.js`, `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/meta.js`, contract rows SD-59 to SD-64 in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/contract.js`, `plugin/agents/cerebrate.md`; tests: `tests/workflow-scripts/standard-delivery.test.mjs`, `tests/workflow-scripts/cerebrate-plan.test.mjs`.
  - Rejected: a drone on Sonnet. The drone is the implementer, with Bash, Write and Edit, and its definition loads governance docs that scoping does not use. The planner agent is read-only by its tool list and already owns repository research.
  - Rejected: extraction by the engine. A Workflow script cannot read files, so it cannot slice a document or check a path.
  - Rejected: instructions only. Read discipline in `cerebrate.md` alone leaves the planner, on its own model and effort, finding and reading every slice itself. It adds instructions and removes no input.
- **Doctrine loads move to the sections that use them.** `cerebrate.md` loads `remediation-doctrine.md` only in its review remediation mode. `drone.md` loads it only in `## Review Remediation`, and `overlord.md` only in `## Review Remediation Posture`, when routing or applying review remediation. `plugin/agents/local-reviewer.md` and `plugin/agents/github-reviewer.md` keep the doctrine loaded on every dispatch, because it is their core job. Test: `tests/workflow-scripts/agent-loads.test.mjs`.
- **Versioning and definitions load lazily in the planner.** `cerebrate.md` no longer loads `versioning.md` or `definitions.md` on every dispatch. It reads the `## Bump Trigger` and `## Bump Type Determination` sections of `versioning.md` only at the versioning decision, and it reads the named section of `definitions.md` where a rule uses it: `## Transient Failure` when a tool call fails, and `## Brood-Plan` and `## Strain` for a brood plan.
- **Read discipline in `cerebrate.md`.** The sentence that over-reading is the cheap failure is gone. The planner puts every independent Read, Grep and Glob of a research step in one turn, as parallel calls; greps to locate and then reads only the needed line range; never reads a file or range already in context again; greps long append-only documents, such as a changelog, an ADR, a plan or a handoff, and never reads them whole; and reads only the section of a named document that the request is about. Scope must still be complete and exact. No body line of `cerebrate.md` takes the form of a list item that holds only a name, because `tests/policy/safety-planner-no-write.json` would count it as a granted tool.
- **Local review remediation: the scope holds line ranges, not copied text (local review finding 57aa38e7).** Root cause: the scope copied each slice into an agent-written field, `documents[].text`, word for word under a 20,000-character bound, and a Workflow script cannot read files, so it could never check that copy against the file. A copy cut by its bound or by the model gave no sign, and the planner planned from it as if it were whole. The free-text fields `lines`, `text` and `drift` also carried no external-content label. The cause came with this amendment's Scope step. Fixed: the scope returns each slice as its path and line ranges, with no text, and the planner reads those ranges from the file, which is ground truth. Invariant 4 holds by construction. The scope copies no document text, so there is no copy to cut. Every string field of `SCOPE_SCHEMA`, `lines` and `drift` included, starts with `externalLabel()`. Both lists are partial by definition: a document or path left out is read or checked by the planner itself, per the Scope Block rule to research only what the block does not cover, so no step depends on a list being complete. `drift` is the scope agent's own bounded note, at most 400 characters, not a cut copy of a source, the same as other bounded model-written plan fields. Rejected: keep `text`, with each slice returned whole or left out, and label `lines`, `text` and `drift`. The script cannot verify that a slice is whole, so that rule would be an instruction, not a guarantee. Surface account: removed the `text` field, its 20,000-character bound and the word-for-word copy rule of Scope Mode; added the label on the descriptions of `lines` and `drift`, and reworded one sentence of `## Scope Block`. The surface shrinks. Nothing is deferred, and no residual is recorded. Text: `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/scope.js`, contract row SD-61, `plugin/agents/cerebrate.md`; tests: `tests/workflow-scripts/standard-delivery.test.mjs`, `tests/workflow-scripts/cerebrate-plan.test.mjs`.
- **Local review remediation: the scope locates sections and bounds its lists (local review findings 61758d1a and f6871d5b).** Root cause: ranges written by a model bounded what the planner read, and no step could check them. The Scope Block told the planner to read only the line ranges the scope named. The script cannot read files, and the planner was told not to read past the ranges, so a range that was cut, malformed or too narrow silently dropped plan input, against invariant 4. The two lists also had no length bound, so the cost of a run had none either. The 57aa38e7 fix above changed the payload from copied text to ranges but kept the scope as the bound, so this new surface raised the same kind of finding again. That second finding on one surface forced an approach-level zoom-out, per `plugin/governance/remediation-doctrine.md`, instead of hardening the ranges. Fixed: each document entry is `{path, line}`, where `line` is an integer that points at the line a section starts on. It locates the section and does not bound it. The planner reads the whole section that holds that line, and takes where the section starts and ends from the file, not from the entry. `SCOPE_SCHEMA` bounds `documents` to 10 entries and `paths` to 30 by `maxItems`, and `validateSchema` in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/schema.js` gains the `maxItems` keyword for this, in both built scripts. Eliminated class: no scope value states a section's extent, so no range can be cut, malformed or too narrow. A wrong start line points the planner at the wrong section, and the planner reads that section from the file, which is ground truth.
  - Rejected: a completeness flag with more checks. Its fields are asserted by the model, so no step could check them, and it only completes the set the model already knows.
  - Rejected: checking ranges against the file in the script. A Workflow script cannot read files.
  - Rejected: cutting long lists in the script. That is the pattern Amendment 2026-10-25: user-facing text comes from bounded fields (issues 441, 445 and 452) rejects.
  - Rejected: removing the documents list (Q1 option B).
  - Rejected: recording that the lists need no bound (Q1 option C).
  - Bounded impact of the paths list. `exists` is a Glob boolean. `drift` is the scope agent's own bounded note, at most 400 characters. The planner reads each whole section, so it sees every path the section names and checks any path the list leaves out. A wrong drift note meets the real file at the wave, and the step comes back as `needs_replan`.
  - `maxItems`, like `maxLength`, is outside the strict structured-output subset. It shares the `maxLength` bounded residual of Amendment 2026-10-25: user-facing text comes from bounded fields (issues 441, 445 and 452), "The bound rests on two schema checks": it relies on the Workflow agent schema check and on the script check. It was never tested in the harness (Amendment 2026-10-26: the harness schema check on agent() output (issue 459)). A list over its bound that reaches the script fails `SCOPE_SCHEMA`, and the run stops blocked before planning.
  - Surface account. Removed: the free-text `lines` field, its 100-character bound, its range syntax and the rule to read only the named ranges. Added: the integer `line` field, the two `maxItems` bounds, and the `maxItems` keyword in the validator bundled into both built scripts. The surface grows by one validator keyword. The user accepted that growth on 2026-10-27 by choosing Q1 option A, and this bullet records the user's decision at its source.
  - Nothing is deferred, and no residual is recorded.
  - Text: `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/scope.js`, `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/meta.js`, contract row SD-61 in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/contract.js`, `plugin/workflow-src/lib/schema.js`, the `## Scope Block` and `### Scope Mode` sections of `plugin/agents/cerebrate.md`, and the built `plugin/workflows/standard-delivery.js` and `plugin/workflows/analysis-only.js`; tests: `tests/workflow-scripts/standard-delivery.test.mjs`, `tests/workflow-scripts/cerebrate-plan.test.mjs`, `tests/workflow-scripts/schema.test.mjs`.
- **Local review remediation: governance citations are named-section reads (local review finding 0ed9c49a).** Root cause: the lazy loads above removed the eager loads of `definitions.md` from the planner, but its citations kept the form "per `definitions.md` (Section)", which names a section without saying to read it. A planner that no longer loads the doc could apply the rule without its text. The older citations of `security-policy.md` (External Content Boundary) and `decision-autonomy.md` (Promotion Gate) in the review remediation mode had the same flaw. Fixed: each citation in `plugin/agents/cerebrate.md` says to read the named section where the rule applies: `## Transient Failure`, `## Brood-Plan` and `## Strain` of `definitions.md`, `## External Content Boundary` of `security-policy.md`, and `## Promotion Gate` of `decision-autonomy.md`. The memory rules no longer cite `definitions.md` and apply the Research Rules retry rule instead. The new test in `tests/workflow-scripts/cerebrate-plan.test.mjs` covers the class: every sentence of `cerebrate.md` that names a governance doc either reads a named section of it or loads and follows it. Nothing is deferred, and no residual is recorded.
- **Measured lengths.** The built `plugin/workflows/standard-delivery.js` is 151,155 characters, up from 148,222 and 38,845 under the 190,000 budget of decision 18, which is unchanged. It holds 42,450 characters of string and template literals, so its entry in `PROSE_BUDGETS` in `tools/build_workflows.mjs` rises from 42,000 to 43,000, a reviewed change within the margin of 1,000 that `tests/workflow-scripts/build.test.mjs` enforces. `plugin/workflows/analysis-only.js` is 70,297 characters, up from 69,999 for the `maxItems` keyword in its bundled validator. It holds 18,564 characters of string and template literals, within its unchanged entry of 19,000.
- **Surface account.** Added: the Scope step, which is one agent call per pass, one schema (`SCOPE_SCHEMA`), one phase (`Scope`) and the module `scope.js`; the `### Scope Mode` and `## Scope Block` sections and the read discipline lines of `cerebrate.md`; contract rows SD-59 to SD-64; and the test `tests/workflow-scripts/agent-loads.test.mjs`. Removed: the eager doctrine loads in the planner, the drone and the overlord, the eager versioning and definitions loads in the planner, and the planner's whole-document reads of what the request names. The fix for local review finding 57aa38e7 removed the scope's `text` field, its 20,000-character bound and the word-for-word copy rule, and added the external-content label on `lines` and `drift`. The fix for local review findings 61758d1a and f6871d5b removed the `lines` field, its 100-character bound, its range syntax and the read-only-the-ranges rule, and added the integer `line` field, two `maxItems` bounds and the `maxItems` keyword of `validateSchema`, a growth of one validator keyword that the user accepted through Q1 option A. The fix for local review finding 0ed9c49a rewords four governance citations of `cerebrate.md` as named-section reads, points a fifth, in the memory rules, at the Research Rules retry rule, and adds one test. The built `standard-delivery.js` is 151,155 characters with 42,450 characters of string and template literals, and `analysis-only.js` is 70,297 characters with 18,564.
- **Recorded decision: no measurement of run cost.** Issue 463 item 5 (Measurement) and its two done-when lines, the per-phase cost lines and a rerun that shows the planner's share under 87%, are out of scope by user decision on 2026-10-27. The plugin tracks no token usage, per run, per phase or per agent. A finding or plan that asks for that measurement is a repeat of this decision.
- **Bounded residuals.** Neither is a found root cause.
  - `analysis-only` has no Scope step. It gets the `cerebrate.md` changes, the lazy loads and the read discipline, and its planner still reads a named document by section itself.
  - The facts file keeps its shape. The read-once rule removes the repeated whole-file reads of it, so no change of shape is needed.

It updates this place in place, with a dated note:

- Consequences: the fixed agent calls per run.

The rest of ADR-0037 is unchanged.

Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/463, `docs/adr/0038-root-cause-at-the-first-finding.md` (triage).

## Amendment 2026-10-27: workflow cost outside the planner (issue 464)

This amendment covers https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/464, the companion to issue 463. Local transcripts showed the validate drone at a median of 11.1 minutes and 11.7 hours in total, a whole agent for every script step, wave drones of up to 117 turns, and local reviewer rounds of up to 159 turns. Per ADR-0038, the fix removes work at its source, one owning layer at a time, before it makes any step cheaper.

**Root causes, by owning layer.** Each is inherited from main.

- **Governance.** The Validation Procedure in `plugin/governance/definitions.md` said to run every command the project's `CLAUDE.md` lists. This repo lists `--changed`, `--all` and `--self-test` there, so 34 of 35 validate agents ran all three, and `--changed` already escalates to the full suite by itself.
- **Engine step design.** The validate prompt sent the drone to look the procedure up. A validate-commit runner followed a step that edits nothing. Validation results were not keyed by commit, so a fix round that committed nothing validated the same commit again. The bump took three agents, ran again on every head move and made its edit on Opus, as the scan did. Commit runners got no subject, so each ran `git status` and `git diff` to write one.
- **Tests.** `tools/test_change_detect_poll.sh` and `tools/test_brood_compat.sh` slept in real time, and `tools/test_brood_compat.sh` built each git fixture per case.
- **Agent contracts.** `plugin/agents/drone.md` and `plugin/agents/changeling.md` loaded three governance docs on every dispatch. The local reviewer ran the full Validation Procedure after each fix, and its long calls were backgrounded and then polled. The planner had no step-size rule, and it made git probes into drone steps.
- **github-review-loop.** `fix-history-classify.jq` never read the Codex summary state, so a Codex Review Summary with no findings was actionable on every fetch.
- **The harness.** The harness relays the user's message to every workflow agent, and the script cannot stop that (Q2 below).

**The fixes.**

- **Validation runs each gate command once.** The Validation Procedure runs each gate command the project's `CLAUDE.md` names, once. A command listed as another form of the same suites, a diagnostic or a CI-only run is not part of the gate. This repo's `CLAUDE.md` names one gate command, `bash tools/validate.sh --changed`, and lists `--all` and `--self-test` as CI and manual checks only. Text: `plugin/governance/definitions.md` (`## Validation Procedure`), `CLAUDE.md`; fixture: `tests/policy/safety-validation-gate.json`.
- **The plan names the validation commands (Q1, the user's decision).** The plan gains the required field `validation_commands`, each entry one gate command line copied from `CLAUDE.md`. When the list is not empty, the validate drone gets the commands in a JSON inputs block, and running each once, in order and in the foreground, is its whole task, under `VALIDATE_COMMANDS_SCHEMA`, which offers no `not_run`. The user chose option A, with one change: a missing list falls back to option B and never skips validation. The plan format requires every field (the whole-plan rule pinned in `tests/workflow-scripts/plan-schema.test.mjs`), so the field stays required and a missing list arrives as an empty one. An empty list gives the drone the Validation Procedure as its whole task, which runs the gate command `CLAUDE.md` names, once, under `VALIDATE_SCHEMA`, which keeps `not_run`. Only when `CLAUDE.md` names no command is validation "Not run", as before. The earlier rule that an empty list calls no agent is dropped. The plan check refuses, before the branch step, an entry that is blank or holds a line break or another control character, naming the entry and its reason. Text: `plugin/references/plan-schema.json`, `plugin/agents/cerebrate.md`, `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/gates.js` and `plan.js`, contract rows SD-08 and SD-25; tests: `tests/workflow-scripts/plan-schema.test.mjs`, `tests/workflow-scripts/standard-delivery.test.mjs`.
- **The validate-commit runner is folded away.** Validation is read-only, so no commit runner follows it, and validation ends on the head it ran on. A dirty path the validate drone leaves is refused by the next commit runner or by the dirty check of `hivemind:open-plan-pr`, so no dirty tree reaches a push (invariant 1). Contract rows SD-25 and SD-27; the label `validate-commit-P-N` is gone from SD-28.
- **Results are keyed by head.** A failed validation result is kept for the head it ran on. After a fix round that commits nothing, no validate agent runs, and the next `validation_remediation` decision takes the kept failures, which count toward `maxFixRounds()`. Contract row SD-26.
- **The bump collapses, and a skip key keeps it held.** With no version files, a haiku runner invokes `hivemind:bump-type`, label `bump-type-P-N`. With version files, one `hivemind:drone` on `BUMP_MODEL` `sonnet` invokes it and, only on a `bump_required` or `no_bump` verdict, sets each version file in the same call, label `bump-P-N`. The commit runner then commits exactly `artifact_files`, so the bump takes 2 agents, not 3. The skip key is `keepBumpHeld`: once the bump held on head H in a pass, a later head move keeps it held on the new head, with its note and no agent call, only when the counts the held bump relayed on H, kept as the gate-state value `bumpValues`, have one non-zero row and `dropped_reverts` 0, some run commit up to H is one the commit runner of this pass made with the plan commit type and breaking flag and with no `artifact_files` path among its files, and every run commit since H is such a commit. The key is that narrow because bump-type's verdict depends on the row counts of the whole evaluated range (the dominant-row rules 1 to 6 of `plugin/skills/_shared/bump-type-derive.sh`), not on each commit alone. The branch's local review (finding 0f560bb6) found that the earlier key, which read only each new commit's metadata, could keep a verdict over a commit that changes it: a restart whose waves commit nothing holds `no_bump` over older docs commits, and a later `feat` fix commit then ships unbumped. A kept commit now adds only to the range's one row, which already holds a commit of its kind, so it cannot change the dominant row or the verdict, and no bump-type rule is copied into the script. Any other move, such as a recorded range, a commit made outside the commit runner such as reviewer fixes, or a runner commit that lists a version file, runs the bump again. The scan runs on `sonnet` too, not on the drone's Opus default. Contract rows SD-06, SD-21, SD-24, SD-27, SD-41 and SD-65. Amended 2026-10-29 (issue 457): the bump step is gone, and with it the collapse and the keep-held key: `keepBumpHeld`, `bumpValues`, `BUMP_SCHEMA`, `BUMP_MODEL`, and rows SD-24 and SD-65. A plan step sets the version, and validation checks it (see Amendment 2026-10-29: a stop pauses unless nothing can go on, and the version check moves into validation (issue 457)).
- **The bound on decide() running after the edit.** The bump drone edits the version files before `decide()` reads its verdict. The edit is bounded: it stays in the working tree, it is reversible, and nothing is pushed. `version_bump_type` is `auto` at the default level (`balanced`) and at `high` in `plugin/references/decision-autonomy-table.json`, so at the default level the verdict is taken and the edit is committed. At `low`, or on an `ask_user` or indeterminable verdict, the run asks; the drone prompt tells it to edit nothing on an `ask_user` verdict, and any edit left behind is refused by the dirty check of the next facts step, which stops the run before planning. Contract row SD-22. Amended 2026-10-29 (issue 457): closed. No drone edits a version before a `decide()` call: a plan step sets it, and validation only reads it.
- **Commit subjects are built by code.** Every commit inputs block carries a subject, so the commit runner reads no git state to write one. A wave of one step gives its outcome, and a wave of several gives their ids, then the first outcome, made one line and cut at a word boundary to at most 72 code points. The bump, fix round and review commits have fixed subjects. A derived subject that is blank, holds `!:` or a `BREAKING CHANGE` marker, or holds an attribution string the molt skill forbids falls back to a fixed subject naming the wave. `hivemind:molt` gains the optional input `subject`, and when the caller passes both `files` and `subject` it runs no git command of its own. Text: `plugin/skills/molt/SKILL.md`, `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/relay.js`, contract rows SD-15 and SD-66.
- **The reviewer's caller_validates input.** `plugin/agents/local-reviewer.md` gains the optional input `caller_validates`, absent meaning `false`. With `true`, the check after each fix is the Worker Self-Check over only the files that fix touched, and never the Validation Procedure or a project-wide suite, because the caller validates every commit the loop leaves. The script passes `caller_validates: true`, contract row SD-32. The local reviewer and `hivemind:adaptation-cycle` run every long call, the Codex review included, in the foreground with the Bash tool's `timeout`, and never poll a background process.
- **Worker and planner contracts.** The drone and the changeling load no governance doc on dispatch, and read a governance section only when a rule names it and the task reaches that rule. The changeling's review remediation names the External Content Boundary and the Destructive Fix Confirmation Gate itself. The planner sizes each step as one concern that one drone can carry, splitting a step that mixes concerns or edits more than about four files beyond its own tests and generated output, and never makes research, a probe or a measurement a plan step. Text: `plugin/agents/drone.md`, `plugin/agents/changeling.md`, `plugin/agents/cerebrate.md`; tests: `tests/workflow-scripts/agent-loads.test.mjs`, `tests/workflow-scripts/cerebrate-plan.test.mjs`.
- **Tests.** Both shell suites fake `sleep` through a PATH shim that advances the fake clock, and `tools/test_brood_compat.sh` builds one seed repo per git fixture shape and copies it per case.
- **The Codex summary state.** `fix-history-classify.jq` reads a Codex Review Summary as handled when the Codex bot account authors it, its body starts with the summary marker, and its state parses and reports no findings or a running review. A missing, unparseable or findings state falls back to the `EYES` rule, so a summary edited in place to report findings is actionable again on the next fetch. Text: `plugin/skills/github-review-loop/SKILL.md`; fixtures: `tests/fix-history/case21` to `case24`; test: `tools/test_fix_history_classify.sh`.

**Recorded limit: request relay (Q2, the user's decision).** The harness relays the user's message to every agent a workflow run starts, and the script cannot drop it. So each runner and worker still receives the request text, whatever its task. This is a recorded limit on ADR-0038's ground that no fix on this engine can close the class, and the user decided so. What the script controls is closed: runner prompts carry no request text (SD-18), and decision 24's sentence tells each later agent that a resume reply was already applied. Cheaper runners, which would also shrink what the relay costs, are issue 467, not this change.

**Recorded decision: the 3-minute validate median is not measured here.** Issue 464's done-when line for a validate median under 3 minutes, and its per-phase cost lines, are not measured in this change, by user decision. The plugin tracks no token usage or wall time per run, phase or agent, as the decision on issue 463 records. Validation now runs once per head and runs `--changed` once instead of three suites. The time of that one run depends on the suites it selects: an edit under `tools/**` escalates `--changed` to the full suite, so a run that changes `tools/**`, as this one does, stays as long as the full suite. A finding or plan that asks for that measurement is a repeat of this decision.

**Recorded decision: a running Codex review keeps the summary handled.** Issue 464 scope item 7 asks that a Codex Review Summary not be actionable on every fetch, and the user confirmed this reading in the answer to the branch's local review question. A Codex Review Summary from the Codex bot account whose state reports `code_review.status` `running` is handled, even when a review entry already reports `has_any_findings` `true`. Bounded impact: the summary carries no finding, because Codex posts each finding as its own review or thread comment, which the classifier reads on its own surface. A clean exit still needs an in-scope approval and the quiet-exit check. Trunk dispatched a summary once and hid its later edits behind `EYES`, so trunk did not wait for Codex either. A summary edited to a findings state that is no longer running is actionable again on the next fetch. A later finding contests this decision, and is a repeat of it, when it asks to treat a running summary as pending or to hold a clean or approval exit while Codex runs.

**Measured lengths.** Each length is the JavaScript string length of the committed built script, measured with node, and each prose count is `countProseCharacters` in `tools/build_workflows.mjs`.

| Script | On main at 1672b65 | After issue 464 | Literals after issue 464 | Prose budget |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `standard-delivery.js` | 151,903 | 157,315 | 43,614 | 44,000, up from 43,000 |
| `analysis-only.js` | 70,375 | 70,488 | 18,676 | 19,000, unchanged |

`standard-delivery.js` stays 32,685 characters under the 190,000 budget of decision 18, which is unchanged. Its prose budget rises by 1,000, a reviewed change within the margin `tests/workflow-scripts/build.test.mjs` enforces. `analysis-only.js` grows only by the `validation_commands` field of its bundled `PLAN_SCHEMA` and the scan's `sonnet` model.

**Surface account.** Removed: the validate-commit runner on every gate run, the separate bump-type runner when version files exist, most bump and validation reruns, the eager governance loads of the drone and the changeling, the per-fix validation runs of the local reviewer, the rule to run every command `CLAUDE.md` lists, and the real sleeps of two test suites. Added: the plan field `validation_commands`, which the user accepted with Q1; the optional molt input `subject`; the optional local-reviewer input `caller_validates`; `keepBumpHeld` with its check that the held bump's relayed counts have one row and no dropped revert and that its range already held a matching runner commit, the gate-state value `bumpValues`, `BUMP_SCHEMA`, `VALIDATE_COMMANDS_SCHEMA` and contract rows SD-65 and SD-66; and the step-sizing and foreground rules.

It updates these places in place, each with a dated note:

- Consequences: the fixed agent calls per run, and the cost of a validation fix round.
- Risks: the relayed user message outranks runner tasks.

The rest of ADR-0037 is unchanged.

Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/464, https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/467, `docs/adr/0038-root-cause-at-the-first-finding.md` (triage and recorded residuals).

## Amendment 2026-10-28: answers kept across replans, and a build check on resume (issue 472)

This amendment covers https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/472.

**Evidence.** Consumer run `wf_fb7e8d56-6ad` (game-test #77). The user answered Q1 with A. After a branch replan, the new plan pass had no record of that answer. It asked again with the recommendation the user had rejected, its gate flags were false, and `decide()` took it, because the `planner_question` cell at `high` is `auto`. A second loss came from a plugin update during the run. The cache keys changed, every step ran again from pass 1, and the stored answers were never read. Separately, plan questions about files outside the agreed list were taken automatically.

**Root causes, by owning layer.** Triaged per ADR-0038.

- **Run state.** A reply was tied to its question only by round number, and it was read only at the pause that asked it. No later planner call saw it. Nothing checked on resume that round N was still the same question.
- **Acceptance.** No code compared the plan's files with a limit that the request or the user set.

**Decisions (the user's, 2026-10-28).**

- **The answer log.** `run.answerLog`, a list of `{question, settled, reply}`, replaces `run.replies`. `question` holds every question shown in the round, `reply` the user's words, and `settled` the Q and D labels of that round the reply settled, such as `Q1` or `D1`. In standard-delivery, `settled` comes from the revise result's `settled` enum once the plan check passes. In analysis-only, it comes from a new required `settled` field in its revise schema. A tail ask, which no revise reads, logs `settled` as `[]` through `buildAnswerEntry`. Every later plan, revise and scope prompt carries an `answers` block: after a branch, step, tail or review replan, and in later passes. Only the labelled questions and decisions are called settled. Every other question in an entry stays open and is asked again. Pass-1 prompts are unchanged, so replay from cache on the same build still holds. Amended 2026-10-28 (issue 460, Q1 option A): `settled` holds the Q labels of the round only. A pause shows no taken decision, so a reply can settle no D label (see Amendment 2026-10-28: one message shape (issue 460)).
- **The build id (Q1, option A).** The build injects `BUILD_ID`, the first 16 hex characters of the sha256 of the built output. `resume_args` is `{answers, build}`. A resume whose build is absent or different stops before any agent call, with: "The plugin changed while the delivery was paused, so nothing ran. Run the request again with your answers in it." Amended 2026-10-28 (local review finding 49be981e): the stop is one cause with the problem "The plugin changed while the delivery was paused, so nothing ran." and the code-only next action `answer`. `finish` builds the message, so the stop ends "Next: run the request again with the answers in it." It no longer repeats that sentence in its text and no longer tells the user to fix a cause.
- **Limits (Q2, option B).** The scope step returns `limits`, taken from the request and from the user's answers. A later answer wins. The step runs again on each pass with the answers block, and once more after each reply round that settles no shown acceptance (label `scope-P-R`). A plan acceptance, `plan_acceptance` or `remediation_plan_acceptance`, whose plan paths, its step files and version files, fall outside the limits, has no recommendation and is always asked. Its detail line names up to 5 of the outside paths. Limits are never dropped. A bad limit matches nothing, so the plan is asked about. Amended 2026-10-28 (issue 460): the detail line is gone. The planning pause lead names up to 5 outside paths, then a count of the rest, each whole and encoded. Plan path items carry `maxLength` 400, so no path is cut (see Amendment 2026-10-28: one message shape (issue 460)).
- **Prose budgets.** Each is set to its measured count, within the existing rule of a margin of at most 1,000.
- **Local review, 2026-10-28.** Finding f20424de: a run-wide list of accepted outside paths let a later pass take a plan that changed such a file without asking. The list is removed, so the limits come only from the scope step and a later pass asks again unless its limits now hold the file. Finding 753d0c8e, that malformed resume answers are treated as a fresh launch, is rejected under ADR-0036 decision 13.
- **Local review, 2026-10-28, findings 96e0598c and 4abfb21a.** Root cause: an answer-log entry recorded one reply against every question shown in its round, and the data line called the whole entry settled. So questions the reply left open were told to later plan, revise and scope passes as settled. 96e0598c is the flaw in standard-delivery `plan.js`, and 4abfb21a is the same flaw in analysis-only. Fix: each entry carries `settled`, the labels the reply settled, so the claim is keyed by round and label from ground truth. The data line and the planner rule call only those settled. D labels are included because a reply can change a decision made for the user, and that change is settled too. Class removed: a log entry or data line that claims a question settled when the reply did not settle it. Rejected: the reviewer's option, which logs only settled questions plus a separate `run.replies`. Declined acceptances such as "no, only docs", changed D decisions and the open state of Q2 would be lost to later passes.
