Residual: which questions a reply settles stays the planner's judgment (decision 3). A question left out is asked again, and its reply stays in the log as the user's words. Amended 2026-10-28 (issue 460, Q1 option A): D labels are no longer settleable, because a pause shows no taken decision. The claim keyed by round and label stands for the Q labels (see Amendment 2026-10-28: one message shape (issue 460)).
- **Local review, 2026-10-28, finding b61a140f.** Root cause: each producer of an answer-log entry decided `settled` itself. Nothing checked the tail claim of `['Q1']`, and that line was written on the branch, while the user chose option A: per-label marks from checked results. Fix: one writer, `buildAnswerEntry(round, revise)` in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/brief.js`. It takes `settled` only from a schema-checked revise result, and a source-pin test fails on any other writer. Class removed: an answer-log entry that calls a label settled when no schema-checked revise result named it. Rejected: taking `settled` from the next plan result or the rerun review result, or adding a classification step. Each grows the surface for a claim nothing needs, since tail asks are built in code, belong to their moment and are asked again when the condition recurs. Bounded consequence: a tail reply is never settled, so a later pass may raise its question again. At the default level, a planner question with clean gates that departs from it is taken. This is bounded. Tail asks belong to their moment, and code asks them again. Review decisions are recorded at source in the reviewer's record. Binding answers come through a planner question and a checked revise.
- **Local review, 2026-10-28, finding 75535594.** Brief text the planner writes is shown as written by `renderText` in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/brief.js`, so it can show fake Q or D labels, options or reply lines. This is inherited from `main`. Destination: issue 460, the existing tracked home, under its finding 2c8e2101. Its plan assigns positional labels and option ids in code and adds a schema-walk test. Linked ids: 75535594 and 2c8e2101. Bounded impact: display only. The `settled` enum is built in code from `listShownLabels`, and `buildAnswerEntry` keeps only shown labels, so a forged label can never be logged settled. Until issue 460 lands, ADR-0037 Risks residual (b), "Planner prose is shown as written", covers it. Link: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/460. Amended 2026-10-28 (issue 460): closed by construction at the one render point. Option letters are assigned by position in code, agent text is one line, and a forged label or option can no longer start a line of a question. Residual (b) narrows to inline Markdown (see Amendment 2026-10-28: one message shape (issue 460)).
- **Local review, 2026-10-28, finding 2ecc5af1.** Root cause: the limit check read a hand-picked field, `steps[].files`, while `versioning.artifact_files`, which the bump commit takes, went unread. So a version file outside the limits was committed with no ask. Fix: `collectRepositoryPaths` and `listPlanPaths` give one mark walk, shared with `findRepositoryPathProblem`, and the limit check and the detail line both read it. Class removed: a path the plan names for a commit that the outside-limit check does not read. A test pins it: every wave and bump commit takes only paths from `listPlanPaths`. Recorded decision (user, Q1 A): the limits fence the paths the plan names and are checked at plan acceptance. Edits made by a validation fix round or by local review are outside them. REPEAT scope: a finding that asks the limits to cover fix round or local review edits. NEW scope: a finding that shows a plan path field the check does not read, or a wave or bump commit taking a path outside `listPlanPaths`. The record is matched by this scope, not by finding id. Bounded impact: fix edits are branch commits, listed in the final message and in the PR the user reviews before any merge. `molt.sh` refuses dirty paths outside a fix round's own list. Agents never merge. Cost: a limited run with a version file outside the limits asks at plan acceptance even when no bump follows. The ask adds a line to the existing plan acceptance question, never a new stop, and answering it triggers no replan. Rejected: naming `artifact_files` by hand, which completes only the known set. A commit-time limit check, which stops after the edit, needs per-pass accepted paths like those removed under f20424de, cannot see reviewer commits without a new file report, and grows the surface. Exempting version files, because the planner names them and planner-declared fences were already rejected.

**Rejected.**

- Stopping at the waves when stored answers go unused. That would also stop legitimate resumes of a tail question and of a later pass.
- Feeding stored replies into pass 1 again without their questions. A reply without its question is ambiguous.
- Storing the question text in `resume_args`. It uses the room for carried data, and it still binds a reply to the wrong question across builds.
- Limits declared by the planner. The planner would set the fence for its own plan.
- A gate flag in the brief.
- Setting the `planner_question` cell at `high` to surface. Every question would then be asked.

**Residuals.**

- A planner that raises a settled question again despite the answers block, with clean gates, is still taken automatically.
- A run paused on 7.3.0 stops once after the upgrade, because its resume carries no build. This is intended.

Decision 2 is unaffected. The build id is a replay guard, not a run record, and git and GitHub stay the record.

It updates this place in place, with a dated note:

- Recorded residuals: a plugin update between pause and resume (decision 8), now closed.

The rest of ADR-0037 is unchanged.

Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/472, `docs/adr/0038-root-cause-at-the-first-finding.md` (triage).

## Amendment 2026-10-28: one message shape (issue 460)

This amendment covers https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/460. After #458 (7.1.3), the user still called run messages a wall of text that did not say what happened or what to decide, and the #442 live check failed. This fix gives every pause and every run end one short shape, built in code, and makes an agent failure say what it is. It also closes local review findings 7703cd64, 2c8e2101 (claim c) and 75535594 at their source. The work was first done on an earlier branch and is ported here onto main with the #472 answer log, build id and file limits kept.

**The logged evidence.** The session logs the issue names, and the runs of #459 and #464, show three classes of message.

- **Review question menus.** On #459 (run wf_7401a671-110, 7.1.3), a local review question reached the user as a generic menu, "A: Answer the finding" and "B: Stop", followed by the reviewer's question, its recommendation, the finding and 11 commit hashes. The user asked "What do you want from me". The real choice was buried in quoted text. On #464, the high-severity rejection of local review finding 0b989e77 showed the same kind of menu, and the user could not tell what it asked ("You give me the choices but I have no idea what the problem is"). The engine also labelled its own firm recommendation weak.
- **Stops that named no cause.** On #464, an expired login stopped the run at the branch step with "Fix the cause named above", and no cause was named above it. The bump step stopped as malformed output after its runner printed valid routing lines and then one line of prose.
- **Planner question pauses.** A planner question pause put a bold heading, a criticality line and a "Why I am asking" line around each question, and after the questions the taken decisions and, for plan acceptance, a detail block. A message that ran long switched to a compact form of the same questions. The ask sank under text the user did not need to read.

**Root cause and owning layer.** Triaged per ADR-0038, the cause is inherited from main. The owning layer is the engine's message composer: `renderQuestions` and the compact form in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/brief.js`, the pause and run end builders in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/record.js` and `plugin/workflow-src/analysis-only/main.js`, and the review question in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/review.js`. #458 bounded each agent field the composer shows in length (Amendment 2026-10-25: user-facing text comes from bounded fields (issues 441, 445 and 452)), but it did not bound what the composer puts together: headings, criticality and ask-reason lines, detail blocks, commit lists, taken decisions in a pause, and a menu around the reviewer's question. So each part was short and the whole was still a wall. The second root is in the same layer. `agent()` returns `null` when its agent fails on an API error, such as an expired login (Amendment 2026-10-26: the harness schema check on agent() output (issue 459)), and the engine read that `null` as malformed output, so the stop sent the user to look for a cause in the repo. Removed instead of hardened: every part of a message that is not the lead, the ask, its options, its recommendation or the Next line.

**The relay rule stays.** Decided, Q1 of the first planning pass: the overlord still presents `message` as its whole reply, as `plugin/agents/overlord.md` (`## Engine`) says, and that file does not change. The engine is the one author of user text, so the fix belongs where the message is built. An overlord that rewrites each message stays rejected, as Amendment 2026-10-25 records, and #459 shows why. There the user's bare "A" to the overlord's plain rewrite clashed with the engine's own option A, which carried no answer, and a confirm round was needed. A second author's labels and reply tokens drift from what the run reads back.

**The message shape.**

- **A pause is a lead, the questions and the reply line.** The call site builds a one-line lead that says what happened. Each question is its "Qn. problem" line, one line per option with its letter and " (recommended)" on the recommended one, and one line "Recommendation: X (strength). why". The reply line closes the message. There is no heading, no criticality or ask-reason line, no detail block, no commit list and no taken decision, and there is no compact form. analysis-only pauses the same way with its own lead, `renderPauseLead`. Text: `renderQuestions` in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/brief.js`, `pauseDelivery` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/record.js`, `plugin/workflow-src/analysis-only/main.js`.
- **The outside-limit paths move into the lead.** The #472 detail line is gone with the detail block. A plan acceptance or remediation plan acceptance whose plan paths fall outside the limits names up to 5 of them in the planning pause lead, each whole and encoded, then the count of the rest. Plan path items in `plugin/references/plan-schema.json`, in `steps[].files` and `versioning.artifact_files`, get `maxLength` 400, matching the scope path fields, so no path is cut. `describePlanLine` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/plan.js` builds the lead.
- **A run end is its lead and one line per fact.** A blocked end is its lead, then the Next line, then one line naming the branch, the commit count and the head only when the run made commits. A complete end names the pull request and its commit, and the gates that held. No chat message lists the commits. Only the pull request body and comment do. Text: `finishDeliveryBlocked`, `finishDeliveryComplete` and `finishOpenQuestions` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/record.js`. Amended 2026-10-28 (local review finding 49be981e): the lead and the Next line of every blocked end are built by `finish` in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/result.js` from the causes, so the text is `finish` there, with `finishDeliveryBlocked` adding the later blocks. Amended 2026-10-29 (PR 477 review, round 2): the branch line names the encoded branch and its head once a wave ran, with no commit count. The pull request body and comment no longer list the commits either: the branch and the pull request on GitHub hold them (decision 2).
- **A local review question is the reviewer's own question.** The `user-input-required` and `high-severity-rejection` exits carry `finding_id` and `user_question`, a brief the reviewer writes for the user: `found`, what the finding claims and why it matters, at most 200 characters; `ask`, the question in one line, at most 200; 2 to 4 `options` with ids `A` to `D` in that order and summaries of at most 160; and a `recommendation` that names one option, with an honest `strength` and a `why` of at most 200. A rejection always offers both keeping the rejection, recorded at its source, and fixing the finding. The pause lead names the commit, the finding by the first 8 code points of its `finding_id`, and the encoded `found`. The question's options are the reviewer's options, so the user's reply is the reviewer's own choice, and no menu that asks whether to answer or stop wraps it. A `user_question` whose option ids are not `A`, `B`, `C`, `D` in order, which covers a repeated id, or that recommends no option of its own, is unusable, as a schema breach is. Text: `plugin/agents/local-reviewer.md` (the Output Contract and The Loop step 1), `REVIEW_SCHEMA`, `hasUnusableQuestion`, `describeReviewProblem` and `describeReviewQuestionLead` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/review.js`, rows SD-33 and SD-37.
- **An agent failure names itself.** `describeNoResult` in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/result.js` gives one fixed sentence for a `null` result: the step's agent failed before it returned a result, as an agent API error such as an expired login, a rate limit or a network failure does. `describeUnusableResult` in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/result.js` is the one helper both engines use when they read an agent result they cannot use. For a `null` result it adds that sentence once and sets the next action to `run_again`. Any other result keeps its problem alone. No other file appends the sentence: the facts messages in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/facts.js`, the planner stops and the analysis-only stops all go through it. Amended 2026-10-28 (local review findings 3827ab1a and 58a21e45): the helper moved here from `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/relay.js` and lost its `endsWith` check, because no site appends the sentence any more (see Local review findings 3827ab1a and 58a21e45).

**Eliminated classes.**

- **The shape (row SD-53).** Every pause is a one-line lead, the questions and the reply line last. Every run end is its lead, followed by the Next line when blocked. No message shows a commit list, a criticality or ask-reason line, a detail block, the plan summary, or a word-for-word section other than an irreversible effect's. No pause shows a taken decision, and each taken decision takes one line. Each message stays within the fixed bound its test states. Class eliminated: nothing reaches a message but a lead, the questions with their options and recommendations, the Next line and one-line facts and decisions. Text: row SD-53 in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/contract.js`; test: `tests/workflow-scripts/standard-delivery.test.mjs`.
- **Agent failures (row SD-54).** A `null` result from any `agent()` call ends the run blocked, with the `describeNoResult` sentence once in its first block and the `run_again` Next line as its second. The test takes its labels from a real run: it runs a happy delivery, reads every label that run calls, and makes each one in turn return `null`, so a step added later is covered with no change to the test. Named cases cover the labels a happy run does not reach, such as the scan, a revise, the bump and a fix round. Class eliminated: no agent failure reads as malformed output. Amended 2026-10-28 (local review findings 3827ab1a and 58a21e45): the class now covers compound stops and analysis-only. A `null` result ends the run blocked with its sentence shown once. On both engines the Next line follows the lead, and a commit stop on the same step cannot hide the agent failure. Text: row SD-54; test: `tests/workflow-scripts/standard-delivery.test.mjs`.

**Where taken decisions now show (Q1, option A, the user, 2026-10-28).** A pause shows no taken decision. Every run end, complete, blocked or ended on open questions, shows each one on one line, "Dn. problem Chose id: summary.", with no why and no verbatim (`renderDecisionsCompact` in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/brief.js`). The pull request body and the reuse comment keep the full form with the text it accepted, so decisions 3 and 15 still hold on the pull request. This replaces the 2026-10-24 rule that a pause shows each taken decision as one line. It conflicts with #472, where a reply could settle D labels, and the user chose to follow the one-line shape: the `settled` list of the answer log holds the Q labels of the round only. `listShownLabels` returns Q labels only, and the `settled` enum built in code from it never offers a D label. `decisionLabel` and `buildAnswerEntry` in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/brief.js` are unchanged. `plugin/agents/cerebrate.md` says the same: the reply settles the Q labels. Rejected: one D line per taken decision in a pause with "You can change any of these", and D labels kept settleable. It keeps text in the pause that the user did not need to answer. Text: `plugin/agents/cerebrate.md`; test: `tests/workflow-scripts/cerebrate-plan.test.mjs`.

**Only a null result says to run again.** A `null` result gets the `run_again` Next line, because an agent failure has a cause outside the repo. A non-null result that fails its schema or its routing keeps the fix line, as the review step already did, because a prompt or schema in the repo can cause it. Whether a step should end blocked or pause is https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/457, not this change.

**Local review findings 2c8e2101 and 75535594.** Added 2026-10-28. Agent-written brief text and agent-authored option ids were rendered inside the question structure the code owns.

- **Triage of 2c8e2101, per ADR-0038.** The finding holds three claims. (a) A line break or Markdown in brief text forges option or reply lines. It repeats the recorded residual (b) of Risks, "Step text in a blocked message": planner prose is shown as written. (b) A malformed brief is rendered instead of refused. It is by design under ADR-0036 decision 14, "Brief checks come from the schema". standard-delivery already refuses a malformed brief at the plan check, and analysis-only asks it as `malformed_brief`. (c) Option ids and the recommendation's `option_id` had no bound, and `options` had no `maxItems`, yet every renderer showed them. Claim (c) is new. It is not covered by the 7703cd64 decision below, which covers review strings only. 75535594 is claim (a) as it reaches the answer log, and #472 sent it here. Linked ids: 75535594 and 2c8e2101.
- **Root cause.** Inherited from main. The renderer trusted two kinds of agent output inside code-owned structure: the text of every brief field, and the option ids the agent chose. Size and shape were bounded by checking untrusted input, not by how the renderer is built. 7703cd64 was the same class, bounded by code cuts. Owning layer: the one point where a brief is rendered, `plugin/workflow-src/lib/brief.js`.
- **Fix, by construction.**
  - `optionLabel(index)` assigns option letters by position, and every renderer shows letters by position. The recommended mark, the recommendation line, open questions and decisions use the letter of the option whose id matches the recommendation, and show "not given" when none matches. So an agent-authored id never reaches a message as a letter or a label.
  - `renderText` is the one display form of agent prose. It turns C0 and C1 control characters, DEL, U+2028, U+2029 and bidi controls into spaces, and makes each whitespace run one space. So agent text is one line and can never start a line of the question structure.
  - The question's "Word for word:" text shows through `encodeUntrustedText`, which loses nothing. The pull request body keeps the verbatim raw inside its fence.
  - The brief schema allows at most 4 options, and its `id` and `option_id` descriptions say the question shows options by position, A to D. A review `user_question` must use the ids A, B, C, D in order.
  - Two tests hold it. A structure fuzz in `tests/workflow-scripts/brief.test.mjs` puts hostile text in every brief string and checks that the line count and the code-owned line prefixes of the four renderers never change. A scan in `tests/workflow-scripts/data.test.mjs` checks every brief field the renderer reads against `plugin/references/decision-brief-schema.json`. Each field must be shown with a bound, internal (`id`, `option_id`, `kind`), an ask or round field, or `verbatim`, recorded as shown encoded in an exact ask and whole in the pull request body. A field that fits none fails.
- **Class eliminated.** No agent string starts a line of a question, and no agent text decides a label or a letter. The rule was "check the agent's text". It is now "the renderer does not trust it". A new brief field fails the scan test until it is classed.
- **Decided by the user (Q2, option A, 2026-10-28).** The fix goes ahead as above, with the test deletions and rewrites it needs. Rejected: the narrower fix of positional letters and the 4-option limit alone, which leaves forged question lines possible.
- **Residual (b) narrows.** What stays under it is inline Markdown within one line.

**Local review finding 7703cd64.** Added 2026-10-28. Messages still cut some agent values in code.

- **Root cause.** Inherited. The 2026-10-25 bounded-fields amendment wrote "or is cut by code" into row SD-58, so the class stayed open. The scan excerpt slice in `renderSuspectLine` and the step id slice (PR #458 review) are older. Owning layer: the step result schemas. Approach: remove before harden, option A, which the user approved on 2026-10-28.
- **Removed.** `cutExcerpt` and its tests, `quoteReviewValue`, `reviewFieldNames`, the conflicting findings list in a stop, the titles of rejected findings, four `cutExcerpt` calls, the step id slice, the scan excerpt slice, the cut of the 300 character blocker to 200, and the SD-58 carve-out.
- **The bounds.** `plugin/workflow-src/lib/schema.js` gains the `pattern` keyword: a string compiled with the `u` flag, not anchored, for string values only, and it fails closed on a bad pattern. The scan's `field_excerpt` gets `maxLength` 200 and `item_ref` 16, and `plan.branch` gets 200, each shown whole. In `REVIEW_SCHEMA`, `finding_id` gets `maxLength` 64 and the pattern `^[0-9a-f]{8,64}$`, at the top and in each entry of `rejected_findings`. `hypothesized_root` gets 300, `root_class` 100, `same_framing_rationale` 600, `classification` 100, `file` 400, `title` 300, `field_excerpt` 200, each `cluster_files` entry 400 and each `cluster_ids` entry 64. `pattern_category` is an enum of P1 to P4. Lists get `maxItems`: `signals_fired` and `signals_observed` 10, `conflicting_findings`, `cluster_files` and `cluster_ids` 20, and `rejected_findings` 50. `buildReviewFieldSchema` throws on a list with no `maxItems`.
- **What a stop shows.** A break-fix stop shows a count. An injection stop shows the short id, the category and the whole encoded excerpt. A blocked stop quotes the blocker whole at its 300 bound. Rejected findings show by short id only. The irreversible effect question names no step id. Rows SD-33, SD-36, SD-55 and SD-58 are rewritten.
- **Class eliminated.** No message cuts an agent value in the script. The rule was "bound it or cut it". It is now "bound it at the source or do not show it". Row SD-58 holds it. Its schema-walk test checks that every local review string a message shows or the planner gets has a bound in `REVIEW_SCHEMA`, and that every list in it has `maxItems`. Text: row SD-58 in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/contract.js`; test: `tests/workflow-scripts/standard-delivery.test.mjs`.
- **Decided by the user, option A.** An injection stop keeps the excerpt and the category, bounded at the source and shown whole and encoded. `plugin/governance/security-policy.md` is unchanged.
- **Recorded decision.** Review strings that reach no message and not the planner keep no `maxLength`: `ledger_path`, `stage`, `prior_fix_commit`, `latest_severity_max`, the entries of `signals_fired`, `signals_observed` and `conflicting_findings`, and the title and record of each rejected finding. A later finding that asks to bound them repeats this decision. A finding that shows one of them reaching a message, the planner or pull request text is new.
- **Bounded residual.** A branch over 200 characters, an `item_ref` over 16, or a list over its `maxItems` makes the step's result unusable after the harness retries. The `pattern` keyword was probed only with the default subagent, so a smoke run checks it before a run relies on it.

**Local review findings 3827ab1a and 58a21e45.** Added 2026-10-28. A later stop could hide an agent failure, and an analysis-only stop showed no Next line.

- **Triage, per ADR-0038.** Introduced on this branch: the agent-failure stop and row SD-54. The commit-before-read order at the wave, bump, fix and review sites is older (invariant 1). It became a defect only once a null result carried its own sentence and Next line. It repeats the surface of this amendment's own structural fix, "`describeUnusableResult` is the one helper every step uses", which did not close its class. So the gate sets `repeats_surface`, and the fix changes the primitive instead of adding checks.
- **Root cause.** A blocked stop was put together at each call site. Each site appended the agent-failure sentence itself, picked one next action and called the finish with one message. The four file-changing steps (wave, bump, fix round, review) ran `runCommitStep` before they read their own result, so they could only pick one of two stops. A commit stop then replaced the step's agent failure. analysis-only had its own one-block `finishBlocked` with no Next line. Owning layer: the run end builder in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/result.js`, shared by both engines, and `runCommitStep`, the one end of every file-changing step.
- **Fix, closed by construction.**
  - The blocked finish on both engines takes a list of causes, `{problem, nextAction?}`, never a message plus a next action. Amended 2026-10-28 (local review finding 49be981e): `finish` itself takes the causes. It accepts no caller lead or Next line, and throws when causes are not given exactly when the outcome is blocked (see Local review finding 49be981e).
  - `buildStopBlocks(causes)` in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/result.js` builds the lead, the Next line and the later causes. Causes that need a fix come first, then agent failures, each group in step order. The Next line is the fix line unless every cause says `run_again`. It throws on anything but a non-empty array.
  - `finishDeliveryBlocked(causes, run, endLines)` in `record.js` and analysis-only's `finishBlocked(causes)` call it. `nextStepLine` and `maxBlockedLeadLength` moved into the same file. Amended 2026-10-28 (local review finding 49be981e): `finishDeliveryBlocked` and `finishBlocked` call `finish` with the causes, and `finish` calls `buildStopBlocks`.
  - `runCommitStep(run, plan, commit, stepCauses)` takes the step's causes as a required argument and ends the run itself. Each file-changing site reads its result first and passes its causes. A commit problem adds the commit cause to the step's. When the commit succeeds and the step has causes, the run ends blocked, and an end line names the commit when one was made, because that commit can hold partial edits.
  - A new blocked end or a new file-changing step can only go through these functions, and each throws on anything but causes. Amended 2026-10-28 (local review finding 49be981e): this was true of the functions and not of every end, because the open-question stop and the plugin-changed stop built their own message. They are causes now, and the next section closes the class in `finish`.
- **Class eliminated.** A blocked end whose shown causes or Next line disagree with the step results that stopped the run. That covers a lost agent failure behind a later step, a stop with no Next line, and `run_again` shown over a cause that needs a fix. Row SD-54 and its compound tests hold it. Text: rows SD-14, SD-15, SD-16, SD-26, SD-34, SD-51 and SD-54 in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/contract.js`.
- **Recorded decision.** A cause that needs a fix leads, with the fix line, and the agent failure follows. Rejected: always showing `run_again` first. A commit stop can leave a dirty tree that must be fixed before a rerun. A later finding that asks for `run_again` over an in-repo cause repeats this decision.
- **Surface account.** Removed: `appendSentence` and four per-site sentence appends, the `endsWith` check, the `nextAction` parameter of `finishDeliveryBlocked`, `buildBlockedLead`, `describeBlockedGateStep`, the `commitValues` parameter of `describeBlockedWave`, the "left nothing to commit" variant and its per-site copies, the second stop branch of `commitBumpEdit`, the four post-commit result checks, the `result` parameter of `malformedFactsMessage`, and analysis-only's one-block blocked shape. Moved with no growth: `describeUnusableResult`, `nextStepLine` and `maxBlockedLeadLength`, all into `plugin/workflow-src/lib/result.js`. Added: `buildStopBlocks`, the step causes and commit object parameters of `runCommitStep`, the `endLines` parameter of `finishDeliveryBlocked`, and tests. The surface shrinks.
- **Tests.** `tests/workflow-scripts/result.test.mjs` tests `describeUnusableResult`, `nextStepLine` and `buildStopBlocks`, with a blocked finish over a 20,000 character lead that never throws and keeps the Next line whole. `tests/workflow-scripts/standard-delivery.test.mjs` adds compound tests at all four sites (`commit-1-1`, `bump-commit-1-1`, `fix-commit-1-1`, `review-commit-1-1`): a null step then a null commit, a null step then a blocked or malformed commit, and a fix-cause step then a null commit. Each shows every agent-failure sentence once, and the end line shows only when the commit was made. `tests/workflow-scripts/analysis-only.test.mjs` checks the lead and the Next line in every blocked assertion, and adds null and malformed results at the facts, scan, analyze and revise steps. The SD-54 universal null test stays. Deleted: the dedupe assertion that "adds the sentence once", whose check moved to `result.test.mjs`. Rewritten: `facts.test.mjs` cases for `malformedFactsMessage` and `runScanStep`, the wave lead and commit-end texts, the fit test (now over `buildStopBlocks` and `finishDeliveryBlocked`), and every analysis-only blocked assertion.

**Local review finding 49be981e.** Added 2026-10-28. The claim that every blocked end is `buildStopBlocks` over its causes was false. The open-question stop and the plugin-changed stop built their own message, and the plugin-changed stop's Next line told the user to fix a cause when nothing needed a fix. Cluster `blocked-run-end-has-no-single-owner`: findings 58a21e45, 3827ab1a and 49be981e.

- **Triage, per ADR-0038.**
  - Introduced on this branch: the over-broad claim, in the `plugin/workflow-src/lib/result.js` header, the 7.3.2 changelog bullet and the bullets of the previous finding above.
  - Inherited: the open-question stop builder (`finishOpenQuestions` and `nextActionLine`, issues 434 and 453), and the #472 build-skew text. The previous round left the build-skew Next line as a residual because the wording was out of scope. The doctrine does not accept that ground.
  - This is the second structural fix on the same surface that did not close its class, so the gate sets `repeats_surface`. The review forced a zoom-out to the primitive. It is not a security finding.
- **Root cause.** The key of a blocked `finish`. It took a list of blocks, so "one owner" was a habit each caller had to keep and not a property of `finish`. A new stop could bypass the builder. Owning layer: `finish` in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/result.js`, shared by both engines.
- **Fix, closed by changing the key.**
  - `finish({outcome, causes, blocks, pr})` takes causes for a blocked outcome and builds the message itself: `buildStopBlocks(causes)` followed by the caller's later blocks. A caller can no longer hand it a lead or a Next line.
  - It throws `'finish: causes must be given exactly when outcome is blocked'` when a blocked outcome has no causes or a complete outcome has them. A complete outcome keeps its non-empty `blocks`.
  - `nextStepLine('answer')` is "Next: run the request again with the answers in it." The next action `answer` is set only by code. The agent `next_action` enums stay `fix_cause` and `run_again`. `buildStopBlocks` is unchanged and counts `answer` as a cause that leads.
  - The open-question stop is one cause, "The delivery ran no further step, because questions are still open.", with next action `answer`. `finishOpenQuestions` passes it to `finishDeliveryBlocked(causes, run, endLines)`, with the open questions as an end line. The taken decisions of the round still show.
  - The plugin-changed stop is one cause with next action `answer`, returned by `readLaunchInputs` in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/args.js`. analysis-only's `finishBlocked(causes)` is `finish({outcome: 'blocked', causes})`.
- **Class eliminated.** A blocked run end whose lead or Next line a caller assembled, or whose Next line disagrees with its cause, on either engine. Every blocked end passes `finish` its causes, and `finish` picks the Next line only from the lead cause through `nextStepLine`. Every stop is one of three next actions: `fix_cause` (or none), `run_again` and `answer`. Rows SD-20, SD-51, SD-54 and SD-68 in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/contract.js` hold it.
- **Every run end and its owner.** Found by searching `finish(`, `finishDelivery*`, `finishBlocked`, `finishOpenQuestions`, `outcome: 'blocked'` and `result:`. Amended 2026-10-29 (issue 457): the standard-delivery sites listed under `finishDeliveryBlocked` now stop through `stopDelivery`, which pauses unless a cause is terminal or an agent failure, and the bump sites are gone. `finishDeliveryBlocked` has no caller but `stopDelivery`, `finishOpenQuestions` and the launch end of `runDelivery` (row SD-70; see Amendment 2026-10-29: a stop pauses unless nothing can go on, and the version check moves into validation (issue 457)).
  - standard-delivery, blocked through `finishDeliveryBlocked`:
    - main.js: facts, scope, branch and launch, which includes the build skew;
    - relay.js: the branch switched after a replan, and `runCommitStep`, reached from the waves, `commitBumpEdit`, `runFixRound` and the review gate;
    - waves.js: the replan limit and unschedulable steps;
    - review.js: the review replan limit and the review stop;
    - pr.js: PR text too long and the PR relay;
    - gates.js: a bump with no files and validation;
    - plan.js: an unusable plan, a rescope and a resettled question.
  - standard-delivery, blocked through `finishOpenQuestions`, which is a `finishDeliveryBlocked` call: `reply_size` and `exact_text_size`.
  - standard-delivery, complete: `finishDeliveryComplete`, called only from pr.js.
  - standard-delivery pauses are not ends: `pauseDelivery` from gates.js and plan.js, then `pauseForUser`.
  - analysis-only, blocked through `finishBlocked`: no request, launch (including the build skew), facts or scan, and the analyze or revise result.
  - analysis-only, complete through `finishComplete`: `reply_size`, no asks and `pause_limit`. analysis-only pauses through `pauseForUser`.
  - A script throw is not an end the script builds. No other `finish` caller exists in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/`.
- **Surface account.** Removed: `nextActionLine` and its two forms, the open-question stop's own lead and Next blocks (one stop kind instead of two), `finish` accepting caller-built blocks for a blocked outcome, the build-skew stop's trailing "Run the request again with your answers in it." and its wrong fix line, the `nextActionLine` carve-outs in rows SD-20, SD-51 and SD-54 and in the `record.js` and `main.js` headers, and two call-site uses of `blocks: buildStopBlocks(...)`. Added: the `causes` key of `finish` with one throw, the code-only next action `answer` with its line in `nextStepLine`, and tests. No cause field is added. The surface shrinks.
- **Tests.**
  - `tests/workflow-scripts/result.test.mjs`: the blocked cases, the fuzz blocked branch (its oracle puts the stop blocks first) and the fit test go through `causes`. Added: both throws, a blocked finish with no blocks, the stop blocks before the given blocks, the exact `nextStepLine('answer')` text, and a lone `answer` cause leading with the answer line.
  - `tests/workflow-scripts/args.test.mjs`: the build-skew case expects the new text and `{problem, nextAction: 'answer'}`.
  - `tests/workflow-scripts/standard-delivery.test.mjs`: deleted the `nextActionLine` test, since the function is gone, and the const `ON_BRANCH_LINE`. Rewritten: the open-question stop with commits (the lead, the answer line, the "I stopped asking" paragraph, then the branch line later in the message), the test that no `nextStepLine` value mentions a pull request, the fit test over the answer line, the next action enum assertions, and the skew stop. SD-54 now asserts that `nextStepLine('answer')` is the run-again-with-answers line. Unchanged because they have no commits: the open-question stop tests before any wave.
  - `tests/workflow-scripts/analysis-only.test.mjs`: the build-skew assertions expect the new text and `stopResult(SKEW_STOP, 'answer')`.
- **Decided by the user (Q1, option A, 2026-10-28).** One builder. `finish` builds every blocked stop from its causes. The questions-open and plugin-changed stops end "Next: run the request again with the answers in it." With commits, the branch shows on the branch line. The test deletions and rewrites above are approved.
- **Recorded decision.** The open-question stop's Next line does not name the branch. The branch line names it, as on every blocked end with commits. A later finding that asks the Next line to name the branch repeats this decision. Rejected: option B, two stop kinds with a narrowed claim. It leaves `finish` open to caller-built blocks, so the next stop could bypass the builder again, and it leaves the plugin-changed Next line wrong.
- **Residual extended, not new.** At an open-question stop with commits, the branch line comes after the open questions, so a hard cut past the notice budget can drop it. The lead and the Next line stay whole. See the fit residual below.

**Surface account.** Removed: detail blocks, commit lists in chat, the compact question form (`renderQuestionsCompact`), the ask-reason and criticality lines (`askReasonLine`), taken decisions in a pause, the review meta-menu that asked whether to answer the finding or stop, the review fields `open_findings`, `question` and `rationale_text`, and the items removed for 7703cd64 above. Added: one lead line per pause site, the reviewer's `user_question` brief, `describeNoResult`, `describeUnusableResult`, `capItemLines`, `optionLabel`, the `pattern` keyword, bounds on existing fields, a `maxItems` of 4 on options, and two tests, the structure fuzz and the brief-field scan. The surface shrinks. Amended 2026-10-28 (local review findings 3827ab1a and 58a21e45): `describeUnusableResult` now lives in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/result.js`, and that bullet holds the net account of its fix. The surface still shrinks.

**Measured lengths.** Updated 2026-10-28 (local review findings 3827ab1a, 58a21e45 and 49be981e): the "After issue 460" columns hold the values after that fix, and the budgets are unchanged. Each length is the JavaScript string length of the committed built script, measured with node, and each prose count is `countProseCharacters` in `tools/build_workflows.mjs`.

| Script | On main at 279a883 | After issue 460 | Literals after issue 460 | Prose budget |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `standard-delivery.js` | 161,673 | 160,173 | 42,087 | 43,000, down from 45,000 |
| `analysis-only.js` | 73,410 | 73,673 | 18,864 | 19,000, down from 19,500 |

The prose margins are 913 and 136. `standard-delivery.js` stays 29,827 characters under the 190,000 budget of decision 18, which is unchanged. Both prose budgets fall, so each stays within the margin of 1,000 that `tests/workflow-scripts/build.test.mjs` enforces, and the test pins the new values.

**Bounded residuals.**

- **A pause with many questions can still be shortened by the fit.** Each question is bounded and `options` has at most 4 items, but the number of questions is not. Bounded impact: the harness cuts the end of a very long message, and the questions are asked in order. Amended 2026-10-28 (local review findings 3827ab1a and 58a21e45): the same holds for a blocked stop over the notice budget. A later cause can be cut at its tail, while the lead and the Next line stay whole. Only worst-case hostile text can reach it, because the lead is compact at 1,000 code points at most. Amended 2026-10-28 (local review finding 49be981e): it also covers the branch line of an open-question stop with commits. That line comes after the open questions, so a hard cut past the notice budget can drop it, while the lead and the Next line stay whole. Bounded impact: display only, worst case, and the harness limit is fixed.
- **Inline Markdown stays.** Markdown inside one line of planner prose still renders, as Risks residual (b) now says. Bounded impact: display only, one line, after code-owned text.
- **Reviewer text still shows JSON-quoted.** The reviewer's `found`, `ask` and option summaries are external content, so `encodeUntrustedText` shows each in JSON quotes. Bounded impact: the text reads whole and short, and the quotes mark it as external content, as invariant 4 asks.
- **A deterministic agent API error reads as an agent failure.** `agent()` returns `null` for any API error, so the script cannot tell an expired login from a 400 on a bad schema. Such an error also gets the `run_again` Next line, and a rerun repeats it. Bounded impact: the schemas the scripts send are checked in the repo by tests, and `toAgentSchema` removes the top-level `anyOf` the harness record shows the API refuses.
- **A planner's option order can differ from its own ids.** Letters come from position, so a planner that orders options oddly sees letters that differ from its ids. Bounded impact: the revise step binds the reply through the text shown, as it does for Q labels.
- **An encoded review question can exceed the brief `problem` limit of 300.** `decide()` then asks it as `malformed_brief`, and review kinds ask anyway. Bounded impact: the user is asked, as before. Closed 2026-10-29 by issue 462: `decide()` now reads a brief built in code from the reviewer's option ids, recommendation, strength and gate, with fixed texts only, so a valid review never asks as `malformed_brief`. The shown question keeps the encoded reviewer text (see Amendment 2026-10-29: agreed review calls are taken (issue 462)).
- **The build-skew Next line is the fix line on both engines.** The #472 stop for a resume across builds still says "fix what stopped the run". Amended 2026-10-28 (local review findings 3827ab1a and 58a21e45): both engines now build it through `buildStopBlocks`, so analysis-only's build-skew and missing-request stops gain that Next line, which they lacked. Bounded impact: a small mismatch on a rare path, and the Next line wording is out of scope here. Closed 2026-10-28 (local review finding 49be981e): the build-skew stop is one cause with next action `answer` and ends "Next: run the request again with the answers in it." on both engines, so its Next line no longer says to fix a cause.
- **A run resumed across builds stops once.** A run paused on 7.3.1 and resumed on 7.3.2 carries a different `BUILD_ID`, so it stops before any agent call and asks to run the request again (Amendment 2026-10-28: answers kept across replans, and a build check on resume (issue 472)). This replaces the earlier residual about reruns across versions. Bounded impact: intended, because question keys change, and no agent runs on the stale answers.

It updates these places in place, each with a dated note:

- Decision 17: the local review questions.
- Amendment 2026-10-23 on open questions ending blocked (issue 434): the bullet on the open-question stop and its Next line (finding 49be981e).
- Amendment 2026-10-25 on open questions pausing (issue 453): the bullet on every open question pausing (finding 49be981e).
- Amendment 2026-10-23 on question keys: the bullet on irreversible effects (finding 7703cd64).
- Amendment 2026-10-24 on short run messages: the bullet on the local review open findings.

## Amendment 2026-10-29: agreed review calls are taken (issue 462)

This amendment covers https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/462. A local review question paused every run, even when the reviewer's recommendation was one the run agreed with, the pick could be undone, and it changed nothing about how the system is built. The user had to answer calls the run could take and list. This change lets the run take such a call, record it and list it, and keeps every other review question a pause.

**First-finding triage, per ADR-0038.**

- **Introduced or inherited.** Inherited from main. Every review call was surfaced at every level: the `review_question` and `high_severity_rejection` rows of `plugin/references/decision-autonomy-table.json` were `surface` in all three cells. The script hard-wired the ask: `askReviewQuestion` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/review.js` read `resolution.asks[0]` and had no take branch. It also built the review brief with every gate flag set to false, so the brief said nothing true about the call, and it gave `decide()` encoded reviewer text that could fail `BRIEF_SCHEMA`.
- **Root class and owning layer.** Who decides a reviewer's question had no home. The owning layer is the new `### Review Calls` rule in `plugin/governance/decision-autonomy.md`, and on the script engine the two table rows, resolved through `decide()`. Nothing is added per site.
- **The SD-29 hole.** Row SD-29 checks that every `decide()` kind `main.js` uses is a hard stop or a table row whose `resolved_at` names a standard-delivery site. It never saw the two review kinds, because they were string literals in `reviewQuestionKinds()` and not `*_KIND` consts, and neither row named a standard-delivery site. The kinds are now the consts `REVIEW_QUESTION_KIND` and `HIGH_SEVERITY_REJECTION_KIND`, both rows name `standard-delivery.local_review`, and SD-29 names them.
- **Removed instead of hardened.** Removed: the always-surface cells at high and balanced, the gate code that fixed every flag to false, the hard-wired ask, the `malformed_brief` path for encoded reviewer text, and the residual that said review kinds ask anyway. `decide()` now reads a brief built in code by `removeReviewerText` from the reviewer's structured choices: the option ids, the recommendation, its strength and the gate. Every text in it is a fixed text, so a valid review never asks as `malformed_brief`.
- **Threat model.** Not in question here. Recording a threat model, when a reviewer raises it, is now a review call like any other (Q2 A below).

**Decided by the user (2026-10-28, reply "Take recs").**

- **Q1, option A.** The run's agreement with the reviewer is the two table rows resolved through `decide()`. No second agent judges the call, and no new stop kind is added.
- **Q2, option A.** Recording a threat model, when a reviewer raises it, is a review call, asked or taken per `### Review Calls` like any other. ADR-0038 carries a dated note.
- **Issue 462 (2026-10-27).** Safety relevance alone does not make an agreed, reversible call ask. The issue narrows "surface safety-relevant calls" to "surface irreversible calls, and safety-relevant calls the agents do not agree on".

**What is taken.** A local review `user-input-required` or `high-severity-rejection` call is taken at the high and balanced levels when the reviewer's recommended option is reversible and not architectural. The reviewer writes the call: 2 to 4 options, a recommendation with an honest strength, and the new required `gate` of `user_question`, the booleans `irreversible` and `architectural`, its read of clauses 1 and 2 of the Promotion Gate on the recommended option. A surface-growth call, and any pick that changes a public contract or governance-doc semantics, reads architectural. Clause 3 is not read, and the brief's `safety` is false, because the user decided so: issue 462 narrows the rule "surface safety-relevant calls" to "surface irreversible calls, and safety-relevant calls the agents do not agree on". A pick that keeps or records a safety finding unfixed is taken like any other agreed, reversible, non-architectural call. The Destructive Fix Gate (Tier A) still stops a pick that applies a fix in its categories. A take never pauses and never touches `run.answerLog`. `takeReviewCall` lists the call in `run.taken` and reruns the review gate on the same head with the next `gateLabel` count and `user_answer {question, reply, decided_by: 'run'}`, the reply being the recommended option id. The reviewer applies it as it applies a user reply, and records the decision at its source naming the run, not the user, as the one who decided. A taken call starts no planning pass.

**What still asks.**

- an irreversible call;
- an architectural call, surface growth included;
- a call whose recommendation has strength `none`, which the reviewer sets when only the user holds the fact the call needs;
- every call at the low level, whose cell for both rows is `surface`;
- the Tier A hard stops, which are unchanged;
- on the workflow state machine, the `question` user gate, which still asks every question, the post-PR GitHub review questions included.

An ask pauses as before, with the lead, the reviewer's own question and its recommendation (Amendment 2026-10-28: one message shape (issue 460)).

**How a taken call is listed.** Three places show it, so the user can veto it before the merge.

- **The pull request.** The body and the reuse comment list the call with the fixed texts of `reviewCallTexts()`: a problem, an option summary and a why, with no finding id and no reviewer text. The no-reviewer-text boundary of row SD-45 holds.
- **The diff.** The reviewer's record of the decision at its source, or its fix commit, carries the full text the user needs to veto the call.
- **The chat.** The final message lists the call on one line, by its short finding id with the encoded ask and the encoded summary of the chosen option.

**Surface account.** Removed: the `surface` cells at high and balanced for the two rows, the all-false gate of `buildReviewQuestionBrief`, the hard-wired `resolution.asks[0]` ask, the `malformed_brief` path for a valid review, and the residual named above. Added: the two-boolean `gate` in `REVIEW_SCHEMA`'s `user_question`, `decided_by` in the rerun's `user_answer`, the two kind consts, the fixed texts of `reviewCallTexts()`, and the helpers `removeReviewerText`, `takeReviewCall` and `describeReviewAnswerData`. No new agent, stop kind, state or workflow definition version is added.

**Measured lengths.** The prose budgets did not move. Each length is the JavaScript string length of the committed built script, measured with node, and each prose count is `countProseCharacters` in `tools/build_workflows.mjs`.

| Script | Length | Literals | Prose budget |
|---|---|---|---|
| `standard-delivery.js` | 162,415 | 42,708 | 43,000, unchanged |
| `analysis-only.js` | 73,661 | 18,852 | 19,000, unchanged |

The prose margins are 292 and 148. `analysis-only.js` changes only in the embedded autonomy table and its build id. `standard-delivery.js` stays 27,585 characters under the 190,000 budget of decision 18.

**Texts and tests changed.**

- STEP-001, the prose layer: `plugin/governance/decision-autonomy.md` gains `### Review Calls`; `plugin/agents/local-reviewer.md` gains `user_answer.decided_by`, `user_question.gate`, the strength `none` rule, the record naming the run in step 1, and step 5's pointer to the rule; `plugin/governance/remediation-doctrine.md` routes question 4 to the rule, says a taken call is recorded the same way in `## Recorded Decisions and Repeat Findings`, and updates its consumer list. Policy fixtures: `tests/policy/safety-review-calls.json` (new) pins the section and its consumers, and pin (5) of `tests/policy/safety-first-finding-triage.json` follows question 4.
- STEP-002, the script: the two rows of `plugin/references/decision-autonomy-table.json`; `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/review.js` (`buildUserQuestionSchema`, `buildReviewOption`, `buildReviewQuestionBrief`, `reviewCallTexts`, `removeReviewerText`, `takeReviewCall`, `askReviewQuestion`, `describeReviewAnswerData` and `buildReviewPrompt`); the kind consts and header in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/main.js`; `toPullRequestDecision` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/pr.js`; rows SD-07, SD-29, SD-32, SD-33, SD-37, SD-45 and SD-57 in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/contract.js`. The generated `plugin/workflows/standard-delivery.js` and `plugin/workflows/analysis-only.js` follow. Tests: `tests/workflow-scripts/data.test.mjs` checks both rows, and `tests/workflow-scripts/standard-delivery.test.mjs` adds the take, the three ask reasons, the surface cell, a missing gate, the answer log, a resume of a taken call and the code-built brief, and gives the existing review-question pause tests a gate that asks.

**Local review finding 63d87f94.** Added 2026-10-28. Full id `63d87f94c0553672cae3e83f6d901296c7a1b88448e002352f782f14163846e4`, file `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/review.js`.

- **The claim.** A taken review call never reads the safety clause, so the run can take a pick that keeps or records a safety finding unfixed without asking. The finding calls the review-call gate a narrowed copy of the Promotion Gate.
- **Root cause.** Introduced on this branch. The reason this amendment and `### Review Calls` gave for not reading clause 3 was false. They named the Destructive Fix Gate, which stops only a fix. For a pick that keeps or records a safety finding unfixed, that reason does not hold, so the narrowing read as an unrecorded copy of the Promotion Gate.
- **Decision.** Rejected under the user's decision. Issue 462 narrows "surface safety-relevant calls" to "surface irreversible calls, and safety-relevant calls the agents do not agree on". The request says "Surface only architectural calls". Q2 A is settled. Adding a safety boolean, a third read in the reviewer's gate, is rejected: it grows the surface and reverses the user's decision on the case behind the issue.
- **Correction.** `### Review Calls` in `plugin/governance/decision-autonomy.md` now gives the user's decision as the reason, and `## Promotion Gate` points to the narrowing. **What is taken** above says the same.
- **Bounded impact.** A taken call is one the reviewer recommends and the run agrees with. It is reversible and not architectural. It is listed in chat and in the pull request. The reviewer's record shows in the diff for a veto before the merge. The low level's cell is `surface`, so every call asks there. The Destructive Fix Gate still stops any fix in its categories.
- **Key.** A later finding that contests this narrowing is a repeat, rejected by citing this record, per `## Recorded Decisions and Repeat Findings` in `plugin/governance/remediation-doctrine.md`. A finding that shows a failure this record does not cover, such as an irreversible or architectural pick being taken, is new.

**Bounded residual.** The gate is the reviewer's read. A wrong read of `irreversible` or `architectural` takes a call that should ask. Bounded impact: the call is only one the reviewer itself recommends, it is listed in chat and in the pull request, the reviewer's record shows in the diff before the merge, and the Destructive Fix Gate still stops any fix in its categories. A pick that leaves a safety finding unfixed is taken by the user's decision, and the Destructive Fix Gate does not cover it. A second agent checking the read is rejected under Q1.

It updates these places in place, each with a dated note:

- Decision 17: the local review questions.
- Amendment 2026-10-28 on one message shape (issue 460): the residual on an encoded review question and the brief `problem` limit, closed.

The rest of ADR-0037 is unchanged.

Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/462, `plugin/governance/decision-autonomy.md` (`### Review Calls`), `docs/adr/0038-root-cause-at-the-first-finding.md` (triage and question 4).

## Amendment 2026-10-29: a stop pauses unless nothing can go on, and the version check moves into validation (issue 457)

This amendment covers https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/457. A delivery run ended blocked at stops where a reply could let it go on. A bump step that ran again after a replan also ended a run whose version was already set. This change gives every stop one path, which pauses unless nothing can go on, and it removes the bump gate.

**First-finding triage, per ADR-0038.**

- **Introduced or inherited.** Inherited from main.
- **Root class.** A step that has a way forward ends the run. It lives in two layers:
  - The run-end layer. Each call site picked `finishDeliveryBlocked`, so the call site decided whether to end or to ask, not whether a way forward existed.
  - The bump gate. One agent pair ran again after every replan or head move. One drone both relayed `hivemind:bump-type` and edited files, so its note leaked into the relay as malformed routing lines, and the gate was entered again with nothing to do.
- **Removed instead of hardened.**
  - The bump gate is gone. The version edit moves into the plan step that writes the change log. The check moves into the validate drone, which runs anyway, and it is deterministic in `bump-type.sh`. A version that is already set passes by construction.
  - `stopDelivery` is the only caller of `finishDeliveryBlocked`, apart from `finishOpenQuestions` and the launch end of `runDelivery`. A test pins that, so a new stop site pauses unless it marks itself terminal.
- **Threat model.** The terminal allowlist below keeps every safety stop, and every pause needs a user reply.

**Decided by the user (2026-10-29).**

- **Bump design D.** Neither a bump split nor new relay fields. A plan step sets the version beside the change log, and validation checks it read-only. A version question never plans again. This replaces the bump split of the earlier plan (its Q1, option A).
- **Q1 of this plan, option A, with one retry rule.** Malformed agent output pauses with a `step_stop` question, and the reply takes the step again. A runner relay runs once more in place only when it is blocked and its failure is transient per `plugin/governance/definitions.md` (Transient Failure). Malformed output, a block that is not transient and a null result are never retried.
- **Tests.** The test deletions and assertion rewrites the plan listed are approved.

**One stop path.** `stopDelivery(causes, run, endLines, isReplan)` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/record.js` takes every stop. It ends the run through `finishDeliveryBlocked` when any cause is marked `terminal` or `agentFailure`. Otherwise it asks one undecided question of the new kind `step_stop` through `askTail`. Its lead is the cause problems, then the end lines. Its brief is built in code by `buildStepStopBrief`: fix what stopped the run, then reply, or reply when ready when nothing in the repo needs a fix. The `step_stop` row of `plugin/references/decision-autonomy-table.json` is `surface` at every level. A reply when the cause is still not fixed pauses again, with no cap, because each reply is a user action. Row SD-70.

- A facts, scan, scope, plan, branch or wave stop plans again from fresh facts on the reply. That pass does not count against `maxReplans()`.
- A validation, fix round or local review `blocked` stop, and a pull request stop, take the step again. A commit stop follows its step. Amended 2026-10-29 (PR 477 review): the step is taken again only after one head check (see the PR 477 review section below). Amended 2026-10-29 (PR 477 review, round 2): a local review stop reply runs no head check. Local review runs again, and its commit runner reads the head.
- `max-iterations-reached` and `break-fix-break` plan again with the review carried.
- The worker replan limit and the review replan limit ask a `step_stop` question, and the reply starts a new pass that is not counted.
- A pull request rerun is labeled `pr-P-N`, with N from 2. Amended 2026-10-29 (PR 477 review): each reply to a gate or pull request stop also runs a head check, labeled `head-P-N`, before the step is taken again. Amended 2026-10-29 (PR 477 review, round 2): the head check runs before validation or the pull request step whenever a reply left the head unread, whatever the reply answered, and never before local review.

**What still ends the run.** This is the terminal allowlist:

- the scan's injection suspect, and the local review's `injection-suspect`;
- a commit script refusal, and a commit step that reports a branch other than the plan branch. Amended 2026-10-29 (PR 477 review): the head check is a commit step, so its refusal and its branch check end the run too;
- a `create-working-branch` refusal, a branch step that reports a branch other than the plan branch, and a second branch switch after a replan;
- a pull request text over its size limit, and a `head_ref_oid` other than the commit every gate held on;
- the workflow-defect guard: a settled question that comes back;
- an agent failure: a null `agent()` result, which `describeUnusableResult` alone marks `agentFailure`, and a blocked wave or fix round that holds one;
- the size limits `reply_size` and `exact_text_size`, through `finishOpenQuestions`;
- the launch check, a direct end in `runDelivery`.

**One retry of a transient runner block.** `isTransientFailure(text)` in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/result.js` is true only when the text names a root cause that `plugin/governance/definitions.md` (Transient Failure) lists. The facts, branch, commit and pull request runners call `agent()` through `runRunnerRelay`. A relay that fits its schema, is blocked and has transient stderr runs the same prompt once more under the label `<label>-retry`. A step call retries at most once. Row SD-71.

**The version check in validation.**

- `hivemind:bump-type` gains a repeatable, read-only `--version-file`. It emits `version_check` (`match`, `mismatch`, `unreadable` or `undetermined`), `trunk_version` and `expected_version`. With no such flag, its output is byte-identical to before.
- It reads each file by path. A `*.json` file gives its top-level string `version`, read with jq. A `*.toml` file gives its one `version = "X.Y.Z"` line. Any other file is one version line. A version must be a strict SemVer core. Anything else is `unreadable`, and so are files whose trunk versions differ. The check reads the commit with `git show`, since a gate holds on a commit.
- The planner sets each version file in a plan step, the one that writes the change log when the plan has one. The target version is not a plan field: the step's outcome names it, and the check stays the authority. Text: `plugin/references/plan-schema.json`, `plugin/agents/cerebrate.md`.
- When the plan names version files, the validate drone also runs bump-type once, with the facts step's `trunk_commit` as base, and relays it. Text: `plugin/governance/definitions.md` (`## Validation Procedure`).
- A match holds the gate, and its note names the version.
- A mismatch is a failure line built in code. A version-only failure runs a fix round with no `decide()` call, counted toward `maxFixRounds()`. At the limit it pauses, and the reply resets the round count and runs validation again.
- `undetermined` or `unreadable` asks `version_bump_indeterminable`. The reply becomes `run.versionAnswer` and runs one fix round, outside `decide()` and the round limit.
- No version path plans again. Rows SD-21 (now the version check) and SD-22 (now the version question), SD-25 and SD-26.

**Recorded decisions, each with its repeat scope.** A later finding or plan that asks for the opposite of one of these is a repeat, rejected by citing this record per `plugin/governance/remediation-doctrine.md` (Recorded Decisions and Repeat Findings).

- **Agent failures end the run.** A null `agent()` result ends the run as before. Reason: whether a resumed run reruns a failed call is unverified, and S11-e covers completed agents only. If a resume did not rerun it, a pending reply could answer a question the user never saw. A worker's own `run_again` pauses, because its result is held and cached. Repeat scope: a finding that asks a null result to pause, until a check shows that a resume reruns a failed call.
- **Size limits end the run.** `reply_size` and `exact_text_size` can build no pause. Keeping the replies anywhere else would be the second run record that decision 2 rules out. Repeat scope: a finding that asks either size stop to pause.
- **analysis-only and the state machine are unchanged.** analysis-only writes nothing before it stops, and slice 3 retires the state machine's `pr-feedback-remediation` path. Repeat scope: a finding that asks those stops to pause in this change.
- **No version file means no version check.** With no file named there is nothing to set, and the only possible reply action would be a replan, which design D rules out. So there is no bump-type run and no version question. Repeat scope: a finding that asks for a check or a question with no version files.
- **A version answer holds for the run.** Once the user answers a version question, no later version check in the run fails or asks. Repeat scope: a finding that asks a later check to verify the answered version.
- **A version-only failure at the fix limit pauses.** It pauses through `stopDelivery`, and the reply resets the round count and runs validation again. It never asks the fix limit question, whose reply plans again. Repeat scope: a finding that asks it to end the run or to plan again.

**Eliminated class.** A stop that has a way forward ends the run. Every stop now goes through `stopDelivery`, and only a cause marked terminal or an agent failure ends the run there. The size limits and the launch check are the named exceptions. Row SD-70's exclusivity test reads the built script and fails when `finishDeliveryBlocked` is called anywhere but `stopDelivery`, `finishOpenQuestions` and the launch end of `runDelivery`. So a new stop site pauses unless it marks itself terminal. The bump gate's reruns are gone with the gate: a version already at its target passes the check, so a replan or a head move costs no agent and no stop. That is the live case in the issue 457 comment.

**Issue 447.** The bump-step relay case of https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/447 is gone with the bump gate. The `open-plan-pr` stdout leak stays with issue 447.

**Surface account.** Removed: the bump gate and its agents, with `runBumpGate`, `runBumpStep`, `commitBumpEdit`, `buildBumpPrompt`, `buildBumpTypePrompt`, `checkBumpTypeRelay`, `resolveBumpVerdict`, `describeBumpNote`, `keepBumpHeld`, `isKeptRunnerCommit`, `isSingleRowBump`, `buildBumpSchema`, `buildBumpTypeBrief` and `buildBumpIndeterminableBrief`; `BUMP_SCHEMA`, `BUMP_MODEL` and `VERSION_BUMP_TYPE_KIND`; `run.runnerCommits` and the gate state `bumpMark`, `bumpValues` and `runs.bump`; the Bump phase; rows SD-24 and SD-65; the `standard-delivery.bump` sites of the autonomy table; and the choice at each call site between ending and asking. Added: `stopDelivery`, the `agentFailure` mark, the `step_stop` kind and row, `buildStepStopBrief`, `isTransientFailure`, `runRunnerRelay`, the bump-type `--version-file` option and its three keys, `versionCheckRoutingSpec`, `isConsistentVersionRelay`, `run.versionAnswer`, the version question brief, and rows SD-70 and SD-71. No new agent, state or workflow definition version is added. Amended 2026-10-29 (PR 477 review): see the surface account of that section.

**Measured lengths.** Each length is the JavaScript string length of the built script, measured with node, and each prose count is `countProseCharacters` in `tools/build_workflows.mjs`.

| Script | On main at 60f8e3e | After issue 457 | Literals after issue 457 | Prose budget |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `standard-delivery.js` | 162,415 | 167,249 | 42,980 | 43,800, up from 43,000 |
| `analysis-only.js` | 73,661 | 74,539 | 18,900 | 19,000, unchanged |

The prose margins are 820 and 100. `standard-delivery.js` stays 22,751 characters under the 190,000 budget of decision 18. Its prose budget rises by 800, a reviewed change within the margin `tests/workflow-scripts/build.test.mjs` enforces. `analysis-only.js` grows by the shared `plugin/workflow-src/lib/result.js` changes and its embedded autonomy table.

Amended 2026-10-29 (PR 477 review): after the head fix, `standard-delivery.js` is 167,943 characters with 43,186 of prose, which is 22,057 under the 190,000 budget and 614 under the prose budget of 43,800. `analysis-only.js` is unchanged at 74,539 and 18,900. The prose budget is not raised. Amended 2026-10-29 (PR 477 review, round 2): after round 2, `standard-delivery.js` is 167,383 characters with 43,088 of prose, which is 22,617 under the 190,000 budget and 712 under the prose budget. `analysis-only.js` is unchanged.

**Bounded residual.** Paused stop leads still use wording such as "... so the delivery stopped" while the run pauses. Bounded impact: the pause carries the reply line and the `step_stop` question, which say what a reply does.

It updates these places in place, each with a dated note:

- Workflow outline: step 5.
- Invariant 2: the gate list.
- The hand-off rows "Commits to bump-type" and "Bump to validation".
- Decision 12: the bump step's commit.
- Decision 26: superseded.
- Consequences: the fixed agent calls per run.
- Recorded residuals: a trunk bump that lands after the facts step.
- Amendment 2026-10-25 on open questions (issue 453): the bullet on answers after the last wave, the residuals "Tail asks at non-default autonomy levels plan again" (version questions are excepted) and "Two stops still end blocked", and the "Out of scope" line.
- Amendment 2026-10-27 on workflow cost (issue 464): the bullets on the bump collapse and its keep-held key, and on the edit before `decide()`.
- Amendment 2026-10-28 on one message shape (issue 460): the list of every run end and its owner.

The rest of ADR-0037 is unchanged.

### Review of PR 477: every resume reads the head (2026-10-29)

This section covers the Codex P1 finding on https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/pull/477 (https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/pull/477#discussion_r4413750725). After a fix-commit relay the run could not read, `run.head` stayed at the old commit. The kept validation failure was dropped, validation held on the old head, and with local review off the pull request step sent `expect_commit` set to the old head. `open-plan-pr.sh` refused it, the step paused, and every reply met the same refusal. The run could never finish.

**First-finding triage, per ADR-0038.**

- **Introduced or inherited.** Introduced by this branch. On main, a validation, fix round or pull request stop ended the run, so no reply could resume a gate. The commits that made those stops pause created the path.
- **Root class.** `run.head` was a belief the run learned only from a valid commit relay. A paused stop hands control to the user, or follows a commit relay the run could not read, and the head can move during either. The reply then took a step that has no commit runner of its own, validation or the pull request, and that step held a gate on, or pushed, a head the run never read.
- **Removed instead of hardened.** Nothing could be removed. Planning again on every gate or pull request reply fails because the plan schema needs at least one step, and nothing is left to plan after the last wave. It would also break the recorded decision that a version-only fix-limit reply never plans again. Making those stops terminal again would reverse the settled answer that malformed output pauses. So the fix adds one observation point at the resume, in place of a patch per stop site.
- **Threat model.** In scope. The `step_stop` brief tells the user to fix what stopped the run and reply, so a commit made during a pause is expected use. The second trigger, a commit runner whose relay cannot be read, needs no user action.

**Reply audit.** Every reply to a stop, and every other resume, was checked.

- Unsafe before the fix: a validation or version relay problem, a version-only fix limit, a fix round step or commit relay problem, and a pull request stop. Each took its step again on the old `run.head`.
- Safe before the fix: review stops that run the review again, because its commit runner reads the head, and every reply that plans again, because each new pass runs a wave that ends with the commit runner. One gap remained there. When that wave commit was `committed: true`, the run recorded only the new commit and assumed its parent was `run.head`, so a commit made by a runner whose relay was unreadable fell out of the commit list. The impact was display only, the "Commits this run made" list.

**Decided by the user (2026-10-29).**

- **Q1, option A.** Read the branch head after every reply to a gate or pull request stop, and at the start of each new pass. Accept the small growth of the surface. Option B, which reruns only a commit step whose result was unreadable, was ruled out because it leaves replies stuck after a commit made during a pause.
- **Q2, option A.** The listed test rewrites are approved, and further rewrites of the same kind.

**Eliminated class.** A step acts on a branch head the run did not read after a pause or an unreadable commit relay. It is now impossible for two reasons:

- `run.head`, `run.branch` and `run.commits` have one writer, `observeHead` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/record.js`. It is fed only the commit of a checked molt relay or a checked branch relay. A structural test on the built script pins that no other code writes them. Row SD-72. Amended 2026-10-29 (PR 477 review, round 2): `run.commits` is gone, and `observeHead` writes `run.head`, `run.branch` and `run.headRound`.
- The only two ways to resume each call it before any gate or push reads `run.head`. A new pass reads the head from its branch step in `runDeliveryPass`, after `needsBranchReplan` and before any wave. A step taken again reads it through `runHeadCheck` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/gates.js`. Row SD-73. Amended 2026-10-29 (PR 477 review, round 2): superseded. Two other reply routes skipped the read. The read now keys on the reply, not on the route: the head is unread while `run.headRound` is behind `run.answerLog.length`, and the gate loop reads it before validation or the pull request step (see round 2 below).

This changes where the head comes from. It adds no list of handled cases.

**The head check.** `stopDelivery` returns `{rerun: true}` to the gate loop for a reply that does not plan again. This replaces `{held: false}` from `stopDelivery`, which had two meanings. `runHeadCheck` then runs `runCommitStep` with an empty file list, `stepName` `head` and the label `head-P-N`, so molt reports the head of the working branch with `committed: false`. It is run again while it returns `{rerun: true}`. Then every kept validation failure is cleared. When the head moved, the gates that held on the old head go through the gate rerun of SD-27. Otherwise the same step, a gate or the pull request, runs again. The pull request step is now one call inside the gate loop, with the label `pr-P-N` counted in `gates.runs.pr`, and its internal rerun loop is gone. Amended 2026-10-29 (PR 477 review, round 2): superseded in part. There is no inner loop: `runHeadCheck` is one call, and the gate loop runs `head-P-(N+1)` after a reply to its stop because the stamp is still behind. It clears the kept failures before its commit run, with the same result. `{rerun: true}` no longer triggers the head check; an unread head does (see round 2 below).

**Deviation from the plan.** The head check counter is `gates.runs.headCheck`, not `gates.runs.head`. The SD-72 structural pin forbids any `.head =` or `.head +=` write outside `observeHead`, and a counter named `runs.head` would trip it.

**Recorded decisions, each with its repeat scope.**

- **One head check runner per reply to a gate or pull request stop.** The runner is a haiku molt run with an empty file list, the way local review already reads the head. `hivemind:gather-facts` was too heavy. The rule is the same for a reply to a local review stop, which costs one more runner than before, so that it stays uniform and closed. A run that never pauses at a gate or pull request stop gets no added agent call. Repeat scope: a finding that asks to skip the head check on some replies, or to read the head by another means for one stop. Amended 2026-10-29 (PR 477 review, round 2): superseded. The head check runs before validation or the pull request step whenever the head is unread after a reply, and a local review stop reply runs none, because the review's commit runner reads the head.
- **A refusal at the head check ends the run.** A dirty tree, trunk safety, and a branch other than the plan branch are commit script refusals, which stay on the terminal allowlist. Issue 457 lists commit script refusals as true blocks. To make a refusal unlikely, the `step_stop` brief says to reply with every change committed. Repeat scope: a finding that asks a head check refusal to pause.
- **A reply clears every kept validation failure.** The user may have fixed whatever the failure was about, and a failure kept for an old head must not drive a fix decision on a new one. Repeat scope: a finding that asks a reply to keep a failure.
- **A commit made during a pause enters the commit list as a range.** It is recorded as `<old>..<new>`, the way local review fix commits already are, through `observeHead`. Repeat scope: a finding that asks for its commits to be listed one by one. Bounded impact of the commit list: informational only, since the commit list of the pull request on GitHub is authoritative. Amended 2026-10-29 (PR 477 review, round 2): superseded. The run keeps no commit list, so there is no range to record.
- **`run.head` has one writer.** Repeat scope: a finding that asks another site to write `run.head`, `run.branch` or `run.commits`. Amended 2026-10-29 (PR 477 review, round 2): `run.commits` is gone, and the one writer also writes `run.headRound`. The repeat scope now covers `run.head`, `run.branch` and `run.headRound`.

**Surface account.** Added: `observeHead`, one call to it at the start of each pass, `runHeadCheck` and the `commitStepTexts` case `head`, the `{rerun: true}` reply marker, the counters `gates.runs.headCheck` and `gates.runs.pr`, one clause in the `step_stop` brief, and rows SD-72 and SD-73. Removed: the kept-failure delete in `runFixRound` and in `stopVersionFixLimit`, the pull request step's internal rerun loop, the two meanings of `{held: false}`, and the unbounded pause loop after a commit made during a pull request or validation pause.
