The net is a slight growth, mainly one runner kind and one counter. The user accepted it (Q1). Amended 2026-10-29 (PR 477 review, round 2): round 2 removes `run.commits` and its part, `runResumedStep`, the head check's inner loop and the head check on local review stop replies, and adds `run.headRound`. Its net shrinks (see round 2 below).

**Measured lengths.** After this change `standard-delivery.js` is 167,943 characters with 43,186 of prose, under the budgets of 190,000 and 43,800. The budgets do not change. The table above has the full figures. Amended 2026-10-29 (PR 477 review, round 2): after round 2 it is 167,383 characters with 43,088 of prose (see round 2 below).

**Bounded residual.** The head check is retried once on a transient block, like any commit runner. A malformed head check relay pauses, and its reply runs `head-P-(N+1)`. Bounded impact: the run never acts on a head it did not read, and each pause needs a user reply.

It updates these places in place, each with a dated note: the bullet on validation, fix round and local review blocked stops and pull request stops; the `pr-P-N` bullet; the terminal allowlist's commit refusal line; the surface account; and the table of measured lengths.

Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/pull/477#discussion_r4413750725, `docs/adr/0038-root-cause-at-the-first-finding.md` (triage).

### Review of PR 477, round 2: the head read keys on the reply (2026-10-29)

This section covers round 2 of the review of https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/pull/477, with local review finding e3a520d9. Round 1 read the head on one route only: a reply that came back as `{rerun: true}` from `stopDelivery`. Round 2 found two more routes that skipped that read, the reply to a version question and the reply to a local review question. It was the second finding on the same surface (same-surface recurrence, N=2), so the remediation was a forced zoom-out to the approach. Adding the two routes to the list would have been the same fix again, so the fix changes the primitive instead.

**First-finding triage, per ADR-0038.**

- **Introduced or inherited.** Introduced by this branch: the stop and resume surface, and round 1's `observeHead` and `runHeadCheck` (756f3ad, 4055331).
- **Root class.** The run kept two beliefs about the branch, `run.head` and `run.commits`. It refreshed them by route: a commit relay, the branch relay at the start of a pass, or a head check that only `{rerun: true}` triggered. A reply on any other route came back into the gate loop with both beliefs as they were before the user had control. On the version and review routes a commit runner happened to read `run.head` again. `run.commits` could not be fixed after the fact: when the version reply's fix commit was `committed: true`, a commit the user made during the pause dropped out of the list. The owning layer is the run state and the gate loop: `record.js`, `gates.js`, `relay.js` and `main.js` in `plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/`.
- **Removed instead of hardened.** `run.commits` and the "Commits this run made" part are removed. The branch and the pull request on GitHub are the record. Decision 2 rules out a second record because it can go stale, and this one was going stale. `runResumedStep` and the inner loop of `runHeadCheck` are removed too. The head read itself stays. Validation has no commit runner and holds on `run.head`, and the pull request step sends `expect_commit` set to `run.head`. Both need a head read after any reply, or a commit made during the pause leaves a stale hold and a refused push.
- **Threat model.** In scope, as round 1 recorded. The `step_stop` brief invites the user to commit during a pause.

**The key.** `observeHead` stamps `run.headRound` with `run.answerLog.length` each time it writes the head. Every reply adds one entry to `run.answerLog`, its round. So the head is unread exactly when `run.headRound` is behind `run.answerLog.length`, whatever route the reply took. Each pass of the loop in `runGates` (`plugin/workflow-src/standard-delivery/gates.js`) does these things in order:

- When the head moved, it runs the gate rerun of SD-27 and starts the gate list again. This block moved from the bottom of the loop to the top.
- It skips a gate already held on the head.
- Before validation or the pull request step, when the head is unread, it runs one head check and goes round again.
- It runs the step.
- It records a hold only when the head is read.

`runHeadCheck` is one call. It counts `gates.runs.headCheck`, clears the kept validation failures before its commit run, and returns the `runCommitStep` result as it is. A reply to the head check's own stop leaves the stamp behind, so the loop runs `head-P-(N+1)`. Local review gets no head check: its commit runner is the same empty-list molt run, and it reads the head before any hold. Row SD-73.

**Reply audit.** Every reply route under the new key.

| Route | Before | After |
|---|---|---|
| Validation, fix round or version-limit stop reply | head check, then the step | unchanged |
| Pull request stop reply | head check, then the step | unchanged |
| Local review stop reply (blocked, malformed, review commit stop) | head check, then local review | local review runs, and its commit runner reads the head. One fewer runner. |
| Local review question reply, head unmoved | local review runs again | unchanged |
| Local review question reply after the review's own fix commits moved the head | validation ran on an unread head, held on a commit it did not run on, then ran again after a later read | head check, then validation. One more haiku runner, one fewer duplicate validation. |
| Version question reply | fix round, and the fix commit reads the head. The user's pause commit dropped from the list. | fix round, and the fix commit reads the head. No list exists. |
| Every reply that plans again | the new pass reads the head from its branch step | unchanged |

The flow of a local review question reply after the move is `head-1-1`, `validate-1-2`, `review-1-2`, `review-commit-1-2`, `pr-1`.

**Eliminated class.** No gate holds on, no push sends, and no run record lists, branch state the run has not read since the user's last reply. It is closed by construction, not by a list of routes:

- Every reply advances `run.answerLog.length`. That length is the round `takeNextReply(run, run.answerLog.length + 1)` reads, so a reply that did not log would break round numbering and resume replay.
- The head is unread exactly when the stamp is behind the log.
- The gate loop reads an unread head before validation and the pull request step, the two steps that do not end with their own head read.
- A hold is recorded only on a read head. This makes the local review exemption safe.
- No record of commits exists to go stale.

A new tail question, stop site or reply route gets the guarantee with no code change. Tests: 8 SD-73 route tests, and a structural pin on the built script that only `observeHead` writes `run.headRound` and only the guarded line in `runGates` writes `gates.held`.

**Decided by the user (2026-10-29).**

- **Q1, option A.** The listed test deletions and rewrites are approved, with further rewrites of the same kind.
- **Plan acceptance.** The plan repeats the surface of round 1, whose fix did not close its class, so its acceptance went to the user. The user accepted it.

**Recorded decisions, each with its repeat scope.** A later finding or plan that asks for the opposite of one of these is a repeat, rejected by citing this record per `plugin/governance/remediation-doctrine.md` (Recorded Decisions and Repeat Findings).

- **The run keeps no commit list.** Per decision 2, the branch and the pull request on GitHub hold the commits. No message and no pull request text lists them. A blocked end names the branch and its head once a wave ran. Bounded impact: informational only, since GitHub's commit list and push events are authoritative. Repeat scope: a finding that asks the run to list or count its commits.
- **The head read keys on the reply round.** Repeat scope: a finding that asks to read the head per route or per stop site, or to skip the head check before validation or the pull request step while the head is unread.
- **Local review reads its own head.** Its commit runner reads the head, so no head check precedes it. Repeat scope: a finding that asks for a head check before local review.
- **The hold guard.** A step that took a reply and then returned `held` is not recorded. The loop reads the head and runs the step again. No current route does that. Repeat scope: a finding that asks to record a hold on an unread head.
- **The head check clears kept failures.** Kept from round 1. Repeat scope: a finding that asks a reply to keep a failure.
- **One writer.** `observeHead` is the only writer of `run.head`, `run.branch` and `run.headRound`. Repeat scope: a finding that asks another site to write any of them.
- **One head check runner on a review question reply after the head moved.** When the review's own fix commits moved the head, the reply runs `head-1-1` before validation runs again. That is one added haiku runner, on this route only, in place of a duplicate validation. Repeat scope: a finding that asks to drop that head check.

Two of round 1's recorded decisions are superseded: one head check runner per reply, uniform for local review, and a commit made during a pause entering the commit list as a range. Both were planner decisions. The user's round 1 answer to Q1, option A, still holds: after a local review reply, the review's commit runner reads the head before any hold.

**Surface account.** Removed: `run.commits`, `renderCommits`, the `commits` record part and its `waveCount` gate in `buildRunRecordParts`; the `committed` parameter of `observeHead` and its push and range branches; the commit count of the blocked branch line; `runResumedStep`, the loop in `runHeadCheck`, and the tie between `{rerun: true}` and the head check; the head check runner on every local review stop reply; the strings "Commits this run made:" and "This run made no commit."; and 2 tests and about 12 assertions. Added: `run.headRound` with its one write in `observeHead`, one condition at the top of the gate loop, one conjunct on the hold, and tests. The head-moved block moved within the loop. The surface shrinks. The runner count is about the same or lower, with no new agent.

**Measured lengths.** After this change `standard-delivery.js` is 167,383 characters with 43,088 of prose. That is 22,617 under the 190,000 budget and 712 under the prose budget of 43,800. The prose fell by 98 from round 1. `analysis-only.js` is unchanged at 74,539 and 18,900. The budgets do not change.

**Scope boundary.** Every root found is fixed in this pull request. Nothing is deferred, so there is no tracked issue and no new residual.

It updates these places in place, each with a dated note: the blocked end bullet of Amendment 2026-10-28 on one message shape; the bullets on blocked stops and `pr-P-N` reruns of Amendment 2026-10-29 (issue 457); its measured lengths; and in the round 1 section above, the eliminated class bullets, the head check paragraph, three recorded decisions, the surface account and the measured lengths.

Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/pull/477, local review finding e3a520d9, `docs/adr/0038-root-cause-at-the-first-finding.md` (triage).

Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/457, https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/447, `docs/adr/0038-root-cause-at-the-first-finding.md` (triage).

## Amendment 2026-10-29: script runners are hivemind:synapse behind a plugin hook (issue 467)

This amendment covers https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/467. Each runner step started a full haiku `hivemind:drone`, with the skill listing and the skill text, at about 26k input tokens on its first request, to run one committed script. A lean agent starts at about 3.8k (ADR-0010, Amendment 2026-10-29, U6). But an agent with no Skill tool cannot use the skill `allowed-tools` grant that decision 7 relies on, so the change needed another way to run the scripts with no prompt. The engine facts behind it are recorded in ADR-0010 first (decision 22).

**Decided by the user (2026-10-29).**

- **Route (d).** A plugin PreToolUse hook allows the runner commands. Route (b), a preloaded grants skill, failed the spike (U4), and a `tools:` entry `Bash(...)` grants nothing (U5).
- **The name.** The agent is `hivemind:synapse`.
- **From the feasibility round.** The hook covers all five runner scripts, `open-plan-pr.sh` included, so no runner step prompts. The hook allows only the agent type `hivemind:synapse`. The hook limits synapse's Write to `.hivemind/molt-inputs-<token>.json` and `.hivemind/pr-inputs-<token>.json`.
- **Q1, option A.** The hook denies, for every agent, a Bash command that names one of the five script file names as a path part in any form other than the exact one. Reading those files through Bash is then denied too, and agents use Read.
- **Q2, option A.** Cross-script use is accepted and recorded as a residual below.
- **Q3, option A.** The overlord never delegates to synapse. Only workflow scripts start it, and the roster in `plugin/governance/definitions.md` says so.
- **Q4, option A.** The branch-name taxonomy check and the pull request attribution check lived only in skill bodies, which synapse never loads. Both move into engine code.
- **Q5, option A.** A live smoke runs before merge, in manual and in auto mode, and its results go to ADR-0010 (Amendment 2026-10-29). Status: run on Claude Code 2.1.314 with the plugin at 2dc91ec, and all four checks passed in default and in auto mode. The overlord's workflow started `hivemind:synapse` for facts, branch, commit and pull request, the prompts carried only `{plugin-root}`, the hook allowed the runner commands (12 allows, no classifier call in auto mode), and a lookalike `cat` of `molt.sh` was denied. Runner first-request input tokens were 4,312 to 4,428 for facts, 4,354 to 4,470 for branch, 4,520 to 4,640 for commit and 5,414 to 5,783 for the pull request, against about 26k for a drone. Both runs stopped at the pull request step with "cannot look up the open PRs for head" (local bare origin, no GitHub), before the push, so the push was not exercised live. No security finding.
- **Q6, option A.** The listed test deletions and rewrites are approved.

**First-finding triage, per ADR-0038.**

First finding: synapse has no Skill tool, so it never loads the five skill bodies or the checks in them.

- **Introduced or inherited.** Introduced by this design, so it is fixed at the source in this pull request.
- **Root class.** A skill body is a check layer that a runner with no Skill tool bypasses. Each check moves to the layer that owns it:
  - The scripts already hold the identifier, ref, commit, trunk and dirty-tree gates.
  - The engine takes the two checks only the skill bodies held: the branch taxonomy (SD-08) and pull request attribution (SD-45). Commit subjects were already checked in code by `toCommitSubject` (SD-66).
  - The hook bounds the commands and the inputs-file path.
- **Removed instead of hardened.** The drone's git-script task exemption; the prompt sentences `gitScriptSentence`, `skillRunOnlySentence` and `runnerToolSentence`; the Skill call and skill text in each runner context, about 13 per run; and the manual-mode Skill prompt before runner commits, pushes and pull requests.
- **Threat model.** Decision 6 and the S12 classifier record. The spike's appended and planted runs in auto mode are existing behavior (ADR-0010, Amendment 2026-10-29, the auto-mode classifier finding). Q1 A closes the same-name copy and the appended chain for the five names by construction: an exact grammar plus a name deny. Renamed copies stay a residual.

Second finding: agent frontmatter cannot scope Write. The hook closes it for synapse: it allows the two inputs-file forms only, the parent must resolve to the working directory's `.hivemind`, a symlinked target is denied, and every other synapse Write is denied.

**The gate.** Its rules are stated once, in `plugin/governance/security-policy.md` (Synapse Runner Gate). Text: `plugin/hooks/synapse-gate.sh`, and `plugin/hooks/hooks.json`, an exec-form PreToolUse entry with matcher `Bash|Write` and a timeout of 10 seconds. Tests: `tools/test_synapse_gate.sh`, the `test_synapse_gate.sh` suite of `tools/validate.sh`.

**The engine side.**

- **Agent types.** The facts, branch, commit and pull request runners are `hivemind:synapse` on haiku (`synapseAgentType()`). The scan, validation and fix rounds stay `hivemind:drone` (`droneAgentType()`, renamed from `runnerAgentType`). The analysis-only facts step runs on synapse too.
- **The prompt.** A task line; the commit or push grant sentence, for the commit and pull request runners only; `dataBlocksSentence()`; a `command` block holding the one command line `buildRunnerCommand` builds; and for the commit and pull request runners an `inputs_file` block `{path, content}`. The placeholders `{plugin-root}` and `{token}` are the words of `plugin/agents/synapse.md`. Synapse replaces `{plugin-root}` with the `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}` that Claude Code substitutes in agent content, and `{token}` with a token it makes. The engine cannot make the token: the workflow runtime throws on `Date.now()` and `Math.random()`, and a fixed token breaks the transient retry (SD-71), which sends the same prompt again to a file that already exists.
- **The runner word check.** Before each runner call, the step checks every command word against the hook's value classes with `describeRunnerWordProblem` in `plugin/workflow-src/lib/facts.js`. A word outside its class is a terminal cause that names its flag and never its value, and no runner call is made. Without it, a word the hook refuses would reach synapse, be denied, and pause the run on a stop that no reply can fix. The standard-delivery runners and the analysis-only facts step both check.
- **The branch taxonomy.** SD-08 refuses a plan branch outside `^(feature|bugfix|hotfix|refactor|chore|docs|test|ci)/[a-z0-9]+(-[a-z0-9]+)*$`, the Branch Taxonomy and Branch Naming of `plugin/governance/workflow.md`. Like every plan problem, it pauses and plans again on the reply.
- **Pull request attribution.** Under SD-45, a title, body or comment that holds a `forbiddenSubjectTexts()` string, in any case, is a terminal stop before any push.
- **Rows.** SD-12, SD-15, SD-18, SD-42 and SD-43 are updated for the synapse prompts. SD-74 is added: the runner agent types, the prompt words, the word check, and that every command the engine builds is an exact form the hook allows for `hivemind:synapse`. A test runs each built command through the real hook, and another pins the prompt words in `plugin/agents/synapse.md`.

**Fixtures and checks.**

- `tests/policy/safety-synapse-gate.json`: an equal-mode set check on the hook's `add_gate_member` lines, which are the agent type `hivemind:synapse` and the five scripts. It also pins the claim sentence of the Synapse Runner Gate subsection and its consumers in `plugin/agents/synapse.md` and `plugin/hooks/hooks.json`.
- `tests/policy/safety-synapse-tools.json`: an equal-mode set check that the `tools:` list of `plugin/agents/synapse.md` is exactly Bash and Write, with Skill and Edit absent from its frontmatter.
- `tests/policy/safety-reviewer-fix-commits.json`: gains the `(Commit Authority)` consumer in `plugin/agents/synapse.md`.
- `tools/policy_check.sh`: synapse joins `AGENT_NAMES` and `REQUIRED_FILES`.

**Surface account.**

- Added: the synapse agent; the hook and `hooks.json`; one test suite and its `tools/validate.sh` wiring; two fixtures; the two engine checks and the runner word check; `synapseAgentType`, `buildRunnerCommand`, `describeRunnerWordProblem` and the `command` and `inputs_file` block builders; one security-policy subsection; row SD-74; these amendments.
- Removed: as listed in the triage.
- The net surface grows. That growth is the route (d) feature the user chose, not a remediation, so there is no Surface-Growth question beyond Q4.

**Recorded residuals.**

- **Cross-script use (Q2).** Root cause: the hook keys on the agent type and the exact form, not on the step. Synapse can run any of the five exact commands with no prompt, not only its own step's, so a runner that obeyed injected text could run another of the five with safe arguments. Bounded impact: each value passes its class, every script refuses trunk, a dirty tree and bad names, and the pull request runner already holds the push grant. Why the obvious fix was rejected: binding each runner to its script needs five agent types, or a hook that reads the agent's prompt from an undocumented transcript layout. Both add surface.
- **Renamed copies are not caught.** Root cause: the deny matches the five file names. A renamed or copied script, another interpreter or launcher that runs a script without naming its file, or any command that does not name the five, gets no decision from the hook. The normal flow applies: a prompt in manual mode, and the classifier in auto mode, which ran a planted copy in the spike. Bounded impact: the hook's allow never covers them, so this is the auto-mode exposure decision 6 already records, and no worse than before this change. Why the obvious fix was rejected: a list of interpreters or launchers is a deny-list that grows with each miss.
- **A hook failure falls back to prompts.** Root cause: when the hook cannot decide, it outputs nothing and never exits 2. That covers a missing `jq`, an empty `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT`, malformed input, a timeout, `disableAllHooks` or `allowManagedHooksOnly`, and a Windows host with no bash. Runner commands and Writes then go through the normal flow: a prompt in manual mode unless a settings rule covers the call, and the classifier in auto mode. Lookalike commands are not denied. Bounded impact: the hook itself fails toward a prompt, never toward an allow of its own. A settings allow rule that covers the call still applies, with none of the hook's checks; for synapse's inputs-file Write that is the seeded rules, recorded below (local review finding 793dbf65). Why the obvious fix was rejected: failing closed, with exit 2, would block every Bash call in every hivemind session whenever the hook breaks.
- **Made-up inputs-file tokens, extended to synapse (smoke-603 N4).** Root cause: synapse makes the token for its commit and pull request runs, because the engine cannot. Bounded impact: a reused token makes the Write fail, since synapse has no Read and Write refuses to overwrite a file the agent has not read. The step returns blocked, the run pauses, and the reply runs the step again with a new token. The failure is visible and recoverable, never a silent overwrite. Why the obvious fix was rejected: the workflow runtime has no clock or random source, and a fixed token breaks the retry.
- **A plugin root inside the repository (inherited).** Root cause: with `--plugin-dir` or a marketplace added from a local path, as when this repository dogfoods itself, `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT` is the checkout. A branch edit to a runner script then runs with no prompt under the hook. The skill grants had the same exposure. Bounded impact: a marketplace install loads the plugin from the plugin cache, outside the repository, so this applies only to a session that loads the plugin from a checkout, such as work on hivemind itself. A second effect in this repository: with the plugin loaded from the checkout, every Bash call that names one of the five scripts must be the exact absolute form, or the hook denies it (ADR-0010, Amendment 2026-10-29, the live observation of 2026-10-30).
- **A same-user program racing the inputs-file Write is outside the threat model (local review finding 149210d3).** The finding, severity high, titled "PreToolUse symlink checks are vulnerable to a check/use race", said the hook checks `.hivemind` and the leaf for symlinks and then allows, and Claude Code does the Write afterward. A program that swaps the leaf or the `.hivemind` directory for a symlink in that gap can send the allowed Write outside the checkout, and the script's later containment check only refuses to read the escaped file. It recommended a trusted helper that creates the file exclusively, with no symlink following, under an already-open directory. Root cause: the hook is a PreToolUse check by path, and Claude Code opens the path again for the Write, so no check the hook makes is bound to the file the Write opens. Bounded impact: the race needs a program running as the same user in the checkout at the moment of the Write. Such a program can already write any file the user can, so the race gives it nothing it lacks. While the hook runs, a symlink that is already there, committed on the branch or checked out by git, cannot race: the hook refuses it at check time, at the leaf and at `.hivemind`. When the hook cannot decide, the seeded inputs-file rules approve the Write with no check; see the next residual (local review finding 793dbf65). Ground: the same threat model as the build's (local review finding 916ad92b above) and the brood leaf symlink-swap residual (ADR-0019, amendment on #168, point 3; ADR-0021 decision 10), where bash has no portable no-follow open either. The redesign was rejected because the Write tool is Claude Code's, and a helper that creates the file would add a new script and a second step to every commit and pull request runner. Decided 2026-10-30, user call: option A. Record it as outside the threat model and keep the hook's path check. This is a recorded decision under `plugin/governance/remediation-doctrine.md` "## Recorded Decisions and Repeat Findings" (ADR-0038 decision 4).
- **A broken hook lets the seeded inputs-file rules approve synapse's Write with no symlink check (local review finding 793dbf65).** The finding, severity high, titled "Gate errors can bypass all Write-path security checks", said the hook outputs nothing and exits 0 when `jq` is missing, the hook input cannot be parsed, or another check before the decision fails, while the seed template's `Edit(.hivemind/molt-inputs-*.json)` and `Edit(.hivemind/pr-inputs-*.json)` rules (decision 9, `plugin/skills/_shared/settings-merge.sh`) already approve Writes to those paths. So a broken hook lets synapse Write there with none of the hook's checks, and a symlink committed at the leaf or at `.hivemind` could send the Write outside the checkout. It recommended removing the seeded pre-approval and relying on the hook's own allow, or another decision for synapse's Write that fails closed without the hook's dependencies. Root cause: the hook adds its checks on top of the permission flow, and when it cannot decide it allows nothing, so any settings allow rule that covers the call applies unchecked. The two seeded inputs-file rules cover synapse's Write, so the claims that the hook "never fails toward an allow" and that a committed symlink "cannot race" held only while the hook runs. Both are narrowed above and in `plugin/governance/security-policy.md` (Synapse Runner Gate). Bounded impact: it needs both a broken hook and a symlink committed on the branch. Write refuses to overwrite an existing file the agent has not read, and the script's containment check still refuses to read the escaped file. It is the exposure main already has: there the runner Writes used the same seeded rules, and no check looked for a symlink. Removing the rules from the seed template would not withdraw them from settings already seeded, because the merge never removes an entry (decision 9). Ground: a broken hook together with a committed symlink is outside the threat model of this change, by user decision. Decided 2026-10-30, user call: option A. Record it as a residual, narrow the two claims so they hold only while the hook runs, and keep the seeded rules and the hook as they are. This is a recorded decision under `plugin/governance/remediation-doctrine.md` "## Recorded Decisions and Repeat Findings" (ADR-0038 decision 4).

**Risks.**

- **Unverified: workflow agent types.** The docs do not say whether the overlord's `Agent(...)` tool list also limits the agent types a workflow can start. If it does, each synapse call returns a null result, an agent failure, and the run ends. Settled by the Q5 live smoke: the workflow started `hivemind:synapse` in default and in auto mode, so the `Agent(...)` list does not limit it.
- **Lookalike denials.** Reading the five script files through Bash, such as `git diff`, `grep`, `cat`, `sed`, `shellcheck` or `bash -n` on them, is denied for every agent. The deny reason gives the exact form and says to use Read.
- **The taxonomy check is code.** A run on a user branch whose name is outside the taxonomy now pauses at the plan check. Before, a haiku drone's judgment blocked it at the branch step.
- **Hook cost.** One hook process runs per Bash or Write call in every session where hivemind is enabled. It was not measured (U7).
- **A session started as `--agent hivemind:synapse`** gets the allow. That is the user's choice, and only the exact runner commands are allowed.

**Measured lengths.** Each length is the JavaScript string length of the built script, and each prose count is `countProseCharacters` in `tools/build_workflows.mjs`, both measured with node at commit 550d8b2.

| Script | On main at 72afb6b | At 550d8b2 | Prose at 550d8b2 | Prose budget |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `standard-delivery.js` | 167,383 | 169,958 | 43,332 | 43,800, unchanged |
| `analysis-only.js` | 74,539 | 75,797 | 19,073 | 19,200, up from 19,000 |

`standard-delivery.js` stays 20,042 characters under the 190,000 budget of decision 18. The analysis-only facts step's word check lands after 550d8b2 and grows `analysis-only.js` again, and the build check holds it under its prose budget.

It updates these places in place, each with a dated note: decision 7, decision 11, the Deferred items row settled by PR5, and the recorded residual "Made-up inputs-file tokens (smoke-603 N4)".

The rest of ADR-0037 is unchanged.

### Local review finding 6bc3a379: the plugin script path is one double-quoted word (2026-10-30)

This section covers local review finding 6bc3a379 on issue 467. Claude Code pastes the plugin path into skill, agent and governance text as it is, and the engine's `buildRunnerCommand` wrote `bash {plugin-root}/skills/<s>/scripts/<s>.sh`, the script path as a bare shell word. When the plugin path holds a space, such as a home folder named `Jane Doe`, the shell splits the path in two and the runner fails. The hook allowed the bare form, so the engine form passed every test on a CI path with no space. The same bare path was in 17 skills and `plugin/agents/github-reviewer.md` and `plugin/governance/versioning.md`, which the reviewer did not list.

**First-finding triage, per ADR-0038.**

- **Introduced or inherited.** Both. Introduced on this branch: `buildRunnerCommand` and its test pins, and the hook accepting the bare path as an exact form. Inherited from main: the command lines and `Bash(...)` grants of 17 skills (the 5 runner skills among them), `plugin/agents/github-reviewer.md` and `plugin/governance/versioning.md` step 3. Both kinds are fixed in this pull request.
- **Root class.** Shell command text pastes the plugin path as an unquoted word. The owner is every place that writes such text: the engine's one command builder, and the hand-written command and grant lines in skills, agents and governance. A grant is written in the same form as its command, because Claude Code matches a Bash rule against the command text as written.
- **Removed instead of hardened.** The hook's second accepted form, the bare path, is removed. The hook keeps ONE exact form, `bash "<root>/skills/<s>/scripts/<s>.sh"`, and denies the bare form. The Synapse Runner Gate in `plugin/governance/security-policy.md` says the path is always one double-quoted word. This is done in this pull request, not left as a tail: the engine, the skills and the hook now agree, so a bare runner command is denied on every machine and not only on a path with a space.
- **Threat model.** A robustness failure inside the recorded scope: normal users whose home or config path has a space. It is not an attack.

**Zoom-out.** This is the second finding on the `{plugin-root}` surface. Commit e0018d2 (local review finding 5dd80225) limited where synapse replaces `{plugin-root}` to the `command` string and `inputs_file.path`. This finding is about how the replaced path lands in the command, so the repeat trips the zoom-out and the approach was questioned. One option takes the path out of command text: the hook would rewrite the command with `updatedInput` and escape the path. It fixes only the 5 runner scripts and leaves the skills as they are. It adds rewrite logic to a security gate, needs a live check that subagents honor `updatedInput`, and turns a hook failure from a prompt into a failed runner. It was considered and rejected (user decision, Q2). Quoting the path at every layer, with a positive lint, closes the class instead.

**The fix.**

- `plugin/workflow-src/lib/facts.js`: `buildRunnerCommand` returns `bash "{plugin-root}/skills/<skill>/scripts/<skill>.sh"`, then each word, one space apart. Its header comment and the one for `describeRunnerWordProblem` say the script path is the one double-quoted word and value words are never quoted. Both generated scripts follow (commit e5d56bd).
- The tests: the command pins in `facts.test.mjs`, `analysis-only.test.mjs` and `standard-delivery.test.mjs` carry the quoted path, and `runCommandBlock` runs the filled command with `bash -c` and no longer splits it on spaces. A new `facts.test.mjs` test runs the built gather-facts command under a plugin root whose folder name holds a space: it exits 0 through bash, the real hook allows it for `hivemind:synapse` and gives no decision for `hivemind:drone`, and the same command with the path unquoted is denied.
- 19 files (b65540e): the grant and the command line of each of the 17 skills, the 9 commands of `plugin/agents/github-reviewer.md`, and the command in `plugin/governance/versioning.md` write `bash "${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/skills/<s>/scripts/<s>.sh"`. The 7 pinned command patterns in `tests/policy/safety-git-identifier-out-of-band.json` and `tests/policy/safety-working-branch-no-track.json` match the new text.
- CHECK 17 in `tools/policy_check.sh`, with a detection canary: in every plugin `*.md` file, each `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/<path>.sh` must sit directly in one of three forms: a path-only code span, a Markdown link target, or one double-quoted word. Anything else is a finding that names the file and line. The check is a positive allowlist, so the next skill cannot bring the bare form back and still pass on a CI path with no space. CHECK 6's path regex excludes the closing quote, so a quoted path still resolves. `CLAUDE.md` gains the editing rule, enforced by CHECK 17.
- The hook and its suite: one exact form, the quoted path; the bare form is denied; the deny reason prints the quoted form (`plugin/hooks/synapse-gate.sh`, `tools/test_synapse_gate.sh`).
- A live spike (claude 2.1.314) ran before the skills were quoted: quoted skill grants and a quoted settings allow rule matched a quoted command, with no prompt, in default and in auto mode, and a path with a space stayed intact. Results: `~/code/hivemind-workflow-spikes/results-467-quote.md`. Not tested there: lookalike forms against a quoted grant, grants in subagents and workflow agents, other Claude Code versions.

**Surface account.**

- Added: CHECK 17 with its canary; one engine test; one `CLAUDE.md` rule; this subsection and the changelog entries.
- Changed with no change in count: one engine string, the command and grant lines in 19 Markdown files, 7 policy pins, the engine test pins and one test helper, and the hook's accepted form.
- Removed: the hook's bare form, with its charset test and bare prefix; the bare rows of its suite became deny rows.
- Net: the surface grows by the lint and one test. The user chose it (Q3, option A), because without the lint the fix patches only the known files.

**Scope boundary.** Every known site of the root cause is fixed in this pull request, and nothing is deferred. The synapse placeholder limit of e0018d2 and the quoted path are separate layers: the first decides where `{plugin-root}` is replaced, the second what the replaced text looks like.

**Recorded residual: a plugin path holding a double quote, a dollar sign, a backtick or a control character.**

- **Root cause.** Claude Code pastes the plugin path into skill and agent text as it is, and no fixed shell quoting carries every byte. Inside double quotes `"`, `$` and the backtick are special; inside single quotes `'` is. So the double-quoted form cannot hold a path with those bytes, and text substitution cannot escape them.
- **Bounded impact.** The five runner commands are denied for every agent, and other script commands fail in the shell. It fails closed: nothing runs that was not asked for, and there is never an allow. It needs a home or config folder whose path holds one of those bytes, which is unusual.
- **Ground.** Unclosable with text substitution, and a user decision (Q2, option A, 2026-10-30). The rejected fix was the `updatedInput` rewrite described in the zoom-out.
- **Unverified: Windows backslash paths.** The hook turns `\` into `/` before it matches, so a command with backslashes is denied and fails closed. This was not run on Windows. Update 2026-10-30: the hook no longer rewrites the root, and a backslash in the root is now a residual byte with no exact form; see "Local review finding 27d328fd" below.

Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/467, `~/code/hivemind-workflow-spikes/results-467-quote.md` (the quoted-grant spike), `docs/adr/0038-root-cause-at-the-first-finding.md` (triage).

### Local review finding 27d328fd: the hook uses the plugin root byte for byte (2026-10-30)

This section covers local review finding 27d328fd on issue 467. The hook rewrote `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT` before it trusted it: every `\` became `/` and trailing slashes were stripped. It then used the rewritten string for two things, the exact-form prefix it matches and the folder it sources `skills/_shared/allowlist.sh` from. On Linux and macOS a backslash is an ordinary filename byte, so a root `a\b` was rewritten to `a/b`. That pointed the match and the sourced code at a sibling folder, not the folder Claude Code named. The reviewer suggested that an `a\b` root stay allowed. This fix does not follow that suggestion (Q2).

**First-finding triage, per ADR-0038.** This is the third finding on the plugin root flow (5dd80225, 6bc3a379, 27d328fd), so the approach was questioned.

- **Introduced or inherited.** Introduced on this branch, by commit 620cccd, through the design line that turned backslashes into `/` and removed a trailing slash before matching.
- **Root class.** The hook rewrites its root input before it trusts it (`plugin-root-identity-rewritten-before-trust`). The owning layer is the hook's root read.
- **Removed instead of hardened.** The rewrite and the trailing-slash loop are deleted, and no check replaces them. The backslash joins the bytes that have no exact form. Inside double quotes bash reads only `$`, the backtick, `"`, `\` and newline specially, so with the backslash the byte set is complete under the bash grammar and cannot grow. This restores the original approved design, which named the backslash. The rewrite had made that rule dead code.
- **Threat model.** The attack needs a sibling folder `a/b` with chosen content, which takes a same-user write next to the plugin cache. A program that can do that gains nothing it lacks, as with finding 149210d3. The defect is still a correctness root cause: the hook's root differs from Claude Code's, so the engine's real command is denied. It is fixed by removal, not by hardening.

**Zoom-out.** The hook should learn the root as one string, taken unchanged from Claude Code. `hooks.json` launches the hook as `bash ${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/hooks/synapse-gate.sh`, and Claude Code substitutes the same value into `synapse.md`, so synapse writes it into the command. The env value asks for no more trust than running the hook already does. Each layer copies the root verbatim: the engine emits only the `{plugin-root}` placeholder, synapse pastes the value only into the command and the inputs path (e0018d2), the command quotes it as one word (e5d56bd, b65540e, 5ed4028), and the hook now reads it unchanged.

- **Env value, kept.** One source, no transform.
- **BASH_SOURCE, rejected.** It would be a second source for the root that can disagree with the first: the bootstrap form in `tools/test_script_bootstrap.sh` uses `pwd -P`, which resolves symlinks and then no longer equals the substituted text. That contract also fails closed with a blocker message, while the hook fails open. And the suite could no longer point the real hook at test roots.
- **Inlining the identifier check, rejected.** It would copy the shared check that all the scripts use (`allowlist.sh`, about 250 lines with its helpers), so the gate and the scripts could drift apart.
- A project `.claude/settings.json` that overrides the env var is outside the threat model. A trusted project can already define its own hooks, so the override gains nothing.

No normalization is needed. A backslash root is reachable only on native Windows, and even there the rewrite never produced an allow: synapse writes the raw `C:\...` root, and the rewritten `C:/...` prefix never matched it. Stripping a trailing slash guaranteed a mismatch whenever both values carried one.

**The fix.**

- `plugin/hooks/synapse-gate.sh`: `plugin_root` is `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT` byte for byte. The backslash rewrite and the trailing-slash loop are deleted. The absolute check accepts `/...`, or a drive letter and colon followed by `/` or `\`; anything else exits 0 with no output. `allowlist.sh` is still checked and sourced from `"$plugin_root/skills/_shared/allowlist.sh"`. The no-exact-form case adds the backslash. The header comments say the root is never rewritten.
- `tools/test_synapse_gate.sh` (Q1, approved by the user 2026-10-30): the rows that pinned the rewrite are deleted, which are `root:backslash-normalized`, `root:backslash-command` and `BACKSLASH_ROOT`. `root:trailing-slash` and `root:trailing-slashes` now allow synapse's quoted command whose path is the root text followed by `/skills/...`. New rows: `root:trailing-slash:rewritten` gives deny; `plug\root` joins the unsafe-root loop with deny for both the quoted and the bare form; two-tree rows with a real root `bs/a\b` and a sibling `bs/a/b` prove the sibling is neither matched nor sourced, one with a copy of `allowlist.sh` and one whose `allowlist.sh` is `exit 2`.
- `plugin/governance/security-policy.md` (Synapse Runner Gate): `<root>` is `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT` exactly as Claude Code sets it, and the residual byte list adds the backslash.

**Acceptance test.** What becomes impossible: any finding where the folder the hook matches, the folder it sources from, or the file bash runs differs from the folder Claude Code named. The key changes: the root is an opaque byte string that the hook never interprets, with no separator, slash, case or symlink handling. The quoted form exists only when bash's reading of the word equals its text, and that byte set comes from the grammar, not from findings. With no transform left, nothing can map one folder to another. This is removal, not one more conjunct.

**Surface account.**

- Removed: the rewrite and the trailing-slash loop; `BACKSLASH_ROOT` and 2 test rows.
- Changed: the absolute-root pattern, the no-exact-form byte set, 2 test rows, comments, 2 policy sentences.
- Added: 5 test rows, this subsection and 1 changelog line.
- Net: less mechanism, about 3 more regression rows.

**Scope boundary.** Every site is fixed and nothing is deferred. The residual below is the only write beyond the fix.

**Recorded residual extension: a plugin path holding a backslash (user decision Q2, option A, 2026-10-30).**

- **Root cause.** The quoted form needs a root with none of the bytes bash reads specially inside double quotes, and the backslash is one of them. A backslash root has no exact form.
- **Bounded impact.** On Linux or macOS it needs a plugin path that holds a backslash, which is very rare. The runner commands are then denied for every agent, so it fails closed and never allows. Windows behavior is unchanged: the gate stays on and runner commands are denied, as recorded above. It was never run on Windows.
- **Ground.** User decision Q2, option A. The rejected option B allowed an `a\b` root when bash reads the path unchanged. It would also have opened no-prompt runner commands on Windows backslash paths, which have never been run.
- **Residuals not touched.** 149210d3 and 793dbf65 are unaffected.

Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/467, `docs/adr/0038-root-cause-at-the-first-finding.md` (triage).

Links: https://github.com/brenpike/hivemind/issues/467, `~/code/hivemind-workflow-spikes/results-467.md` (the spike), `docs/adr/0010-permission-allowlist-posture.md` (Amendment 2026-10-29), `docs/adr/0038-root-cause-at-the-first-finding.md` (triage).
